SERIES: Structural Integrity of the Heart
START OF BOOK 1
PART 1: The Remnants and The Beginnings
In those hours where night bled into morning, the young woman sat on a stool in the garden of her home, watching the sky. The coldness of the air did not bother her; she clutched the cardigan draped over her shoulders only lightly. She continued to watch the street and the sky
until a human shape materialized on the pavement before her. The shape was a man. As he entered through the garden gate, the woman rose from the stool.
"Oh god, you’re okay. You’re alive." The young man walked briskly toward the woman who had just been sitting and hugged her.
"I am fine, Severus," the woman said. She was trying to stand strong, but she was on the verge of tears. At that moment, a third person emerged from inside the house. This, too, was a young man.
"You came?" he said to Severus. His voice was harsh, yet there was no sign of hostility.
"The Secret-Keeper was Peter," the woman said. She did not want to watch the two of them bicker, so she cut straight to the point. "Peter betrayed us and told Voldemort our location."
The young man who had come out of the house groaned in grief. "The lowlife traitor! I cannot believe this."
Severus turned to the woman. "Where is the b-boy?"
"Here," the woman said. She pointed to the pram behind her, which no one would naturally notice in the dark. "When Sirius found me, I ran straight to the nursery. He survived the Killing Curse, but—" her voice trembled.
"Then we gathered some personal effects," Sirius said. "Actually, we weren’t expecting you, Snape."
"Boys, please, we have more important matters than squabbling," the woman warned loudly. Severus and Sirius turned to her.
"I’m not going to hide it," Severus began. "I live in the same flat with four Death Eaters, solely to gather information from them. If you don’t believe me, ask Dumbledore, Black," he said furiously.
"Tonight, something happened suddenly; everyone panicked. And when Regulus showed me his arm, the Dark Mark wasn’t there. It was gone. When he told me 'the prophecy has been fulfilled,' I understood immediately and came here as fast as I could," he finished.
"Lily, you know I would never lie to you," he added, his dark eyes looking at the young woman. Lily nodded. Severus never lied.
"Let’s say we believe you," began Sirius. "Why couldn’t you stop it?"
"I was the one who suggested the Fidelius Charm. If only you had been the Secret-Keeper, Black, we wouldn’t be here right now." Sirius flinched as if slapped across the face. "How dare you!"
"Severus, I was the one who approved Peter being the Secret-Keeper," Lily said. "I chose to trust him just as I trust Sirius," she added bitterly.
"So what happens now?" Sirius asked. "Yes, the prophecy has found its mark, but is Voldemort truly dead? What will happen to Harry?"
Severus took a deep breath. "'Neither can live while the other survives,' it said. Voldemort may not be completely destroyed. We cannot stop being cautious."
"His body wasn’t inside," Lily said. "A good reason to be suspicious."
"He was a Dark Wizard. His corpse wouldn't be natural," Sirius said. Severus approached the pram and peered inside out of the corner of his eye. There was a sleeping child inside; his chest rose as he breathed. Severus relaxed in an instant. They had survived a great catastrophe.
Severus was just about to say to Lily, 'Move into my house, we can cast other spells, it’s more protective, and Sirius can be the Secret-Keeper,' when a massive man appeared on the street. All three young people recognized the newcomer: Hagrid, the Gamekeeper of Hogwarts.
They told him what had happened. Hagrid, in turn, explained the instructions he had received.
"According to Dumbledore, yeh need to live with yer sister," Hagrid said. "He’s relying on the bond of blood. If yeh had died, Harry was meant to go to them tonight."
"Not in this lifetime!" Lily said with a sudden wave of anger. Her relationship with her sister was worse than bad. "If Dumbledore suggested it, it’s fer the best, Lily. But the choice is yers," Hagrid said.
"Lily, you can stay wherever you want," Sirius said. "Before coming here—" he extended his hand toward the street, indicating Godric’s Hollow, "—you were staying at Remus’s house, you can go there."
"That option is impossible now," Hagrid said. "I’d be very surprised if crimes by rogue Death Eaters don’t break out soon. Remus’s condition is known to them as well," he finished his explanation.
"His house would be a target. Come to me," Severus said. "At the very least, go with Black. Wherever you are, Black will be the Secret-Keeper anyway."
"Since when do you trust me this much, Snape?"
"I know you, you fool. Even if a hundred wands tortured you, you wouldn’t talk." In the end, Lily decided to live with Severus Snape at Spinner's End.
"You might be busy this week and in the coming term, Lily," Hagrid warned. "The Ministry of Magic has mobilized."
"Then tell the Ministry that Lily doesn’t have to play the fool for anyone right now," Sirius said dismissively.
-----
Lily Apparated to Spinner's End with Harry and two suitcases as dawn broke. Severus was with her. "Currently, I count as the Secret-Keeper of this house," Severus said. "It can stay that way; meaning there would be two Secret-Keepers."
"I don't know, Severus. I don't want to think for a while. I'm still surprised I even survived," Lily said. Severus flinched slightly but then smiled at her. If anything were to happen to Lily, he would likely be devastated. He changed the subject. "Come, let me show you your room. You’ll be more comfortable here." Severus led her to the master bedroom that had once belonged to his parents. It was quite clean and sparse; there were no personal items or paintings on the walls.
"I haven’t stayed in this house for years," he explained. "I came three weeks ago, threw out some rubbish. Damaged furniture, everything. I mopped the floors downstairs."
Lily knew Severus well enough to know he had done all this work so he wouldn't have to endure a filthy environment when he visited. Perhaps Severus hadn’t seen this place as perfect for living and had merely organized it when he last came, but for Lily, it was functional enough.
"Thank you for everything, Severus," Lily said. "Don't mention it. We are old friends."
He turned his back to Lily and went downstairs.
(Flashback) Severus POV, Hogwarts, 1977
On the day Severus Snape decided to define Lily Evans’ existence as a problem, it was raining at the North Tower of Hogwarts. This detail was important. The rain fogged up the windows from the inside, giving Severus the opportunity to completely erase the outside world while he thought.
Lily Evans was his sole friend. Lily Evans was logical. But she was intuitive as well. Logic and intuition could not reside together in the same body for long. There was an error here. One evening in the library, Lily came and sat right across from Severus without saying a
word. This was an intervention.
"You are watching me," Lily said. "Don't watch."
Severus did not look up. "No," he said. "I am observing you." He dipped his quill into the ink and continued writing. Lily’s presence was distracting him, but he did not want to admit it.
"A human," he said, without lifting his head, "cannot be simultaneously this well-intentioned and this destructive." This was a  determination. Not an accusation. The calm tone Lily used when answering disturbed Severus. People usually became defensive.
"I have no idea about what you term as destructive. Perhaps the problem," Lily said, "is that you always read the world through results. You are not a philosopher, Severus."
"The world consists of results," Severus said. This was not an argument; it was a boundary line.
"No," Lily said calmly. "Results are merely side effects. Is it drinking water from the fountain because you are thirsty, or going to the fountain with an empty flask?"
This example was illogical. Thirst, fountain, flask... Metaphors. Severus did not like metaphors. He put down the quill.
"A fountain?" he said. "Why don’t you use Aguamenti?" Lily’s sudden outburst was uncontrolled. This was not a result Severus had expected.
Severus regretted it. Regret was inefficient, but undeniable.
The next day, when Lily came to the Slytherin table in the Great Hall and sat beside him, Severus’s first reaction was defense. "Stop observing me," he said. His voice was hard. Too hard.
Lily’s answer did not fit anywhere on Severus’s logic map. "The moment you realize it, you won't be alone anymore." This was not true. Loneliness did not change by someone noticing it. Loneliness was a state; not a perception.
Therefore, he began to ignore her. "You are ignoring me," Lily said one day.
"No," Severus said.
"That," said Lily, "was, I think, the first lie you’ve ever told me."
"Yes," Severus said. "That is why it is true."
That night, Severus considered the possibility that Lily loved him. He immediately rejected this possibility. Because love did not work this way. Love would be more dramatic.
"You love me," he said one day. This was not a question. It was a hypothesis. "Sometimes," Lily said. "But not always. I love you when you aren't acting like an arse."
"That is not possible." Lily Evans loved him, but Severus’s existence was not a fixed point in Lily’s life. This was as attractive to Severus as it was disturbing. Because for the first time, someone loved him but did not cling to him.
And Severus Snape knew that the most dangerous bonds were the loose ones.
On a mild spring day, when Lily suggested drinking in Hogsmeade, Severus’s perception shifted. Because he knew: If Lily did not put him at her center, he could put Lily at his. In Hogsmeade, the cobblestone streets were wet. The slippery ground pleased Severus. On grounds like this, people focused their attention downward and missed their surroundings. Lily’s attention, however, was upward. To the sky. As if it wasn't on Severus.
When the drinks arrived, he noticed Lily hesitate. "I can go if you don't want this," he said. This was not an offer; it was an escape route.
"I want to," Lily said. Too fast. Too clear. This made Severus even more uneasy. Lily was looking at him this time. Without hiding it. Without analyzing.
"You are looking at me," Severus said.
"Yes."
"Are you analyzing?"
"No. Not this time."
This answer pierced Severus’s defense. Because it showed that Lily was in intuitive mode. Severus actually wanted Lily. It was simple. But he might not want the possibilities that came with Lily. The change. The questions. "Wanting me this much," he said slowly, "might be temporary."
Lily smiled.
(Now)
“The scar on Harry’s forehead was not merely the result of a curse, but formed because the excess energy could find no place to escape. This mark is not a badge of honor. The curse rebounded, but it did not destroy Voldemort. It transformed him into a shadow, a whisper. No,
Lily, he was not destroyed. He fled.”
They were in Dumbledore’s office at Hogwarts: Lily and the sleeping Harry in her arms, Severus, Sirius, and Dumbledore. Dumbledore continued in a calm voice.
“Today, the Ministry of Magic denies the truth; they say he is dead. I fear they will not change this stance. Some of the Death Eaters have changed their names, some are in Azkaban, and the rest have fled. There are also those who sit quietly for now, awaiting revenge. Therefore, Severus, I will ask you to come to Hogwarts. As a teacher, and for your own protection.”
Dumbledore turned to the young people sitting before him. Only a few years ago, they were rushing through the corridors of this ancient school with exam panic and excitement. Now, their faces bore a sorrowful but fearless expression, aged ten years in a single night.
“We have placed charms on the house where Severus stays,” Lily said. “All three of us are quite protected.”
“The Death Eaters are currently in a panic. When the panic subsides, they will begin to relax, and there will be those asking questions,” Dumbledore said. “They will look for Severus, and indeed, they will find him. Some will want extra information, some will demand accountability... Staying there is risky as of this moment, Severus.”
“But Lily will be left alone,” Severus said, weighing the absurdity of this teaching plan in his mind. Dumbledore wasn’t wrong, but was this the solution?
“Lily is surrounded by the protective wards of the house, plus her close friends. She is very well protected,” Dumbledore said, his eyes twinkling as he looked at Sirius. “I know the Ministry is putting pressure on you, Sirius. They may come down harder on you to learn your
brother’s whereabouts.”
“I haven’t been in contact with Regulus for years,” Sirius said. “If he has any sense, he’s fled abroad.” For a while, no one spoke; Severus broke the silence. “Dumbledore is right, Lily. You should stay at the house.”
“And what will happen to you?” Lily asked.
“I will be a moving target,” Severus replied. Let the Death Eaters come to Hogwarts and catch him if they dared.
“What about us?” Sirius said. “Remus and I exchanged owls last week, but he told me nothing. Did you tell him something?” he asked, looking at Dumbledore.
“It is true that I have a different agenda with Remus; I made a request of him for a purpose different from that of this group.” Sirius fell silent; he didn't want to argue, but was this request so important that Remus wasn't with them?
After leaving Dumbledore’s office, the group split up; Sirius went elsewhere, while Lily and Severus returned to Spinner's End. Lily went to the bedroom to change, and Severus began preparing dinner.
Sirius had arrived the previous day with a newly bought cot so Harry could sleep more comfortably, and they placed it in the narrow space between the bed and the window. Lily laid the sleeping child in the cot. She would need to wake and feed him soon.
They ate dinner in silence, but afterwards, she thanked Severus because he cooked very well. When Severus left the house to go to Hogwarts, she might miss the taste of a well-cooked home meal just a little; Lily was not good in the kitchen. Dumbledore had told Severus he
could come to the school within a week. “By the way, while you were upstairs, this letter arrived. From the Ministry. I didn’t open it,
thinking it might be private,” he handed the letter to Lily. Lily shrugged and opened it.
“Not that private. I had applied to use my maiden name. And... it’s been accepted, it’s Evans again.” She looked at Severus with a smile.
“The Potter surname had felt like a tombstone to me for a long time. Evans is more alive. Muggle, yes, but why should I be ashamed of my roots?”
What would the pure-bloods say to this? It didn't matter. Lily would not hide the surname Evans; she would carry it.
-----
Before Severus left, he made one final move, and Lily’s jaw dropped. “Severus... this is your father’s house.”
“It was his house. And now it is yours,” Severus said. The deed had been sealed by magic. Lily was now the owner of the house, both under the Fidelius and in the legal sense. The Secret-Keeper was first Severus, then Sirius.
The Saturday after Severus left the house, Sirius arrived. He gave the information that Peter could not be found anywhere. They were still looking for him. Lily gave Harry to Sirius. The two playmates giggled at each other as Sirius tickled him. Harry was already used to Sirius; he was happy his uncle was home. But Sirius was quiet while attending to the baby. Lily realized that Sirius’s thoughts were on
Peter’s escape; she hardened her voice as she called out to him.
“You will leave him to the Aurors, Sirius. Peter.”
Sirius turned. “Are you telling me this?” “Yes,” Lily said. “Because I know what will happen if you go to Azkaban. I would lose you,
too.”
This sentence silenced Sirius. “So, Snape has gone,” he said, grimacing as Harry pulled his hair.
“Severus has been at Hogwarts for a few days,” she said. “He started teaching. Assistant Professor of Potions, teaching the first and second years.”
Sirius’s brows furrowed. “Dumbledore should have offered you a teaching post as well,” he said, handing Harry back. “If he offered, would you go?”
“No.”
“Why?”
“Because I couldn’t live doubting Severus,” she said. “And if I went to Hogwarts, after a while, I would do that.”
“And here I thought you were close friends.”
“We still are,” Lily said. She couldn't look into Sirius’s eyes; she straightened Harry’s romper. “When Voldemort fell,” she said slowly, “half of the Death Eaters changed sides. That is exactly why I ask myself what difference Severus has. And then I give this answer: He didn’t change sides. He never truly belonged to a side.”
Harry reached his arms toward Sirius with a sweet squeal, and Sirius took him again. Lily continued. “I think Regulus was Severus’s only Death Eater connection who didn’t harbor hostile feelings toward me,” she added, turning to Sirius. Sirius’s expression was unreadable. “Great news. But I have no such brother.”
“Sirius, I understand you best when it comes to sibling dramas,” Lily said. “But your reconciliation is as easy as an owl post.”
-----
For the first time, Severus Snape did not feel happy about being at Hogwarts, and this was somewhat contrary to his nature. For him, school meant being away from the chaos at home, from his father who didn't love him. School meant being among people who accepted him. It meant Lily’s presence, sitting side by side in shared classes taking notes, then comparing notes and studying together. It meant going to Hogsmeade together with joy and excitement, and returning in the evening with a stomach ache because they had eaten their fill of sweets in Hogsmeade.
After a short adjustment period, he settled into a routine. Breakfast in the Great Hall every morning, small morning gossip with the other teachers, then a teaching marathon for the students all day long. When there was a gap between his classes, he planned what he would teach in the coming days and weeks down in the dungeons. Strangely, he had started to like teaching; students falling behind lowered his mood as well.
The brightest factor lifting this mood was, of course, Lily. News came from her frequently; she wrote to Severus almost every week. These were short notes: The house is quiet without you, Harry is fine, Sirius came and went, no Peter but they are looking for him. Severus would fold these notes and put them in his pocket. He couldn't answer every note. Because if he wrote back, he would want to be at home with her.
The whole year passed in chaos; half of 1982 was over. Nymphadora Tonks had broken a cauldron; Callum Figg’s exploding potion had shed all the hair on the left side of his head, leaving half his head bald. Bill Weasley was the most hardworking student among his peers, followed by Eliza Prewett. Miriam McKinnon was successful in lessons, apart from being clumsy.
Fortunately, this group consisted of first and second years, so Severus wouldn't deal with them again. And the summer holidays were coming.
(Flashback) Severus POV, August 1978
The weather that day was hotter than seasonal norms. He sat up in bed, opening his mouth in a huge yawn. Severus usually slept without a T-shirt on certain summer nights if he sweated, but for the last week, he had been sleeping only in shorts. With a strange restlessness, he
pulled a T-shirt on and went to the kitchen. Slow and cautious, as if he might carelessly scald himself with boiling water, he put water on for tea and prepared Lily's coffee.
When Lily didn't come, he panicked; they were going to be late for work. He knocked on her door four times. Was she ill?
"Lily? Are you still sleeping?" He opened the door slowly. The bed was made. Hadn't Lily come home? Where was she?
He went up to the third housemate, the kind, sweet, but gambling elderly woman on the upper floor, and asked for Lily. If something had happened or if Lily hadn't come homeyesterday, she would know.
The old woman, Amanda, told Severus in a calm and reverent tone that Lily had left the house early in the morning. But why had she left at this hour? Was she coming to work later?
Severus wasn't aware he was thinking out loud. This intensity of emotion was causing his control over his tongue and logic to diminish. Amanda grinned slightly: the same smile as when she found the weekly bonus on a scratch card. Her words stabbed into Severus's heart like a stake, making it bleed.
Apparently, a black-haired, bespectacled, medium-height man had picked her up from in front of the building door, holding a wrapped gift in his hand. When Lily went out the front door, she hadn't seemed surprised by his arrival; she had greeted him, and then they had hugged. They had walked south.
Severus was sure his blood was boiling, literally melting his veins, spreading to his internal organs, and burning his stomach and abdomen. So how was he still alive? Moreover, he was cold. He went out the door. No, this wasn't cold.
He stepped onto the street. A workday he had to go to... There was sun, but no light. It was a July morning, but it wasn't summer. Severus walked, and once past the dustbin, he Apparated without anyone seeing. “Black-haired… bespectacled… medium-height…” James Potter's name hadn't been mentioned. But Severus's mind didn't need names. Recognizing the shape was enough.
This wasn't jealousy. Jealousy was lighter. This was a catastrophe. This was the feeling of displacement. As if, for the first time in his life, while walking towards something, he learned that someone else had arrived there before him.
He was losing control of his body again. “Don’t be ridiculous,” he said to himself. Lily had a life. He knew this. Severus realized at that moment that what burned him was not that Lily was with someone.
What burned him was that Lily hadn't told him.
(Now)
Lily, meanwhile, was chasing something else. Driven by a desire to build a life under her own control, relying especially on her talents in security, research, and spell creation, she had applied to the Ministry. However, the fat, high-pitched woman in the recruitment department and her team, disregarding Lily’s successful academic record and the experience she had gained at the Ministry after graduation, appointed her as an intern in the Muggle Liaison Office. This department was a newly opened subdivision of the Misuse of Muggle Artefacts Office.
The biggest plus of Lily being appointed to this department was that she would be working with Arthur Weasley, whom she knew and trusted from the old Order. For Lily, working with Arthur was not just a job, but also an effort to make friends she could trust outside her small
circle and to build a social network.
The downside of this new job was that she would be working four days a week, six hours a day. This meant that Harry would need Severus’s care in the mornings and afternoons.
Severus had never provided care for a small person at any point in his life. Since Lily knew him well, she understood his desire to flee from this responsibility. "I have never looked after a child," he said, as he had pointed out, but Lily replied, "Because he is so young, Muggle nurseries won't accept him." Wizards did not have institutions like nurseries.
Lily: "Besides, Harry isn't a baby anymore; he talks. For example, he says if he gets hungry. But we're having trouble with toilet  training."
Severus: "I don't understand?"
Lily: "Well... he says he needs the toilet, and we go to the toilet together, but on the way, he wets himself."
"So," said Severus slowly, "after reporting this... he can't make it in time?"
Lily: "Eh, if this happens, you have to put new trousers on him. Also, he feels a sense of shame, so try to stay calm if he wets himself. Sometimes I can't, and... when my voice comes out harsh, his eyes fill up."
Severus: "What if he dressed himself?" he said, as if the obvious solution were right there and Lily just hadn't thought of it.
Lily laughed at this. "Hah. I wish. But he's only two years old." After Lily Evans put on a dark green summer robe for her first day of work and left, Severus and Harry were left alone. They had eaten breakfast; Severus was solving a crossword puzzle to distract himself when
Harry left his toys, came to him, and said something unintelligible. Severus sighed; he hadn't understood what the child said. Harry was repeating it.
"What do you want? Something to eat?" he asked; he couldn't get angry at the child for not speaking properly. He gave him a wafer and a sweet, but Harry refused. Then, thinking of the worst-case scenario that came to mind, he took his hand and led him all the way to the toilet. This time, Harry said "no." Finally, a word he could speak had come out of his mouth. He could only say about 10 words anyway.
"I don't understand you, I'm sorry," he said finally. Strangely, the feeling of anger didn't even cross his mind. Because the familiar green eyes expressed that he genuinely needed something. He could have asked Lily, but he didn't want to disturb her at work.
Harry walked towards the kitchen, Severus following. "I don't understand what you want," Severus said, looking at the various baby items there. Did he want a toy? There was a sippy cup with bears on it, full. The moment Severus picked up the cup, Harry
squealed.
"Water?" He immediately gave the cup to the child, and Harry drank as if he had been stranded in a desert, finishing it.
"Maybe I can get used to this," Severus thought. They had survived the first crisis and the first hour; five hours remained. When Lily came home, Severus looked like he had come out of a war, exhausted and sweaty. Harry, on the other hand, was sleeping.
"After a few small crises, I understood what he wanted," Severus said. Lily wondered, 'Is he happy because he spent time with Harry or because Lily came home?' A shadow seemed to pass over Severus’s face, but he continued in the same tone. "We had a toilet accident; you warned me anyway. Then I took him outside, he flew a bit. We came inside, and he went to sleep; he put himself to bed."
Lily thought he described the day they spent together as if he had kept a dog company, but this situation didn't bother her at all. Severus's new responsibility in the house was a new but temporary dynamic.
Severus would return to Hogwarts when September came.
Neither Lily nor Severus could understand how two months passed. It was as if time was in a hurry. On the last working day of August, Lily slowly went to Arthur and said she was resigning. Arthur was shocked. "Are you unhappy with the office or the workload?" he asked. The room contained three desks, and truth be told, they barely fit. But for Lily, this wasn't a negative.
"No, Arthur, quite the opposite. I love this department and the work. But... my friend is going to start working, and leaving Harry with him during the days was working for me," Lily said, with an embarrassed voice.
Arthur smiled understandingly: "Can't you find someone else? If you want, let's rearrange your working hours?"
"There is no nursery in the wizarding world," Lily said with obvious sadness.
"It will be hard to part with you, Lily. Don't make yourself a stranger; you can come here whenever you want," Arthur said. He seemed sincere.
When it came to 3 o'clock, the shift ended. It was time to leave. Arthur, in protective and watchful big brother mode, hugged Lily: "I expect you to visit us without fail," he said. Lily waved and left.
Lily arrived at Spinner's End within a few minutes; she had Disapparated right on the street. Before entering the house, she heard a laugh belonging to Harry. The sound was coming from the garden. Seeing the scene, she could have swallowed her tongue. Harry was on the toy broomstick Sirius had bought him last year, about half a meter up, and he was flying. Severus was also levitating a small, round object in the air. He was trying to get Harry to catch the object, a soft ball, but when Harry got too close, he would levitate it somewhere else.
Just like a real Snitch.
Harry laughed with joy again and lunged forward towards where the object flew. "Wonderful," Lily said, grinning from ear to ear. "When do we start applauding the future Quidditch champion?"
"Mummmyyy," Harry stopped with a sudden brake in joy, jumping off the broom. He was at a non-dangerous height anyway; neither Severus nor Lily panicked. He hugged Lily’s legs, while Severus came to their side. He was holding the broom in his left hand. "He really is talented," he said, smirking.
They went inside; while Lily attended to Harry, Severus went into the kitchen. He took out the frozen meatballs from the freezer (they had bought them at a 40% discount) and put them all in a large pan to cook. Both of their laughter could be heard from inside. Severus could
neither leave his work halfway to watch them nor eavesdrop.
During the last two months living in his house, he had updated his list of things he liked to do:
1 – Seeing Lily laugh
2 – Seeing Harry laugh
3 – Seeing the two of them laugh together.
He was aware he was acting emotionally; according to the old Severus, this was quite dangerous. But... there was a but. Giving his house to Lily and Harry was one thing; living together with them in the house was another. He was overflowing with impossible, crazy, chaotic desires inside. Every particle of him was practically lighting warning lamps. Even the thought of it was giving him a heart
attack.
The old Severus wasn't this impulsive. His own internal change threatened the area he saw as logical. But why not try? Even if his chances were low, it was worth it to win Lily back. The thought of finally winning Lily back was worth everything. Was it worth it – of course. Lily was his oxygen, the center of life. He could forget everything he learned and start over, be crazy and emotional. Even if it was contrary to his existence... Even if his chances were low.
He flipped the meatballs, and Lily and Harry entered the kitchen. Harry had changed in two months too. He no longer wanted to be carried around in arms, he acted more ambitious while playing games, and when he received a 'no' answer, he tried again, asking with a softer voice. Neither night nor day did he ever cry, he wasn't stubborn, he loved riding the broom most of all. Moreover, his vocabulary had increased.
Severus flipped the meatballs again and made sure they were cooked. They ate the meal calmly; usually, Lily would talk about work with that characteristic liveliness every day. Today, that liveliness wasn't there. Either she was tired, or something else had happened to make her unhappy. Severus made a note in his brain to talk to Lily after Harry slept.
Harry, on the other hand, couldn't finish the meatballs; he visibly couldn't hold his head up. Lily took him to sleep. Meanwhile, Severus had brewed tea, poured himself a cup, and even drunk it.
Lily came and stood at the entrance of the kitchen. Severus pointed to the empty cup he had drunk tea from. "Tea?" Lily shook her head negatively. Her mind seemed elsewhere, as if thinking about things.
Severus continued to organize the common area, taking the empty cup to wash.
-----
(Flashback) Lily POV, August 30, 1978
However inexperienced Severus was, I was just as inexperienced. I felt like we were both standing in the same void. We were sitting side by side on the sofa. The hour and minute hands of the old wall clock, shining in the moonlight, showed 3:25. We weren't sleepy. Or rather, we were resisting the night. Without wanting it to end. As if, if morning came, the meaning of this moment would dissolve.
Before sitting on the sofas, Severus had brewed tea. His tea was always like this. It carried sentences he couldn't say. Sometimes an apology, sometimes a promise, sometimes just "I am here."
When I was a child, before Hogwarts, teachers had taken us to a cinema festival. We watched a black and white French film. Subtitled. The words didn't quite fit the image, but you still understood everything.
The tea brewed tonight was like that too. It was subtitles. What he said flowed together with what he didn't say: 'I value you. I see you. But I want to be unfair to neither you nor myself.'
