"1, 2, 3!" Bill let go of the rat in mid-air, opening his hand directly and allowing the animal to fall; as he and Charlie ran away, Lily and Severus sent a double red curse at the rat. The rat transformed into a man in the kitchen. As Molly screamed in shock, Arthur joined the other two and they shouted, "Stupefy!"
Peter Pettigrew slumped to the floorboards, hit by three Stunning Spells.
While the four adults looked at the man lying on the floor, pandemonium was breaking out upstairs. It was clear they had watched everything from above; Bill and Charlie were excitedly explaining to their younger siblings how the rat Scabbers had turned into a human. Severus bound the unconscious man with ropes shooting from his wand, impossible to break or cut with a blade. While Percy cried over the fact that his rat had turned into a human, Arthur tried to calm him down. Fortunately, the younger ones and Harry hadn't fully grasped what was happening. Lily told the twins there was no need for panic. Then she straightened up and turned to Severus with a knowing look. "Things are going to get very messy, Sev. I just need five minutes. Buy me time, please." Before Severus could say anything, Lily left the house and Apparated.
While Arthur and Molly struggled to convince the children upstairs to go to sleep, Severus's mind was on Lily. Just then, Ministry squads Apparated in the garden. Arthur headed towards them to explain what had happened. The newcomers were openly surprised when they saw Severus first, then Peter lying bound on the floor. A Ministry employee grunted discontentedly. "Your intern Lily Evans just went abroad without a visa," he said.
Arthur replied without breaking character, "You can see we are in an emergency situation. I am sure her reason for going is valid." At that moment, the pieces fell into place in Severus’s mind.
"Evans went to find Sirius Black," he said to the Ministry teams. Everyone turned to him in astonishment. "Black? Did he flee abroad without a visa too?"
Just as Severus was about to say, "What do I care about Black!", the sound of Apparition came from the garden. Lily, Sirius, and Remus appeared at the same time. Remus was wearing terrible clothes but looked alert and confident. Sirius was the exact opposite; he was wearing a sports jersey, his face white as a sheet. Severus noticed lipstick marks on his cheeks.
"Here are our witnesses for the upcoming investigation. I picked one up from a party, the other from the forest," Lily said to the Ministry officials. They were all looking at Lily in bewilderment.
Sirius didn't look well. Remus held him and said, "Come on brother, let's sober you up." Arthur went to them too; they brought Sirius to the sink and poured water over his head. Then they turned to the Stunned man.
Peter was young; a man of medium height with a round, slightly chubby face. His messy blonde hair fell over his forehead. The Ministry teams woke Peter up. Peter was bewildered at first, then started objecting almost reflexively. "No... I'm not guilty. I'm not. I didn't do it."
"Liar!" roared Sirius. "You betrayed your friends. You betrayed Lily. Don't lie."
"No! I didn't want to... no betrayal!" Peter was crying now, but no one pitied him.
"We will arrest him immediately," said the official who had asked where Lily was earlier. "I am arranging a court hearing for the morning. Everyone is on holiday, but this is an emergency."
The biggest shock was for Arthur and Molly. They had taken a liar and a hypocrite into their home and introduced him to their children.
Lily turned to the two of them. "What a Christmas... I am truly very sorry for this mess. I ruined your holiday too." Molly hugged her immediately. "Are you crazy? You just saved our whole family's lives."
As Peter was taken to the Ministry of Magic for interrogation and trial, one of the officials stopped and turned to them. "Arthur, Molly, you are witnesses too. But—"
"Of course," Arthur said without hesitation. "We will come. We'll be at the Ministry first thing in the morning." The Christmas mood had completely deflated. Lily told Severus she would go upstairs to check on Harry. It wasn't hard to find her way in the house. Harry was in Molly's room with the Weasley twins.
"What happened, Mum?" he asked innocently. He was taking a bite from the wafer in his hand.
"We will explain," said Molly in a soft voice. "But tomorrow. Now it's bedtime for all of you."
"Okay, but let Harry sleep with us. In this room," said Fred. Lily smiled. "I was going to say that, Molly. We need to leave."
"Oh Lily, don't mention it," said Molly. "Of course Harry can sleep here." As the children shouted cries of victory in unison, Lily kissed her son. "We might come late. You sleep."
Harry was already tired. He went under the quilt without objection. As Lily was leaving the room, Harry called out to her. "Mum... I love you."
-----
Severus, Lily, Sirius, and Remus found themselves at the Ministry within two minutes. Sirius looked himself up and down. "Ah, I wish I had something more serious on," he said. Lily looked in amazement when she realized that what he was wearing was a Juventus jersey. Remus shrugged.
"What are you telling me? This coat isn't even mine. Twenty years old, covered in patches." Lily sighed. "I wore my mother's old dress for tonight too. Anyway... it's fine. You look normal."
Three pairs of eyes turned to Severus simultaneously. "What?" said Severus in a dry voice. "I am the only one among you who looks like I went out shopping." They entered the interrogation room together. The official asked them to explain in turn. The young scribe next to him lifted his quill and began to write, although he looked quite tired.
Severus spoke first. "I knew this group of friends, but the only person I was close with is Lily. Peter's betrayal was obvious because he was the Secret-Keeper. I knew it would be Peter even before the protective charm was cast. I think we all knew."
When he finished his words, he looked at the others. Remus cleared his throat slightly.
"Um... Peter and I were friends for a long time. I was devastated when I heard the news, but due to my special condition, I couldn't be with my friends much."
He didn't have much to tell. Still, the scribe continued to take his notes. Lily started speaking. "I chose Peter as the Secret-Keeper at the last moment. Sirius was in a more dangerous position, an easier target. He said so himself. It sounded convincing. I was persuaded. And we chose Peter."
There was a brief pause. "The charm's effect ended. We were attacked that night. Voldemort threw me aside, I fainted. It wouldn't be an exaggeration to say the house collapsed on top of me when I woke up. First Sirius came. Then Severus. Finally Hagrid and Albus Dumbledore.
Everyone suspected Sirius at first. But I... I told them all that the Secret-Keeper was Peter."
Sirius: "I will [redacted] that rat—" His sentence turned into a tirade full of words that couldn't be committed to paper. The scribe
paused his quill in the air. "I cannot write what you said, but it is certain that you spoke extremely... roughly."
Lily leaned in and whispered in Sirius’s ear. "Sirius, please pull yourself together. It's all about to end." Sirius didn't stop. It was as if he had no intention of closing a door he had kept shut for a long time.
"After James died in a very stupid accident, I started seeing Peter more often. He explained the nature of grieving to me. He changed my perspective. He was like a real friend back then. With Harry, the four of us started hanging out. At that time, Lily was living in Remus’s
parents' house. Then she left there. She moved to a more protected house. Everything was under control with the Fidelius Charm."
The tone of his voice dropped. "At that exact point, I suggested Peter. Lily accepted. For him to betray our friendship of so many years in this way... It's terrible."
The official took a deep breath and added: "As a result of the examinations performed, we also detected the Dark Mark on Peter."
With these words, he put a period to the interrogation. A suppressed anger and heavy weariness could be read on the four faces before him.
While Severus and Lily were preparing to stop by The Burrow first and then return home with Harry, Sirius stopped them.
"It's four o'clock. Dawn is near."
They hadn't expected the interrogation to last this long. Sirius continued:"I say go home. You look miserable. Get some sleep."
Severus seemed ready to object, but silenced when Lily quietly said, "Alright. Let's go home." Lily and Severus arrived at Spinner's End together. When they reached the house, Lily practically fell apart. She collapsed onto the armchair, staring as if into a void.
"Come, let's go to bed," Severus said in a low voice.
"I relived everything," said Lily.
"It will pass when you sleep," said Severus. Lily stood up with difficulty, took the hand Severus extended, and followed him upstairs.
She stopped for a moment when they entered the room.
"Um..." she said. "Will you hold me until I sleep?" No sound came from the young man. But his eyes said the answer had already been given.
(Flashback) Severus POV, March 22, 1979
They had just finished breakfast in their house in central London. Severus took his coat and put it over his shoulders. He looked at the people walking in front of the window.
The sooner he finished this, the better. Before he got mixed up with anyone. Lily had already
Apparated to the Ministry.
He left the house and Apparated. A moment later, he was in Hogsmeade. His recent contact,
Regulus Black, was waiting for him in front of a bar. Like two strangers, they went inside.
They sat at a dark table that received no daylight. Regulus began.
“I don't know, Severus, you're refusing for the second time, and a third offer won't come.
Why, brother?”
“Regulus, I remind you I already have a job, at the Ministry.”
“This isn't like a side job. Frankly, I'm having fun,” Regulus said. “If you prove yourself,
you'll earn the Dark Mark too. I want to earn it.”
“Then good luck to you in the game. Count me out.”
“You’re making a mistake, Severus. There are many aspects you could develop with us. You
are a talented wizard.”
“I won't join you, that's it. And stop spying on the house where I stay.”
Regulus smiled. Too relaxed. A smile that knew too much.
“Being lovers with the attractive redhead Evans shouldn't hinder your career jump, Severus,
take my advice.”
His blood pounded in his ears for a moment. He was on the verge of losing control—he
remembered where he was.
“You will not say a single word about her, to anyone.”
“She's your toy, mate, not mine.”
“And you need to keep your advice to yourself.” He left the bar without even buying a drink.
He saw a familiar face at the door. Remus Lupin. His gaze... pity, shock, sadness. Mixed and
heavy. Severus didn't look.
But he carried those looks on his back until he Apparated.
Was the Potter group up to something? Or was the full moon close?
He returned to his job at the Ministry. He worked as if everything was normal. He left when
the shift ended.
When he arrived home, Lily was standing by the window. She was looking at the street. She
had a bottle of wine in her hand. She was drinking from the bottle. She looked sad.
Severus paused. He hadn't seen Lily drunk before.
“Ah, you made it.” This wasn't Lily's voice. Her eyes were red.
“Merlin’s sake, what happened?”
He reached her in three steps. Took the bottle. Wrapped one arm around Lily. Lily started to
cry. Severus, freed from the bottle, hugged her with his other arm too.
But suddenly, Lily broke free from his arms furiously.
“You told me it was over, but you’re still hanging out with Death Eaters, that’s what’s
happening! When will I ever trust you, Severus?”
Was this the issue? He almost smiled.
“I’m not hanging out. Just Regulus Black. I made a promise and I’m keeping it, Lily.”
Lily sniffled. “So what did you talk about?”
“He came to me with a job offer again. It was actually the second offer. Just like the first—”
“So you were meeting with Death Eaters before too?”
“Lily... if you let me explain—”
“I didn't tell you to join the Order, after all, I'm not a member either, but can't you even stay
neutral?”
“Lily, no, it's not like that, please let me explain.”
“I ignored the slanders about Slytherin house, but isn't there a huge grain of truth? Do all of
you have a weakness for the Dark Arts?”
“My love.”
The word came out of his mouth on its own. Lily stopped.
“I only spoke with Regulus. I swear.”
“So what's the second job offer thing?”
“That was Regulus too. Look... the reason they want me is that I was successful at school.
But I explained in no uncertain terms that I don't think like them, I don't want this.”
“Are you telling the truth? So they won't disturb you again, should I believe this? What did
you tell him, Severus?”
“Since when do you doubt what I say?” he said in a calm voice.
“Don't you turn this on me! Don't act like I'm the manipulative one!!”
“I'm not saying anyth- my love, but for you to disbelieve me this much—”
He stopped. Took a breath.
“I'm sorry. I don't want an argument. Please don't cry, my love.”
Lily approached silently, hugged him. She liked Severus calling her “my love”. Something
warmed in her heart.
But the matter wasn't over.
“Okay. Let's talk calmly.”
She sat on the chair. Severus next to her.
“I told Regulus I wasn't interested in the job, I told him the reason. Besides, why would I
accept it? The job offer is fundamentally wrong by nature because I already have a job. Eh,
the place inviting me isn't a gaming club either.”
Lily was thinking.
“Also, Regulus saw that you live in this house. He spoke a bit out of line, I silenced him.”
“What did he say about me?”
“He implied as if you were my toy.”
“Eh, true though.”
Severus went white as a sheet in an instant, his blood drained. “Pardon? I think I misheard,
Lily?”
Lily took a deep breath. “Look, this ‘gaming club’ doesn't seem like it will leave you alone,
Severus. You are dangerous and I will be a toy in your hands.”
She swallowed. Cried. “Severus... let's go.”
“Go where?”
“Abroad. Let's run away. But they'd find us there too, Severus.”
She didn't even wipe her eyes. Something fell into place.
“Where did you learn? About what happened today,” Severus asked.
“Remus. He was going to drink beer with Peter, saw you and Regulus. He told me you... told
me you had a dark aura. I told him no. No. Severus doesn't hang out with Death Eaters. I said
he must have met someone else, not Regulus.”
She paused. “But you told me yourself you met with Regulus. Remus didn't lie.”
“Should I have lied, Lily? If I had given the name of someone not at the bar, you wouldn't be
crying tonight.”
“What kind of logic is that, Severus?”
“It's not logical, that's what I'm trying to say.”
He held her hands. Lily liked the warmth of his hands. But when Severus continued speaking,
Lily froze.
“Besides, since when does the werewolf Lupin send you news?” he said. “You aren't even in
contact anymore.”
Lily’s face changed instantly. He knew this look. She was hurt.
“Severus,” she said. “That is very hurtful.”
Severus didn't look away. Nor did he step back. “Which part?” he said.
“The man is a werewolf, Lily. I didn't state it to be hurtful. Really, he hasn't called or asked
about you all year. Where was he?”
Lily pulled her hands from him. “That is Remus’s private matter.”
Severus’s mouth turned into a thin line.
“Ah, let me explain.” His eyes were on fire. “He couldn't find the job he sought after
graduating, his friends turned their backs on him too, and he is using you to find an easy job
at the Ministry where the workload is light, possibly not working during full moons.”
Lily shook her head sharply.
“No Severus, Remus is not someone like that. He didn't even imply such things, are you
crazy?”
Was he crazy? No. He was just calculating.
“If he didn't say it today, he will say it tomorrow, Lily,” he said.
“He will tell you ‘You were my close friend, we were prefects together’, remind you of the
past.”
“He never talks about such things! Severus, don't, you don't know him.”
Severus’s voice came out lower, sharper this time.
“I would like to know him, Lily. I like seeing that I am wrong. But the man hasn't replied to
two of your owls since graduation. I wish he were eager to be friends with you, dinner here or
something... Did you ask him for an explanation, Lily? Did you get a logical enough answer
to justify him ignoring you?”
Lily fell silent for a moment. “It was logical enough,” she said. Then, she added almost in a
whisper: “How much Remus’s actions affected you.”
Severus retorted without thinking. “Because I take notes of the details, Lily. Especially the
ones about you.”
Silence fell. This silence was the thing Severus knew best. Lily got up from the chair, walked
to the window. She had turned her back.
“Forget Remus, please,” she said. “I think I hurt you by being too hasty in jumping to
conclusions, I'm sorry Sev.”
Her apology was soft. But Severus’s mind had already shifted in another direction. “So what
was the purpose of Remus meeting with you?” he said.
If Severus could, he would have stopped himself. His words were poisonous, even if
unintentional.
“Just to report where I wander, or is he carrying information to you about someone?”
“I don't understand. Information about whom?”
“James Potter. He's been trying to enter your life for months. Or are you still seeing each
other? Does he still pick you up and take you on dates?”
Lily froze. “Sev? No. No, I am not seeing James. Never.”
That was the moment the lock in Severus’s mind opened. “Because he came here once,” he
said. He had never told Lily this.
“It's been about five months. I couldn't see you at home and asked Amanda downstairs where
you were. She practically described James to me.”
Lily couldn't say anything, which offended him even more. “What the hell were you doing
with that pig that day, Lily?”
Lily’s voice trembled.
“That pig you call James took me to a funeral that day, Severus. His mother had passed away.
He has no family anymore either, only his friends. He is no different from us.”
Severus’s insides turned dark.
“Oh God. Am I going to pity James now?”
Lily’s patience snapped. “Maybe you are the real heartless pig, Severus.”
The word hit its target. Severus’s voice remained equally calm, but he was burning inside.
“Lily, I can give up my own life for you.” He paused for a moment.
“You are my everything. But James doesn't see you that way. No one will, Lily. I wish you
would give a chance to the people who are truly by your side as much as you give to those
trying to take advantage of you.”
He hadn't said it to hurt her. But the result didn't change. The wine bottle grazed past
Severus’s head. It hit the wall and shattered.
Lily left the kitchen. Severus remained alone.
With a spell, he vanished the glass and the wine.
Lily was in her room. Severus thought about knocking on the door and going in, but both
needed time.
The next day Lily was sad. Severus prepared breakfast for her as usual. Lily thanked him in a
joyless voice and left the house. Instead of sitting and eating with him in the kitchen, he had
packed it for her to eat on the way.
After two weeks passed with minimal contact, Severus was now sure this was a breakup.
But Lily wasn't saying anything. If they talked, Severus would be sure. Yet all efforts at
communication remained fruitless.
Even Amanda had noticed the tension between them. Severus considered getting help from
her, but what would he say? Amanda was a Muggle. No, he couldn't explain this.
Two weeks later, Lily left the house. Her departure had been sudden. While Severus was
always waiting for an opportunity, he found a long note instead.
‘Sev,
I think I will remember you calling me “my love” for the rest of my life. This word gave me
great happiness and deep relief. It was the strongest emotion I felt for the last two weeks.
I truly understood what it is like to be someone's “love” for the first time. But I have to return
this to you.
As long as conditions continue like this, we cannot proceed on a common path. We both
planted seeds of doubt in each other without realizing.
Could we have continued under other circumstances, or would we eventually have consumed
each other, I don't know. The only thing I know is this: I am consuming you. Whereas your
flesh, bone, and blood belong to you.
Moreover, while the demons chasing you don't want people like me to live, where can we
run? To what extent can we belong to each other?
I have many questions in my mind, but I see only one direction: Emergency exit.
I fear for your life as much as my own, Severus. They are demons.
I hope you have a life without them in the future stages of your life. The Legal Department at
the Ministry is your home. Don't ever leave it.
Take care of yourself.
Lily’
(Now)
On the first Wednesday of the new year, Peter was sentenced to thirty-five years in Azkaban.
Sirius was less interested in rejoicing at this news and more stuck on something else entirely.
“What do you mean… I have to get a visa?”
Lily smiled.
“Look,” she said, “you went all the way to Italy in your Animagus form. All of that is
forgiven now. But if you say you’re going again, at least do it legally.”
“Why do I have to report where I’m going to higher institutions?”
“Sugar,” said Lily calmly, “it’s either the Ministry of Magic or a flight by plane. You have to
choose one.”
The next day, Sirius waved the ticket he bought from the travel agent at his friends like a flag.
Remus fell off the armchair laughing.
“I don't understand,” Severus said, looking at Sirius as if a second head had sprouted from his
shoulder. “Your business at the Ministry would have taken half an hour at most. Now you’re
going to sit in an air vehicle for three hours and wait to arrive?”
“It can’t be that bad,” Sirius giggled. “Muggles do it all the time.”
Reality proved to be harsher.
Two days after Sirius left by plane, an owl arrived from him.
“My luggage is lost,” he wrote. “Can you believe it? I called them, and they mumbled about
insurance and such. I felt so ignorant I could only say ‘find me my suitcase’.”
Lily wrote down what he needed to do item by item. As she sent the owl back, she added a
note at the bottom: “Please go to the Italian Ministry of Magic and register yourself.”
Severus was watching Lily with a mischievous expression. Lily threw herself onto the
armchair and winked at Severus.
“After these recent events,” Severus began, “we haven’t practiced Occlumency.”
Lily closed her eyes. “No. Not now.” They had played in the park with Harry all afternoon,
and Severus was tired too.
“I know you’re tired,” said Severus. “But you must be vigilant at every moment. Physical
exhaustion shouldn't control your mind. Come on, don't be afraid. I won’t push you too hard
today.”
“You always say that, Sev,” said Lily. Still, she straightened up and took her wand in hand.
Within a few seconds, Severus was in the familiar mind. But this time it was different. In just
a few weeks, Lily had learned to protect the boundaries of her mind like a fortress. When he
looked around, he saw many doors, each opening to a memory.
This place resembled a basement with sturdy locks. Severus headed towards the nearest door.
The moment he turned the handle, chains appeared over the door.
Very good.
'I am not the enemy,' he thought. 'But perhaps I am. How would you know?'
He tried the second door. The doorknob was hot, like newly forged iron. He didn't feel
physical pain, but his hand burned; he pulled back by reflex.
'So,' he thought, 'will you fight? Throw me out of your mind with magic.'
At this point, Lily wasn't that good yet. She could only remove Severus from her mind
through concentration.
He turned his gaze to another door. As soon as Severus looked, vines wrapped around the
door; the wooden surface disappeared completely.
“Locks and traps are clever,” said Severus. “But if you really want to keep someone out, you
must cast a counter-spell. Otherwise, someone could just cast Alohomora inside your mind.”
He could feel Lily struggling. But she needed to learn this.
The cellar she had built was impressive; yet without grasping the most difficult detail of
Occlumency, none of this was functional.
Severus went back to the first door. Just as he was about to open it, Lily retaliated.
Well done—
Oh no. Whatever Lily did, suddenly they were in Severus’s mind.
It was a ground-breaking success.
Severus was watching the day of the graduation ceremony. Lily was watching too.
For everyone else, this was a fresh start. For Severus, it was a breaking point. It was the day
he separated from Hogwarts’ order and system.
Lily was looking at Severus, long and without hiding. This wasn't an invitation. There was no
implication, no game, no postponed feelings. Lily was waiting for Severus to take a step.
Severus, in those seconds, believed that no matter what he did, he would lose Lily. All the
possibilities he ran in his mind led him to the same result. That was why he remained passive,
unable to give Lily the answer she expected at graduation.
That was why it took almost a year for their relationship to evolve from friendship to
romance.
Severus removed Lily from his mind with a non-verbal spell.
“You let me see that,” said Lily. There was no accusation in her voice. Severus had expected
accusation.
“I have no secrets from you,” said Severus, smiling. “Am I to hide my feelings and past fears
from my lover?”
Lily blushed. “I wish you could have told me you thought like that.”
“I wish,” said Severus. “But the current you and that eighteen-year-old you are not the same
person. Which counter-spell did you use?”
“A Shield Charm,” Lily said, smirking.
-----
Two weeks later, Sirius came to London, this time by train.
“My suitcase is with me, I never let it leave my side so I didn't lose it,” he said as soon as he
walked through the door, “but I am finished, I am dead. I want a beautiful, grand ceremony.
Everyone should weep.”
It was an exaggeration, yes. But the travel fatigue was real. His shoulders were slumped, his
voice looser than usual, almost sloppy.
Lily took him to Harry’s room without argument.
“Sleep here for now.”
“Can I sleep for twenty-four hours?”
“Of course,” said Lily, laughing. “Why not.”
A few hours later, Severus came home; he had been preparing all week for a job interview at
the Ministry. Afterward, he had stopped by the market; he had two net bags in his hands. Lily
took the bags from his hands, left them on the counter, and suddenly threw herself into
Severus’s arms.
She began kissing his face and lips.
“My love—”
“Hmm.”
Severus had caught her effortlessly. While thinking about which wall to lean Lily against, a
giggle poured cold water over them. The couple let go of each other, Lily fixing her hair.
Harry was outside. He was looking in through the window; his mouth was open, looking like
he was laughing.
Five minutes before arriving home, Severus had said, “I need to talk to the flowers,” and
offered no further explanation.
When Lily, curious, had followed him out to the garden, Harry was indeed explaining things
to the flowers. Severus had arrived right after.
“He was going to see us sooner or later,” Lily said, slight anxiety in her voice. “I wonder
what he’ll say?”
Just then, Harry opened the door and came in. He was still giggling.
“So, what did you talk to the flowers about?” asked Lily, as if it were an ordinary afternoon.
“My dream,” said Harry. After a short pause, he added: “What I’m going to do when I grow
up.”
“That’s very interesting,” said Lily. “What will you do in the future?”
“Be a pilot. I’m going to fly planes.”
Lily and Severus looked at each other. Actually… it wasn't impossible. Harry was giggling
again. “You did a kiss,” he said. “That’s very funny.”
Then, laughing loudly, he went to his own room.
“Harry, wait—” started Lily, but Harry shouted from inside: “Aaaah, Sirius is here!”
Lily turned to Severus. “Do you think I should get Harry out of the room? Sirius was very
tired.”
Before Severus could answer, laughter rose from inside.
“Ah... Never mind,” he said finally. “Harry can't see him as much as he wishes anyway. It’ll
be good for both of them.”
Then his attention turned completely to Lily. “It was a nice kiss,” he said. “Did you really
miss me that much?”
“I miss you every moment, Severus,” said Lily. “Even at work.”
“Thank goodness no one who performs Legilimency works in your department.”
Lily didn't laugh much at this joke. “Sev,” she said, and hit his arm lightly. “I can keep my
mind closed now, you know that.”
(Flashback)
Severus POV, January 1981
His housemates were always in pursuit of alcohol, temporary pleasures, and sex. The young
man, however, couldn't help but wonder if one of these nameless women, whose faces left no
trace in his mind, was an undercover Dementor. They seemed to him like entities that only
took, consumed, and left a void behind. Because for a while now, there had been a deficiency
within him too.
His body was still here perhaps, but his soul felt as if it had been forgotten somewhere else.
Severus Snape could no longer wake up in the mornings. Towards noon, he would open his
eyes with a suffocating weight settling on his chest. He had left his job. It wasn't possible for
him to stay in that house while penniless.
Then Lucius, with that cold and self-serving generosity of Death Eaters, gave him a flat. A
few Death Eaters were staying there together.
Yes. Severus was now one of them.
He hated himself most for this reason. Still, he held onto the fact that he hadn't taken the Dark
Mark. This meant low level. Less connected. Less visible.
But in time, a person resembled those around them.
He hated one more person: James Potter.
The Golden Boy. The school favourite. With an arrogance that led him to his death, he had
made an unforgivable mistake at the potion cauldron. He had risked his life, as he always did,
without taking it seriously. He was a careless, conceited, rough man even in the simplest
things.
Still, Severus would not speak ill of the dead. Especially knowing Lily bore such obvious
grief.
Lily hadn't left his mind since she broke up with him. Now, he jumped at everything just so
his mind wouldn't drift to her. He asked for new tasks, following Lucius around like a puppy.
Thinking of Lily was weakness. She was forbidden. She had a Muggle family.
But Lily was single now.
No. Lily was still forbidden. She was a member of the Order. Still... Lily could choose him.
Then reality intervened like a knife: Lily had a child. She had to choose him. Not Severus.
A teardrop fell on the pillow. Severus was lying on his side in bed. He didn't want to get up.
He didn't want to start the day.
What if Lily had been in the house too when that accursed house exploded?
This thought shattered his mind. No. Harm could not come to Lily. That was why Severus
would stay away. He wouldn't approach. If he approached, he would become a target. Lily
too, the child too.
The meeting was at Malfoy Manor.
Severus Apparated along with a few Death Eaters. Except Regulus Black. Regulus, who
stayed in the room next door and frequently brought girls home. Aside from him, he had
come with the others in the flat. They went inside.
Voldemort was terrifying. He looked like a repulsive, fear-inducing, weak man. But this was
an illusion. Physical frailty was the most dangerous cover. The real threat was in the wand he
carried.
Everyone started talking about weekly or monthly tasks. Voldemort was explaining his plans.
Severus passed a single word through his mind: terrorism.
His mind was strong. This was a condition for anyone wanting to rise among Death Eaters. It
was essential. If your emotions, fears, and goals were obvious, you were already dead.
Regulus took the floor. Severus realized at that moment that he was at the table. What he told
was terrible. He was telling it proudly, as if he had achieved success. He was even boasting. It
was disgusting.
Then the subject changed. A prophecy.
There was someone who would destroy Voldemort. A baby born this year.
The Dark Lord wasn't one to mask his annoyance with other things, but... his silence grew
heavy.
“Lucius,” he said with his high, serpentine voice. “What do you think?”
“My Lord,” said Lucius. “Let us find the child and the family and eliminate them. A clean
job.”
A few people he trusted agreed. Only Regulus, looking at Voldemort, said, “Let us wait and
see, my Lord.”
“Forgive me. But fate already invites you to a duel, my Lord. The inevitable shows itself
when the time comes.”
Severus tensed. Since learning the first concepts of wizardry, prophecy had never felt warm
to him. It was unreliable. It was uncanny. And it could never be left to chance.
So who was this unlucky child?
April 1981
While most Death Eaters were investigating babies born that year or the previous one,
Severus had suddenly said, “There are wizarding families outside of England too, you know
that, right?”
This sentence created an earthquake effect, first among his housemates, then among almost
half of the Death Eaters.
Those who bore the Dark Mark Apparated with eternal loyalty to the cities Voldemort sent
them to.
Severus breathed easily for the first time. Now he could look ahead. While thinking of going
to Lily’s neighbourhood to look for her, fate brought her before him at the Ministry of Magic.
Her hair wasn't long like before; it came down straight to her shoulders. The coat she wore
was pink. Pink was definitely not Lily’s colour. The black beanie on her head was passable.
She had purple bags under her eyes. Either from sleeplessness or crying. Maybe both.
She froze when she noticed Severus. “Severus… what are you doing here?”
“I was looking for you. I had a small job here but…” he paused. “I’ve been trying to reach
you for two days.”
“Is that so?” said Lily. “So you heard.”
“Heard what?” Severus was bewildered.
Lily looked at him. “Everyone is talking, Severus. I mean James.”
“Ah. Right.” said Severus. “It slipped my mind. I am sorry for your loss but—”
“If you have something to say to me, say it immediately,” Lily said with weariness.
“It’s not like that. It is really important.” His voice sharpened. “Please, let’s talk outside.”
Lily flinched. Severus used to soften his voice when making a request. Begging was… this
wasn't the man she knew.
But Lily had already left him.
They went out and entered a small Muggle café. Severus ordered two espressos.
“You drink coffee?” asked Lily, in surprise.
“Regulus Black got me used to it,” said Severus. “Listen, Lily…”
Espresso was the right choice. Lily’s brain working fast depended on that small cup.
“So Voldemort suspects Harry?” Lily’s voice had turned to ice.
“Not yet. I am trying to distract them.” He paused for a moment. “But you must be protected.
Move to my father’s house, to Spinner's End. We will apply all the wards. And you must
choose Sirius Black for the Fidelius Charm.”
Lily looked at him in shock.
“Even if a hundred people cast Crucio at the same time,” said Severus, “Sirius would at most
die, he still wouldn't talk.”
“Stop,” said Lily. “This is too sudden.”
“There is no time, Lily. Please. You must act immediately.” His whisper was harsh. He was
losing control. If Voldemort went after them… what could he do?
THE END OF PART 1
-----
PART 2: The Weight of Souls
On the morning of July 28, 1983, three days before Harry’s third birthday, Lily Evans woke
up and stretched in bed. Today was a day off, she didn’t need to go to the Ministry, but she
had woken up early out of habit. She turned her head to the side and looked at the young man
sleeping beside her.
Severus was sleeping like a rare, giant starfish. On his back. Free. His left arm had slipped
under Lily’s pillow. When he got hot in the night, he had taken the cover off himself and
placed it over Lily; so she wouldn’t get cold. Unaware of how meaningless this was on a July
night.
Or perhaps exactly because of that. Lily suppressed a laugh.
She got out of bed without waking her lover, put on her dressing gown, and went down to the
kitchen.
Harry must be sleeping too; she was treading lightly on the floor so as not to disturb him. She
used magic to wash the dirty dishes left from the evening; meanwhile, she toasted bread and
made an omelette.
While the clock showed 7:42, an owl carrying a newspaper landed on the window. Lily paid
the money, took the newspaper, closed the window, and turned around—
She collided with Severus.
Lily must have jumped two meters in the air, while her lover was laughing at the situation,
trying to make amends.
“My intention was not to scare you.”
“I understand, yes. If you had wanted to scare me, I suppose I would have fainted.” Severus
laughed, took a slice of toasted bread, and took a bite.
“I still don’t know how you can eat that without jam?” said Lily, taking both bread and jam
herself.
“I never eat jam. I have an allergy. It must be homemade,” said Severus laughing, having
picked up the omelette pan and started serving.
“You’re joking!”
“I really am not. An endless wave of coughing takes over me.”
“But I’ve known you for years, Sev, how did I not notice?”
Severus shrugged. “It is because I never showed a reaction... because I never eat jam.”
“That is very sad,” said Lily, picking up the newspaper. “For example, if I become diabetic in
the future, I wouldn't want to think about the things I’d have to give u— WHAT IS THIS?”
Severus lifted his head, looked at Lily. Then he looked at the headline in the newspaper.
HEROIC WIDOW, DARK SHADOW?
Lily Evans (Potter)’s “unexpected” closeness divides the wizarding world.
Special Report | Rita Skeeter
Wizarding Britain holds its breath once more.
Lily Evans (who dropped the Potter surname last year), widowed by the rise of the Dark Lord
and symbolized by her courage and sacrifice, was recently seen with Severus Snape, a former
Slytherin alumnus and one-time suspect.
Yes, you read that correctly.
The Heroic Widow & The Former Suspect
COINCIDENCE, OR A DANGEROUS BOND?
According to our reliable sources, the pair has been seen for weeks late at night in the same
inconspicuous, shabby venues. The occasional presence of little Harry Potter alongside them
has heightened the seriousness of the matter. While Ministry officials refrain from
commenting on the subject, a name from the Department of Magical Law Enforcement, where
Severus Snape works, whispered this sentence in our ear:
“The Snape file was never completely closed.”
This sentence? Not a coincidence.
IS THE SLYTHERIN SHADOW RISING AGAIN?
Severus Snape was known for his interest in the Dark Arts during his Hogwarts years, and
his name was frequently mentioned in Death Eater circles. Although there are no formal
charges, Snape’s past still harbours many question marks.
So, could Lily Potter not know this? Or does she know… and is ignoring it?
WHAT DOES THE PUBLIC SAY?
The opinions of Daily Prophet readers are harsh:
“James Potter must be turning in his grave at this.”
“Heroism does not suit something like this.”
“I worry for the child.”
IS MOTHERHOOD BEING QUESTIONED?
According to experts, the real issue is not love. The real issue is: Harry Potter. “Who a child
grows up with determines their future,” says a Mind Healer from St Mungo's. “And Snape…
is a controversial figure.”
FINAL WORD
Is this relationship an innocent friendship? Or the harbinger of dark days?
The Daily Prophet will follow developments closely.
Because sometimes the most dangerous secrets… are hidden in the quietest corners of hearts.
Lily placed the newspaper slowly on the counter. She was holding the paper with her
fingertips, as if something would break if she pressed too hard.
“She dropped the Potter surname last year,” she murmured. Her voice was calm. Too calm.
Severus said nothing. He placed the omelette on the plate and left the spatula on the counter.
He wasn't looking at Lily’s face. He wasn't looking at the newspaper. But he knew every
word by heart.
“Harry…” Lily couldn't complete the sentence. Her eyes slid up to the stairs for a moment.
“Dragging him into this—”
“I do not accept this,” Severus said, without his voice sharpening. “They can write about me.
They can write about my past. But the child—no.”
Lily looked at him fully for the first time. At that moment she realized: Severus wasn't angry.
He wasn't offended. He was worried.
“You were expecting this, weren't you?” asked Lily.
Severus paused. Then he nodded his head very slightly. “For a while,” he said. “I just didn't
think it would be this soon.”
Lily sat in her chair. Her shoulders slumped.
“My name is Lily Evans now,” she said slowly. “They wrote that specifically.”
“I know.”
“And yet...” Lily pressed her lips together. “I am still the ‘heroic widow’.”
A fleeting expression appeared on Severus’s face. Like a needle. Like pain. “They are trying
to turn you into something,” he said. “Into a symbol. Taking you out of being human, into an
idea.”
“And you?” Lily looked at him. “What are you becoming?”
Severus’s answer was delayed. Lily stood up. She folded the newspaper, neatly. Then she
threw it in the bin. “I will not run,” she said. “Neither from obsessive journalists nor from
gossip.”
Severus was looking at her carefully. “I wasn't going to ask this of you,” he said. “But I want
you to know… if this becomes too heavy for you, I can leave for your sakes.”
“Stop,” said Lily. “Don't retreat now. How can you say that?” Severus fell silent.
“I chose this relationship,” Lily said with a clear voice. “The newspapers didn't choose it. The
Ministry didn't choose it. Rita Skeeter certainly didn't choose it.”
Severus understood again at that moment. His tone was soft.
“As long as you want me by your side, I will walk with you. You are quite right. We will not
run.”
And he thought inwardly:
If this is a war, I won't retreat either.
-----
When Severus closed the heavy wooden door of the Department of Magical Law
Enforcement, the air inside changed. No one was speaking, but gazes turned to him. Not with
curiosity. With a state of alarm.
“Ah, Snape,” said the elderly witch, lifting her head from behind the files. “We didn't speak
to anyone.”
Severus stopped. He didn't take off his robes. He didn't go to his desk either. The six people
he worked with reacted almost simultaneously.
“No.”
“Absolutely not.”
“That woman didn't even set foot here.”
A young wizard leaned forward. His voice was low. “She's a nutter. She writes even if you
don't talk. Even if you do. She's not worth paying attention to.”
“She distorts her sources. She cuts sentences. And in the end, she puts her own sentences in
everyone's mouths.”
Severus nodded slightly. He knew this. Still, hearing it was something else.
“So, what is this: ‘The Snape file was never completely closed,’” Severus said calmly. “This
sentence. If there really is such a file, let's work on that file tomorrow.”
There was a short silence in the middle of the room. “It didn't come from us,” someone said
in a clear tone. “If it had, you would know.”
Severus took off his robes for the first time and sat in his chair. “If someone from within the
Ministry talked,” said the young wizard, “it wasn't us.”
“And if this article extends to Harry,” added the elderly woman, “then things change.”
Severus’s gaze hardened. “Then,” he said, “this won't be gossip anymore. It becomes the law
itself.”
Towards the end of the shift, Severus received mail from a familiar owl (Errol). Lily had sent
it.
‘Sev,
We are at The Burrow. You come too.
Lils’
He grimaced when he saw the nickname, but happiness covered his insides. Lily, as always,
knew how to make him happy with small things.
When he finished his work, he left the office and Apparated. Within seconds, he had arrived
at the familiar house. The house where Peter had been caught last Christmas.
The atmosphere was warm and calm. The eldest of the children, Bill, was on summer holiday,
and Charlie was going to start school this year.
“Everyone has started asking what I want to be when I grow up,” he whined to Lily. “But I
haven't decided yet.”
“You will have plenty of time to choose,” said Lily. “And after choosing, you will change it
at least five times.”
“What did you want to be?” Charlie asked her curiously.
Before Lily could answer, the other children started intervening.
“I will work at the Ministry too, like my dad.”
“No, me! You find another profession.”
“I want to be a Healer.”
“You can never be, it’s too hard.”
“Why is that?”
The little bickering was very sweet, but Arthur intervened, silencing and calming them all.
Meanwhile, Lily, Molly, and Severus were left alone.
“That disgusting article that woman wrote... How dare she pen this?”
“It was a shock for us too, but we don't even think about it,” said Lily. “After all, it’s fake
news.”
“I confirmed it today too. Her sources are downright fabricated scenarios,” said Severus.
“If she plays such a dirty game again, it won't be good for her.”
Molly smiled understandingly. “Of course. You are family now anyway. All this—no kids,
you can't take Ginny out to the garden.” Her sentence was left unfinished, leaving two
bewildered wizards in the kitchen.
“Wow,” said Lily. “It sounded very surreal when someone else said it. We have become a
family already, haven't we?”
Severus smirked. “What were you hoping for a year ago when you left Harry with me and
went to work?”
“I suppose, exactly this life. Just as it is now,” said Lily. “Even if I couldn't tell myself that.”
“Wouldn't you want to change anything at all?”
“Nuh-uh,” said Lily, her eyes shining with mischief.
-----
When Harry’s birthday arrived three days later, there was no big, fancy birthday party.
Lily and Severus took him to a park outside. They spent the whole afternoon watching the
ducks swimming in the pond and eating ice cream. Harry laughed most at the ducks
snatching pieces of bread.
Afterward, Severus had to stop by the Ministry for some boring paperwork he needed to
finish. By late afternoon, Sirius and Remus arrived with a birthday cake. It seemed unlikely
Severus would make it; Lily cut a slice for him too, and Remus packed it carefully.
“I don't want to criticize your choice in men, Lily, but,” Sirius said in an unserious voice,
“was there really no one else but Snape?”
Remus frowned. “If Lily is happy with him, we can't interfere, Sirius.”
Sirius shrugged. “I wonder about that too. Lily, are you really happy with him?” Lily giggled.
She could have shut Sirius up with a cheeky answer if she wanted, but she didn't choose that.
She looked at the two curious faces opposite her and smiled. “I am content when I'm with
him,” she said. “His cooking, his taking care of Harry, his never upsetting me, and lots of
things I can't think of right now... So yes, Sirius, he makes me happy too.”
“Our Snivellus was the man of the year and we didn't know. Just get married already, then.”
Lily turned slightly pink.
“Or is there a wedding soon?” Remus pressed. Sirius whistled. “Do you want to catch the
bridal bouquet again, my dear Remus? Do you have a serious relationship hidden from me?”
“Look Lily,” said Sirius, “Remus is one of the last bachelors among us. If you break up with
Snape, all he has to do to win your heart is cook!”
As soon as the sentence finished, a dish sponge hit his head.
“There is no such thing yet,” said Lily. “We haven't talked about it at all.” Apart from that, it
was a very sweet evening, and the two dispersed to their homes later.
Before summer ended, on the last weekend of August, Severus and Lily took three days off.
They didn't tell anyone where they were going; just camping.
They had been here before. It was a place remaining from the days when only the two of
them lived in London. It had no name on maps. An ordinary puddle for Muggles, a lake that
liked to be forgotten for wizards. Its water was always cool, its wind light; it didn't hold
sounds.
Lily and Harry were laughing, trying to skip stones. Harry couldn't manage it at all, but
failure didn't weary him. With every attempt, he leaned towards the lake again. Severus was
watching them as if he could watch forever if he wanted. In the afternoon, the sun seeped
more distinctly through the clouds. Lily and Severus took off their shoes and jackets; dipped
their feet in the lake. The water was freezing. Harry had fallen asleep in the shade.
Ten minutes later, Lily picked up the sunscreen. “We need to apply it again,” she said.
“Otherwise we’ll get burned.”
Even if the threat was small, they had taken precautions. Severus squeezed plenty of cream
into his palm; carefully applied it to Lily’s shoulders, arms, and back. Lily applied cream to
him in the same way. Lily looked at Harry sleeping in the back. “Do you think he will
remember this?” she asked.
Severus shrugged. “Maybe not. But we will remember.”
In the late afternoon, Harry woke up. They stayed by the lake until sunset; threw stones into
the water, walked, let time pass without needing to rush anywhere.
-----
September 15, 1983
A rainy September day, normal as always. In the evening hours, an owl tapped on the
window with its beak. Lily and Harry were colouring together in a new Muggle colouring
book.
Severus let the owl in. The sender was Sirius: “we need to meet urgently, Hogsmeade
tomorrow, 20:00.” Both wondered why he didn't come to the house or why he wasn't more
explanatory in the letter.
The next day Severus wasn't working, Lily had gone to work via the Floo network. Both were
at home with Harry; they didn't even go out to the garden, played games at home. Still no
news from Sirius.
At 20:00, since there was nowhere else to leave him, they took Harry too and went to
Hogsmeade. When they Apparated, they saw Sirius. His face was frantic. He directed them
upstairs. His expression was so sad that he couldn't even joke with Harry. Dumbledore was
there too. Lily and Severus greeted him quickly, but Dumbledore cut them off:
“The situation is very serious.”
Sirius continued: “Regulus is dead. While duelling with Rabastan Lestrange, 20 metres away
from me. I couldn't intervene because he... he cast a Body-Bind Curse on me. That's why...
that's why he lost the upper hand in the duel and... and...”
He couldn't speak any further, he was crying. Lily hugged him, and Harry snuggled closer to
Severus. Dumbledore continued: “Before Regulus died, he left two very unusual objects to
Sirius. Although my theoretical knowledge is limited, this clearly supports, confirms the idea
I have been thinking about for a long time.”
“What kind of objects? What is happening?” said Severus, having lifted Harry into his arms.
“Should we be worried?”
“Have you noticed any sign of force against your protective spells around the house,
Severus?”
“No Death Eater knows my address,” replied Severus.
“Yes, a small possibility, but I had to ask,” said Dumbledore. “Because Regulus’s loss
worries me. As for what we have...”
“He told me to destroy them. Apparently, they need to be destroyed. Because for now, there
are two,” said Sirius. The wide cloth bag was sitting on the table.
“There is a locket and a notebook inside. Actually, a diary. I... Regulus also said don't let the
items touch skin, don't touch them.”
“Why? Dark magic?” asked Lily.
“No, Lily,” said Dumbledore. “Inside these objects is Voldemort’s soul. For this reason, his
return is inevitable, unless we destroy them.”
“If we destroy them, he won't come,” said Severus. “Wonderful. What will we do?”
“These aren't things to be destroyed with simple spells, Severus,” said Lily. She prevented
Harry from wiping a huge booger on his own coat after picking his nose.
“Voldemort must have thought of everything.”
“Not everything, of course,” said Dumbledore, smiling while handing a tissue to Lily.
“There are poisons, fires, curses that even the best of us wouldn't mess with unless necessary.
We will destroy them using those.”
Then he paused. “Rabastan, isn't it? That means Bellatrix and her team are currently on the
loose.”
“And there is no doubt they are looking for these objects,” concluded Severus.
Lily and Severus looked at each other. When their eyes met, there was no need for words;
both felt the weight of the responsibility.
Sirius clenched his fist tight. “Then our priority is to protect them and prevent them from
falling into Bellatrix’s hands.”
Lily asked slowly: “Were you seeing Regulus?”
Sirius: “He sent me an owl four days ago. I was very sure he was mocking me, but then he
called me to our family home and I went. He was there, scared and emaciated. I understood
something was wrong.”
Sirius took a deep breath, his still trembling hands clenched into fists. “Regulus... didn't tell
me exactly what I should do. But he asked me not to let anyone touch the things he would
leave, these objects.”
Severus nodded slowly, rocking Harry gently in his arms. “Objects protected by magic.
Destroying them won't be a simple task.”
Dumbledore added: “Yes. For now, two different protections and two separate curses... A
complex web that needs to be attended to simultaneously. But this is our only chance. If
Bellatrix’s team seizes them, Voldemort’s return accelerates and becomes impossible to
prevent.”
Lily touched his shoulder, her eyes serious. “Severus, we can do it together. If we are
planned...” Severus turned his eyes to Lily, paused for a short moment, then nodded
decisively.
“Yes. Our priority is to safely destroy these objects. We must be careful at every step. It could
be as insidious as Bellatrix’s plan just now.”
Harry had listened to everyone until that moment. He asked with his small voice: “But we
can succeed?” Lily smiled, but there was a slight sadness in her smile.
“Yes, Harry. But we must be careful. This is not just a mission; it is also a test for all of us.”
Dumbledore scanned their eyes, a heavy silence fell in the room. “Be prepared. An operation
spanning tonight, perhaps a few days, will follow. Do not rush, but we do not have the luxury
of delay.”
September 18, 1983, The Longbottom Residence.
The kitchen window exploded. Glass sprayed outwards. Lily Evans leapt through the empty
window frame; the house was already screaming. The walls, the ceiling, the floor…
everything was vibrating with magic. And that laughter.
Bellatrix Lestrange’s laughter wasn't joyful. It was sharp, shrill, and continuous. “You’re
late,” she said. “But this is fun too.” Lily didn't answer.
The first curse shattered the table, the second tore the cabinet doors off. Lily didn't retreat
into defence; she disrupted the space. She made the floor slippery, refracted the light,
thickened the air. Every curse Bellatrix cast had to change direction before finding its target.
Bellatrix’s face tightened. “Ah, clever girl.”
When the pull of the Portkey ceased, Severus fell to his knees. Wooden hangers swayed. He
took a breath, counted to three, and opened the wardrobe door.
Rabastan Lestrange was surprised; Severus’s curse was short, clean, and purposeful. He
slumped to the floor, not knowing what to do.
The corridor narrowed. Barty Crouch Jr appeared, smiling. “Snape, eh. You… shouldn't have
been here.”
“Neither should you,” said Severus. Spells were not fired; minds were probed first. A
momentary contact, a momentary infiltration. The first curses flew from wands
simultaneously. The living room was now like an arena. Frank Longbottom was on his knees
on the floor; his breathing was irregular, but his eyes were still open.
Rodolphus Lestrange was impatient. Before the Cruciatus rose again, Remus intervened. His
magic threw Rodolphus back. Sirius shattered the floor. Rodolphus laughed; it was a bloody
and twisted smile. “We had just started,” said Rodolphus.
Spells bounced off the walls. Sirius was aggressive, Remus was controlled. Rodolphus knew
both of them, and that was why he was dangerous.
In the kitchen, Bellatrix leapt back. Lily’s magic grazed her shoulder. The laughter stopped.
The house took a breath for a moment. Severus paused for a moment. A name flashed in
Barty’s mind and disappeared.
“I wouldn't have expected this from you,” said Bellatrix. “Flower girl.”
“You always expect the wrong things,” said Lily.
Bellatrix attacked. This time directly. The curses were sharp and consecutive. Lily didn't
retreat; she made a shield out of a chair, then the sink, then a tile torn from the floor.
Bellatrix’s enthusiastic expression was wiped away; there was only hunger.
Severus and Barty leapt back at the same time. The walls darkened, the corridor narrowed,
then elongated. When Severus’s magic exploded, Barty was thrown back. Blood dripped onto
the corridor. The smile was wiped away.
“You’re late,” said Barty, through his teeth. “But this is also part of the plan.” Severus took a
step, stopped. Bellatrix’s laughter came from the kitchen like a knife.
Rodolphus raised his hand; he couldn't lower it. Frank lifted his head for a moment. Remus’s
spell locked his wrist, Sirius’s nailed him to the wall. Rodolphus screamed, but not from pain,
from the loss of control.
Frank collapsed to the floor. Remus immediately crouched beside him; he had a pulse, weak
but stubborn.
Bellatrix retreated. Lily’s magic exploded at Bellatrix’s feet. The floor split open. Bellatrix
leapt, leaned against the wall. Her eyes were on Lily’s face. But a shadow appeared behind
her: Severus. They cast a spell at the same time. Bellatrix was thrown back, hit the table. “It’s
not over yet,” she said and Disapparated.
The house was a ruin. The walls were sooty, the air smelled burnt. When Alice was found,
her consciousness was semi-closed. Lily fell to her knees, held her hand. While Remus called
for help for the Longbottoms, Sirius and Severus looked at each other with wordless
communication. They hadn't saved lives today; they had only prevented worse.
-----
The attack on the Longbottoms hadn't been prevented, but they had escaped worse by a hair's
breadth. After a short treatment process, Frank and Alice were able to return to their homes.
“We can’t thank you enough that the Order came immediately,” Frank had said. But Lily
knew this wasn't entirely true. They had been late to the house because of a clever diversion
set up by Bellatrix; everything was because of false addresses directed by the Death Eaters.
Every time this thought came to her mind, her chest tightened. Still, she smiled at her old
school friend. “Any time, Frank. Don't mention it.”
Sleep eluded her at night. Sometimes Bellatrix’s laughter sounded as if it was coming from
inside the wardrobe in the bedroom, and Lily would wake up with a start. Her body reacted
before her brain, jumping in her sleep. Severus felt this too, and in his sleep-drunk state,
would pull Lily towards himself.
Lily could only sleep again by clinging to her lover like a leech. As if she had never
considered the possibility until this moment that Severus could be harmed too. He was
human after all. Flesh and bone.
And Harry… Right in the middle of everything happening, innocent, unaware of the dangers
awaiting him.
While lying awake, she tried to snuggle a bit closer to Severus but they were already skin to
skin. Under her left hand, Severus’s heart pumped blood in a regular rhythm. His right arm
was around Lily’s waist. Severus was in a deep sleep and… Lily lifted her head slightly.
Severus wasn't breathing properly. She listened for a few seconds. Strange; it was as if his
nose was blocked. She didn't want to wake him, but if it went on like this, it was obvious he
would wake up sick in the morning.
Indeed, the next morning Severus’s voice was wheezing faintly. “The last time I had the flu, I
was fourteen. It was about time, wasn't it?” he said, trying to joke. When Lily prepared sage
tea, he drank it without objection. “You don't have to go to work if you want,” said Lily.
“My absence might draw attention,” Severus replied. They had to continue their normal lives.

“So be it,” said Lily, giggling and kissing him a second time.
“Merlin knows,” murmured Severus, “rather than germs… my nose is problematic.”
“What exactly do you need?” asked Lily.
Severus shrugged. He didn't know either. Then he left via the fireplace.
Following Severus’s blocked nose, a cough had appeared. Lily and Harry weren't affected,
but they consciously kept Harry away from Severus. Severus, on the other hand, was doing
more overtime to avoid staying home, clearly delaying his recovery.
Lily resented this but there was nothing she could do. What could she say? Severus was an
adult man. Two months shy of his twenty-fourth birthday, a grown person. Wouldn't he know
how to protect his own health? Still, when Lily realized it was the morning coinciding with

unrest. She noted that irritating cough that hadn't gone away for weeks in her mind.
Remus stopped by Spinner's End after a very exhausting full moon. He didn't have much
energy to talk; he had brought a bottle of wine with him.
They put Harry down for his afternoon nap together.
“Every time I see him, he grows a little more,” said Remus, sighing.
“You can come whenever you miss us,” said Lily. “Although I don't know what you are up to
with Dumbledore.”
“Werewolves were loyal to the Dark Lord, Lily,” said Remus. “So I monitor their activities.”
“Are you a spy?” said Lily, in disbelief.
“Well… I can't say spy,” said Remus, shaking his head. “I don't quite know what I am either.
Is there a corkscrew, or are we opening it with magic?”
They opened the bottle with magic, poured a glass each.
“I don't like drinking alcohol,” said Lily, “but drinking wine is a nice change.”
Then she smiled. “Petunia was crazier than me about this. Tequila, whiskey, rum… She liked
trying new drinks. I wonder if she is still the same, I'm curious now.”
“She can't still hate you,” said Remus. “Hasn't she called you at all after all this?”
Lily paused for a few seconds before answering. “Remus… I didn't tell her. Anything.”
“How?”
“She doesn't even know James is dead.”
Remus froze. “Oh, Lily…”
“I don't want my sister to pity me,” said Lily. “I miss how we were in the past. She could
