don't even know why I thought of her right now.” She shrugged. “I rambled a bit.”
“Maybe she is thinking of you right now too,” said Remus, smiling. “They say there is a
strange bond between siblings.”
“Maybe…” said Lily. They didn't bring up the subject again. They talked about other things.
“Sirius is taking me to a pizzeria tonight,” said Remus, taking his coat. “He only takes me out
when things are bad with girls. I might be offended.”
“Be careful, the pizzeria maybe in Naples,” said Lily, laughing. Remus was bewildered. “He
would really do that. Crazy boy. Luckily we have our Apparition visa.”
“You finally got your visa,” said Lily.
Severus came home at nine in the evening. He entered through the door, not the fireplace.
Lily was so engrossed in a gripping novel in her hand that she hadn't even noticed the time.
“Sev,” she said, folding the corner of the book and getting up from the armchair. “Where
were you, my love?”
Then anxiety covered her inside. Severus didn't look well. “I went to the ER.”
“WHAT?”
Severus explained what had happened to him. That the countless bad-tasting healing potions
he drank hoping they would be good for his cough for weeks only provided short-term
relief… Until a comment from his colleague Abbott made the truth hit home.
“The reason these potions don't work,” he said, “is that they are all anti-infection.”
It was as if he had reinvented gravity. He took three boxes of Muggle medicine from his coat
pocket, went to the kitchen. “We had a mind-opening chat with the doctor too,” he said.
“Good news; I understood what I was reacting to. Bad news—”
He opened the door of the cupboard they used as a pantry. “All of these are forbidden to me.”
Lily had stayed where she was. She was like frozen. “If you had let me know, I would have
come with you, Sev.”
“There was no need,” said Severus, shrugging. “With regular use, I'll see an effect in a
week.” Then he muttered: “I wish there was a potion to eliminate allergic factors.”
“Actually there is,” said Lily. “It has been known for ten years that a mixture made with
Hyacinth and Fluxweed is good for seasonal allergies.”
She paused. “What I wonder is this: Why didn't you let me know when you felt bad at
work?”
Severus was surprised, stared at her. He blinked his eyelashes. “Um… There was really no
need for you to come. Upsetting you for no reason, asking you to get ready and come-”
“Ah, I understand,” said Lily, her voice growing cold. “My presence either upsets you or is
completely unnecessary.”
“Lily, no,” said Severus. His shoulders slumped. “That’s not true, you know it.”
“Men are all the same,” said Lily, with a voice like poison. “No matter how much they love,
they don't want to be partners with the women they are in a relationship with. Us knowing
about an issue, helping, seems to break men’s honour.”
She paused for a moment, then continued: “James was the same. He hid everything from me.
‘No Lily, you won't understand. No, this is wrong. Take care of Harry, I’ll handle it.’ And he
couldn't handle anything!”
“Where is all this coming from?” said Severus. He said nothing else. His lack of defense
provoked Lily even more.
“Where is it coming from?” said Lily. “Very good question. My love, you can't take care of
yourself! Without knowing which potion is good for what, you even taught at Hogwarts.
Luckily, thank Merlin, you thought to go to the doctor, but you excluded me in that too.
Where are we in your life, Severus? Don't you dare say we are at the center and talk like a
poet.”
Severus was listening to Lily with his mouth open. “Tell me,” said Lily. “What am I in your
life? I know nothing. I can't heal you because you didn't even know the problem was
allergic.”
Lily walked around Severus, took a rubbish bag. She started filling the bag with all the junk
food he had just pointed out.
“Those are forbidden to me,” said Severus. “Not to you.”
“WRONG!” said Lily. “Forbidden to all of us. At least until your symptoms subside.” She
tied the bag. “We are in this house together, Sev. But you don't see yourself as together. I
just… I don't know what to do anymore.”
“Wait a second, listen to me,” said Severus. “I apologize. That you would be affected this
much— what is that?”
Lily frowned. When Severus pulled the cardigan on her slightly aside, the chain appeared.
The chain of the locket.
“YOU— are you carrying this on you?”
He couldn't hold his voice back in shock.
“One of us needs to study and destroy these.”
“Crazy woman!” said Severus. “I can't believe you!” While scolding Lily, he carefully
removed the chain from her neck, saving it without getting it tangled in her hair. “This is a
Dark Magic object, Lily!”
“So what? Yes.”
Severus put the locket back into the cloth bag. “These objects can affect you.”
“Severus, do you never listen to what I say? I am here—”
“They could have possessed your soul,” said Severus. “It could have been worse.” He
reached Lily with quick steps, hugged her tightly. “Thank goodness you are fine.”
Lily, strangely, felt an invisible burden slowly lifting off her. Still… “But I am still angry
with you,” she said, without letting go of the hug.
“I know,” said Severus. “I apologize again.”
Following an intense medication process and disregarding Harry crying for fifteen minutes
the first day because there were no crisps left in the cupboard, they got used to their old
routine again.
Bonus: Harry hadn't had another junk food crisis and cried until Christmas.
-----
A week before Christmas, they received a surprise card from Sirius. And via Muggle post.
Lily’s hands were really trembling as she opened the card. Where had this sudden love for
Italy come from, and when had it taken over Sirius?
‘My dear friend, soulmate Lily, Harry, and aftermarket product Severus Snape,
Forgive my joke. Or don't, I don't care.
My dearest Lily, I want very much to host you and your loved ones this Christmas in the tiny
setup I established here.
I couldn't go to my very dear family’s home. Remus’s parents' home also… got mixed up with
my life here a bit.
In conclusion, I am now a registered British wizard legally residing here. Lily, I did this
specifically thinking you would be happy.
I will explain everything when you come.
You are missed.
Via della Speranza, 94 Sicily’
While they were waiting with great curiosity to Apparate to Sicily, Lily’s department at the
Ministry was shut down with a sudden decision, and Lily received news that she was
promoted and would be working with Arthur. There was also a bad side to this good news:
Lily had to work until New Year's after Christmas.
She wrote the news to Sirius with slight bitterness and sent it with an owl. She was very
curious about his new life, but there would be many more opportunities for them to travel.
On New Year’s Eve, when Lily had to work overtime due to toilets catching fire, she returned
home an hour later than usual. A surprise awaited her in the living room.
Severus was lying on the long sofa, reading a book aloud. Harry was lying next to him, his
tiny head on Severus’s chest. While holding the book with one hand, Severus was holding
Harry steady with the other.
When Lily arrived, he turned his head and put the book on the coffee table. “We missed you,”
said Severus. Harry stood up and lunged forward to hug his mother.
“I missed you too,” said Lily, hugging Harry. The image from a moment ago was still in her
mind. Severus had stood up too. “Harry only ate potatoes. I waited for you, if you are
hungry.”
“I’m very hungry Sev, I could even eat you.” They laughed and immediately set the table.
“Toilets catching fire, eh?” said Severus with a dissatisfied expression on his face. “We
suspect a gang entertaining themselves with bad jokes rather than an obvious attack,” said
Lily.
“Anything could be behind it. Be careful,” said Severus.
He had started voicing this more lately because history seemed to be repeating itself. Lily
understood too. When it was 12 o'clock and they entered the new year and gave each other
gifts, Lily realized one more thing. Apart from her love, she experienced another awakening.
She had no parents. She didn't have many close friends. James, whatever problems existed
between them, was Harry’s father but was no longer alive.
Her ties with Petunia were broken too.
In this world, Severus Snape was the only person worth believing in for the future after
Harry. And he was only an arm’s length away from her.
She put on the new jeans Severus bought for her. They suited her very well. She had bought a
toaster for Severus. As soon as Severus opened the package, he burst into laughter.
Maybe decisions about the future were this simple. Like a laugh. The only thing needed was
a small momentum in the right direction.
For Lily’s birthday, Arthur and Molly had told both of them, “We won't accept even if you
make other plans.” Someone who would go against this hadn't been born yet.
-----
On January 31st, the Weasleys’ garden was unusually crowded. The table was long, plates
were mixed. Sirius had turned a chair around and sat on it, smiling while Remus was telling
something. Everyone was relaxed.
Children were running around, Percy and the twins were playing Quidditch; Ron and Harry,
waiting for their turn to fly, were chatting with each other. Ginny wasn't leaving their trail.
Although a voice inside told Lily this was unnecessary, Lily hadn't done it without warning
her son.
1-No mentioning the rat, he passed away as a result of an accident.
2-Ginny is the youngest, no pushing her during the game.
When Lily stood up, no one noticed at first. She took the box out of her dress pocket. At that
moment she made eye contact with Remus. Then with Molly. They had probably understood.
She opened the box. The ring was simple but clear. Not showy. Like Lily.
“I wanted to thank you for inviting me here today,” she said. Paused for a moment. “But now
—”
She turned to Severus. She would give a hundred Galleons to know what the man she loved
was thinking at that moment. “I wanted to ask if you would marry me,” said Lily, almost in a
whisper.
There were dozens of sentences she wanted to say. How important Severus was to her. How
proud she was of him. That Severus made her feel loved and accepted in a way she didn't
even know was possible before.
But they all slipped from her mind. She had experienced many “firsts” with Severus and
managed to overcome most of them. This was completely different. Both brand new and
terrifying. Lily had married once before. And the past hadn't been very generous to her on
this matter.
Severus looked at the ring. Then at Lily. As if this scene wasn't in any of the probabilities he
had calculated. If Lily hadn't been so tense, she could have smiled at her son’s indifference in
the corner.
Severus took a deep breath. Took his lower lip between his teeth.
“No.”
The sounds in the garden suddenly lost their meaning. Lily’s face went white as a sheet. She
didn't pull the ring back, but her hand trembled.
“Who told you I would say yes?” added Severus with calmness. This wasn't mockery. Nor
was it panic. It was an honesty postponed for too long.
“I wouldn't marry you,” Severus continued in a low voice, “because you offer me a life. Or
because you make my life meaningful. These are fragile enough reasons.”
“I wouldn't marry you,” Severus continued, “because we get along well. Or because I love
you very much. Not because you and Harry are at the center of my life. Not because you
make my life meaningful.”
Lily’s eyes filled with tears.
“I would marry you,” said Severus, “despite the moments you are selfish. Despite the times
you are difficult. Despite your habit of telling people what to do. Despite you snoring in your
sleep. Despite you pulling the duvet off me. Despite you speaking hurtfully occasionally.”
He took a step towards Lily. “And I would marry you only because you don't try to change
this.” He took the ring from Lily’s palm. “Therefore,” he said, and for the first time his voice
trembled, “the question was in the wrong place.”
He knelt down. “Will you marry me?” Lily was crying now.
“Yes,” she said. “Of course yes.”
When Severus finally broke the kiss, there was a huge smile on Lily's face. She giggled
through her tears as she extended her hand to Severus for the ring.
"I'm sorry I couldn't get you a better ring," Lily apologized, murmuring. That weekend, while
buying a new coat for Harry, the question of which ring to choose was also on her mind. "I
found these and... Well, they weren't very expensive, so I thought I could determine your size
first and then buy you something."
"They are perfect, Lily," he promised.
-----
Marriage, of course, wasn't just about planning a wedding, choosing which wedding dress,
and other preparations.
Lily knew she should have done what she was about to do now when James died, of course,
but it hadn't crossed her mind while mourning and crying.
She wondered what was most painful, her marriage that was about to end, or Lily suddenly
being left alone? In front of the vault belonging to James and his family inside Gringotts
bank, holding Harry’s hand, she stood at the door looking inside.
She tilted her head and looked at Harry; at the same time, the child lifted his head and looked
at his mother.
“Where are we, mum?”
“This is the vault belonging to your father, Harry. It was all left to me and you, ironic,
because here- nothing here belongs to me.”
“Meaning Severus’s?” said Harry, bewildered. Lily paused.
“No. I am talking about James, Harry.” Lily bent down on her knees; she used to be able to
talk to Harry at this height, but now Harry was taller.
“James was your father but he passed away. You might not remember him.”
“I don't remember at all,” said Harry. Little children’s honesty could sometimes be very
harsh.
“Don't be sad that you don't remember, though,” said Lily. Harry was a child with a slightly
more special situation; he could see two men as fathers at the same time.
“Is all this a lot of money?” asked Harry.
“It is really a lot of money,” confirmed Lily.
“Can we buy a plane?” asked Harry curiously. “Please, mum.”
“No, sweetheart,” said Lily, adding seriousness to her voice. “As I said, a whole fleet will be
yours when you grow up. But for this, you will need to work hard when you grow up too.”
Harry’s eyes opened wide. “It’s a long time until I grow up. Too many years.”
They went upstairs, a parchment came to Lily at the counter and Lily signed it with a quill.
Then she held Harry’s hand and they left the bank.
“What did you sign?” Harry asked his mother curiously.
“The vault we entered downstairs is now only yours, Harry. This means I can only come here
until you are 17. Then you will be responsible for your spending.”
“Me? Just me, meaning?”
“Exactly,” said Lily, smiling.
“So then I can buy a plane,” Lily rolled her eyes.
-----
Severus liked planning and being organized; he also possessed keen observation skills and
innate analytical intelligence. If he weren't a wizard, he would definitely be top of the class in
a Muggle high school. And in sciences too.
Lily was proud of Severus not because he was her fiancé, but because such a person was in
her life. But she was an impatient person; the draft they were dealing with now required
patience.
They had already been combining their salaries and spending together for years, but with
marriage, they needed to take this more seriously. The vault James left was absolutely out of
the question; Severus hadn't said a single word about it, nor was there a need.
Lily looked at the monthly expenses item again. I mean, they weren't struggling to make ends
meet but...
“We have no choice but to enter the stock market,” said Severus.
“No Sev,” said Lily in a whisper. She could make other calculations, if they tightened their
belts until summer...
“My love, there is no risk. You save small amounts every month; as it continues to
accumulate, the total amount becomes meaningful. A slow but effective system.”
Lily took her head between her hands. “I don't know Sev… The Muggle central bank has
been conducting financial activities within the framework of comprehensive rules for years.
Is there a guarantee we won't go bankrupt?”
Severus smiled slightly. “Do wizards ever go bankrupt? An unheard-of thing.”
Lily: “Let's not be the first.”
-----
On February 27th, Lily was examining an envelope in her hand, biting her lips. When Arthur
noticed her stressed state, he lifted his head from the report he was writing and asked Lily
what was wrong.
“No, this isn't an envelope sent to me. This is an envelope I am thinking of sending. My
wedding invitation is inside.”
“That is very nice though, isn't it? Who are you hesitant to invite?”
“My sister,” said Lily.
Still, it wasn't a matter to think about too much and get stressed over. Lily had other work to
finish. She attached her invitation and the short letter she wrote to Petunia to a grey owl and
sent it to Petunia’s address in Surrey.
'I wonder what reaction Petunia would give when she encountered an owl at her window this
evening after years?'
That evening, the same grey owl indeed tapped its beak on the living room window, making
Vernon and Petunia jump in fear. While Vernon grumbled, Petunia took the two folded
envelopes with trembling hands. She recognized Lily’s handwriting immediately.
“Lily wrote,” she said in an angry voice indistinguishable from a whisper.
“Where was she for four whole years,” said Vernon, grumbling again.
“You are talking nonsense too, Vernon,” said Petunia. “Don't act like you care about Lily all
of a sudden now.”
“I do care,” said Vernon. “Just let them not involve us in their magical business with that
bespectacled foolish man!”
Meanwhile, Petunia was reading the letter. “You don't need to worry about that man
anymore.” She sighed. “He died. Home accident.”
“Oh shi-”
“Vernon! Don't insult a dead man behind his back!”
Finishing the letter, Petunia handed it to her husband to read, then picked up the other
envelope. “Snape. Now she is marrying that poor, dirty boy. My mindless, unfortunate sister,
woe! Such stupidity for such beauty...” She hiccuped.
“She is throwing her future in the trash. Ah Lily, stupid girl. Why didn't she write to me
sooner?” She had started crying, tears streamed from her eyes, her voice went hoarse.
Vernon was patting his wife’s back, not knowing what to do.
“We must go and talk to her and dissuade her then,” said Vernon. “It says here where she
stays, let's go and talk at the weekend. We can give her the... entrustment in the garage too,”
he added.

On Saturday, Lily woke up before her fiancé. She had already been opening her eyes before
Severus in the mornings for a while. She snuggled closer to Severus under the duvet, touched
her nose to his neck and smelled.
Then something else came to her mind; she licked the thin skin with her tongue first, then
pressed her lips and sucked. The effect was immediate, two arms wrapped around her tightly
like an octopus.
“Good morning,” said Lily.
“Good morning my love,” said Severus. The clock on Lily’s arm said 6:48.“We have time,”
said Lily, tilting her face just before kissing Severus. Severus didn't ask time for what, the
answer was obvious.
Then, before Harry woke up, they showered quickly, one after the other. Lily was the first to
dry and dress, choosing a normal white jumper, and Severus wore Muggle clothes too. While
Severus prepared breakfast in the kitchen, Harry was objecting to getting up.
While Severus was making an omelette with a pan and spatula, Lily came out of the
room.“Let me prepare the omelette Sev, and you try to convince Harry to get out of bed.”
Severus winked at his fiancé, then in a slightly loud voice: “Should I convince Harry? What
do you mean, doesn't he want to play with Ron and the others today?”Hearing Ron’s name,

Severus turned his head very fast, almost breaking his neck. Lily turned beetroot red. Two

“The day we went to the bank, you said something to me, Harry. Can you say it to Severus
too?” said Lily.
“Ah, I don't remember,” said Harry, grinning. Lily winked at Severus. Severus was still
astonished.
This was the last weekend they would spend as single people, so they had planned a normal
shopping day without doing very tiring activities. Instead of walking with them and getting
tired, Harry would spend time at The Burrow.
As they were about to leave the house, Severus turned his head and looked towards the door.
“I hear a vehicle sound.” Lily pricked up her ears too. Someone had parked in front of their
house on the street. It couldn't be Death Eaters, but...
They opened the door and two cars were parked on the street. Petunia got out of the first
vehicle, Vernon Dursley out of the other. The vehicle Petunia drove was quite familiar. Lily
looked at the newcomers, squinting her eyes. Severus stood back.
“Did someone come?” said Harry’s voice from inside. “Did Ron come?”
“No,” said Severus, looking at Harry. He felt like this was a moment belonging a bit to Lily.
“Your aunt came.”
“Ah. But my aunt didn't like my mum. Why did she come to us?” Severus couldn't answer
this question.
Lily, a bit surprised, a bit reluctantly, said “Welcome,” to her sister and her husband. “We

beating around the bush. Then she scanned the house from top to bottom.
“But I must admit,” she added, her voice hard but restless, “you are in a better situation than I
thought. I can't let you ruin your future living in a shack with a loser man with no social

“And we are pleased to see you Lily,” said Vernon.
“Petunia hasn't slept at all since we received your letter. Think logically and cancel this

Vernon entered first, Petunia behind, and Lily closed the door behind her sister. Inside,

“Severus? Is this really you?” said Petunia, bewildered.
“Yes, it's me. I was 14 when we last met,” he said in a slightly cold tone.  “And this is Harry.”
But Harry hid behind his legs.
“Sooo what do you do for work, son?” This was the only question that interested Vernon.
Petunia threw a piercing look at her husband but didn't make a sound.
“I am a legal representative at the Ministry of Magic, the term you are familiar with must be
lawyer,” said Severus, with the same indifference.
Petunia and Vernon were looking at each other. As if their opinions changed and this
marriage was actually logical. But then Petunia turned to Lily.
“Can we talk a bit privately, my dear sister?”
Lily’s first reaction would be to refuse. Their last meeting was a disaster. But the words
spilling from her mouth were “okay” and they went to the kitchen. Vernon stood in the room

“The car,” he said. “Lily’s. From her father. Use it or sell it, doesn't matter, we just kept it in
the garage. Do you have a licence then, Snape?”
“I do.”
“Aha, interesting. What class is it?”
“In B and D category,” replied Severus. Vernon blushed.

Petunia and Lily went to the kitchen. “I invited you to my wedding in the letter Petunia, but I
suppose you saw fit to drop by beforehand.”
Petunia gave her a look. “Lily, are we enemies? Why didn't you mention all the shit going on
in your life and returning from deaths to me before?”
“I don't understand how you can ask me this. Moreover, you never had a supportive, sincere
side as you claim. Maybe we aren't enemies but we are worse, Petunia.”
“Because I thought I would protect you, stupid girl! You didn't want to listen to me for once,

ready but you chose it to be this way. And I-”

“No Lily. I wanted to take you with me too. Away from all this, very far away.”
These were words never confessed to each other before. Lily was speaking this sincerely with

barman there about your owls and wrote you a letter,” said Petunia.
Lily remembered that letter. Petunia had apologized for her behaviour for the first time and
offered to spend Christmas together with Lily. But Lily had preferred to spend Christmas
1978 with Severus.
“Then I wrote to you again. Months later, of course. You came with a child we didn't know,

“Let me explain before you say it, yes, that marriage was really a mistake,” said Lily.
“However, James was good for me at that time-”
“It could be seen from a kilometre away that he didn't love you, Lily!”
“Did you ask to talk alone to throw my past mistakes in my face, Petunia?”
“God knows I was very relieved when your letter reached me on Wednesday evening and I
read that James died!” shouted Petunia unable to hold herself back.
“While eating that nightmare dinner, words behind his every sentence stabbing both you and

“I was thinking of filing for divorce. Because I was tired,” continued Lily. “Of constantly

“What do you mean ‘criticize’?” Petunia flinched after Lily. Severus, Vernon, and Harry in

Severus’s face was sullen. “Ah. I didn't hear you come,” said Lily. Severus had 99 reasons to
hate James. This made it 100.
“Harry got thirsty,” said Severus. He was still far from calming down.
While Lily poured water into a glass and gave it to Harry, Petunia was cursing James in a low
voice. “Here, sweetheart. Drink slowly though.”
Harry wet himself anyway. Severus tried holding the glass with one hand to balance it but
Harry mumbled “I’m handling it dad” to him. Then he handed the half-full glass back to Lily.
“I’m full,” he said.
Lily looked at Severus, he had calmed down more. Vernon and Petunia jumped into their own
vehicles and left the house. Before leaving, Petunia hugged Lily. They hadn't hugged for
years.
“We will come to the wedding too,” said Petunia. She didn't say another word but everything
she wanted to say was in her eyes. Lily felt the same.
Both adults had lost their desire to shop now, but Harry wanted to go to The Burrow. Severus

sofa looking at the kitchen with blank eyes. She smiled at him when she saw Severus.
“I didn't really intend to mention these to you,” she said. “But since you learned, you need the
whole story.”
“Ah my love,” she hugged her fiancé tightly. “I would have wanted to kill that man, if he
wasn't dead.”He kissed her red hair, it smelled of shampoo.
“James was never ‘that unforgettable love’ of mine,” continued Lily.
“He was obsessing over you and our past too much. He couldn't manage this. He was
constantly thinking, constantly interpreting, picking at everything.” She paused for a
moment. “Sirius was aware too, do you know? He could put his friendship with James aside
and criticize him.”
“Is that so? I wouldn't expect that from Black.” Lily giggled. Sirius only lost his logic when
the subject was love. “He believed James would understand his mistake and choose to grow
as a person. Maybe he would have succeeded… I don't know.”
She would never know. “But this isn't important right now. How much did Harry hear?”
Severus sighed. “All of it. But he isn't old enough to understand.”
“But he will grow up,” said Lily. Both were worried.
-----
Lily told Severus, “You shouldn't see me the evening before the wedding,” and went to The
Burrow with a mischievous and playful attitude. She left Harry with Severus. Petunia had
invited her that night too, but Lily had made a different choice; The Burrow felt more natural.
The next morning, the day of the wedding, when Molly handed her a vintage hat with
delicate embroidery, Lily felt for a moment as if she were truly in her family's home.
Molly and Arthur were like two older siblings, unrelated by blood but extremely real.
“It suits you incredibly well,” said Molly. Just then, Remus entered The Burrow through the
fireplace. The twins squealed, Percy telling them not to run inside the house. Everything was
chaotic as it should be.
Lily had chosen a beige silk jacket and trousers over a plain white blouse. Her shoes and hat
were in close shades of blue. Her hair was straight, shiny; she hadn't tied it up.
“You look truly dazzling,” said Remus. Lily blushed.
“I wonder what Severus is wearing?” she said, laughing.
“He surely looks like a gothic vampire,” said Remus, with a very confident voice.
They moved towards the fireplace. Arthur cast a small spell on Lily so she wouldn't get dusty.
Molly fixed her hair one last time. But during the Floo powder journey, Lily’s hair got messy
again; she looked even more beautiful.
They came out of the municipality's fireplace. A middle-aged, somewhat timid-looking man
greeted them. His official job description certainly didn't say “managing relations with
wizards in the vicinity”, but that was the requirement of the day.
“Most municipalities,” he began, giggling, “now have their fireplaces built in car parks. But
not many wizards live in this area, so it stayed like this here.” He was right.
When they moved to the corridor, Severus and Sirius had already arrived too. They weren't
standing next to each other. Harry was holding Sirius’s hand, excitedly telling him something.
Sirius was wearing a plain suit like Remus. Severus, on the other hand, really looked like a
gothic vampire.
Black jacket, black trousers, black shirt. He wore boots instead of shoes. His hair was
combed and... Lily couldn't figure out exactly what was different at first glance. Their eyes
met. Lily was sure Severus held his breath.
Harry ran to his mother's side and hugged her.  “Do you know,” he said, laughing, “dad cut
his hair yesterday. With scissors.”
Ah. That was the reason for the difference.
“Yes, Harry,” said Sirius with a serious expression, “it won't make a difference unless he goes
bald. Same greasy hair.”  Severus didn't care. He looked at his family, smiling.
They had been preparing for that day for weeks. This was the final step to becoming a family
in the real sense. And... the whole fuss was over in three minutes. Now they were a huge
three people.
As the same middle-aged man sent all five of them through the fireplace, he made very nice
wishes, congratulating the new couple.
-----
When they arrived at The Burrow, there was a new verbal squabble.
“Ron please put your waistcoat on over that. Percy, dress him, sweetheart.”
“Mum I can't do it, he's running away from me.”
“Mum I want chocolate,” said Ginny.
“Ask your father- ah you handled it quickly.”
They came out of the fireplace smiling. Remus immediately caught Ron wandering around in
a T-shirt, picked him up.
“We’ll dress him, Molly,” he said. For the next half hour, Lily and Severus sent loving
glances, slightly mischievous winks at each other, but couldn't seem to be alone. Just as they
caught a momentary gap in front of the outer door, Lily noticed a familiar vehicle coming
from the dirt road.
“Petunia is here.” They said ‘welcome’ to Petunia, Vernon, and little Dudley. Petunia gave
Lily a short hug, maybe she wanted to say other things too, but only “You look very
beautiful” came out of her mouth.
Dudley and Harry were the same age, Lily introduced the two cousins and Dudley was
included in the children’s game in a short time.
Percy took charge with the responsibility of being the eldest among the children and put the
broomsticks away. It was clear the Weasleys had warned their children beforehand because
Dudley was a Muggle. Still, Petunia was occasionally watching her son from the window.
Vernon sat on the single armchair; although he tried to look relaxed, his tension was evident.
When Arthur offered him a glass of wine, Vernon drank almost half of it in a single gulp.
“Molly has strict orders on this matter, Lily,” whispered Arthur. “I am forbidden from asking
your brother-in-law Muggle questions.”
“For shame, Arthur,” said Lily, unable to hold her laughter. “You can ask me.”
Arthur turned immediately. “What is a carburetor?” While Lily tried to explain as best she
could, Remus and Sirius had started a conversation with Petunia.
“I'm just curious about the shops that are in fashion,” said Sirius. “You know, sometimes you
buy a gift for someone. If you know what is wanted, your gift makes them happier.”
Remus looked with narrowed eyes. “It doesn't work like that, Sirius. People generally accept
a gift for its sentimental value.”
“Not at all,” said Petunia with a clear expression. “New glassware, porcelain... I'm tired of all
of it. My kitchen cupboard is overflowing. I take the gift, say thank you with a smile, but it
goes in the bin the next day.”
“Are you serious?” said Sirius. “What about other useless things that aren't kitchenware?”
“I generally get rid of them too,” continued Petunia. “A vase incompatible with the furniture,
for example. Or the trend I personally hate the most: copper ornamentation. Absolutely not.”
Sirius suddenly took on an extremely thoughtful manner. “You are very right about that,” he
said slowly. “So... what is really fashionable right now?”
The conversation was going well. No one realized how time passed. “So you really don't
have a honeymoon plan now?” said Molly in astonishment. “Are you going home? We would
look after Harry for a few days, it's no trouble for us at all,” she added.
This was Lily’s last worry.
“We already have plans for the summer. Besides, Severus is busy with work right now,” she
said. “Thanks again for the hat.”
“Don't mention it, you're welcome,” said Molly. She and Lily hugged. The Dursleys had left
with their car about half an hour ago too. All the children were dozing off, Harry had fallen
asleep on Sirius’s chest too. “I suppose I was holding him like this last when he was a baby,”
said Sirius. Harry was heavier now of course, but Sirius was pleased with his situation.
Severus looked at them and smiled. Perhaps he was stopping finding Black annoying
anymore.
Later, Sirius didn't want to leave him saying ‘he never got to spend time with Harry’, and
when Molly put a stop to it saying ‘You can't go through the fireplace with a sleeping child’,
they took Sirius, Harry, and Ron into the same room.
Severus and Lily returned home without Harry.
Upon entering the house, Lily showered as the first order of business, “it's impossible for me
to sleep while this hair potion Molly gave me is in my hair.” Severus was brushing his teeth
too, behind him Lily was struggling with her blouse. Finally, the zipper got caught in her hair
and Severus dropped everything and helped his wife.
When Lily took everything off and got into the bathtub, Severus was leaving the door- “Are
you going to get into bed with the filth of the whole day?” said Lily, parting the curtain.
Severus was caught off guard. “Fine. I’ll wash after you too.”
“Don't be ridiculous Sev, come in.” Severus didn't object, closed the door with his foot while
quickly taking off his shirt.
-----
July came very quickly. Following a busy spring work tempo, Lily and Severus going on
holiday the same week was approved by the Ministry of Magic. Lily wrote this to Sirius
immediately.
Sirius wasn't someone who sent letters very frequently but he had started writing more rarely.
In his own words, he had ‘dived into the hustle of life.’ Lily could never imagine him entering
a 9-5 job but something was going on. Sirius Black could be jokey and unserious but when he
wanted he could be very secretive and Lily was about to climb the walls now.
Severus, on the other hand, didn't think like Lily, he was sure Sirius was eating pasta three
meals a day and hanging out with girls.
In the letter she sent to Sirius last month, Lily had asked where Italian wizarding families
sent their children. According to the sources she researched, there was a Ministry of Magic
but no apparent wizarding school.
Sirius’s reply was gloomier than she expected. He wrote that due to the Vatican’s centuries
long strict policies, many pure-blood wizarding families had left Italy permanently about four
hundred years ago. Those remaining had either lived in very closed communities or chosen
to be invisible.
The number of Muggle-born wizards was extremely low. The Ministry of Magic dealt with
them directly, most were directed to other countries at an early age.
After this letter, Lily understood why she saw Sirius increasingly more often in Muggle
clothes. Both Severus and Lily wore jeans in daily life of course; but when going to work,
robes and cloaks were still normal, even expected things. Sirius wasn't dressing like a
Muggle. He was choosing to appear as a Muggle.
Lily was now sure he didn't live in a Hogsmeade-like town where he could carry his wand
freely and wander around wearing robes without attracting attention.
It was more appropriate for them to Apparate to Italy at three in the night. Although Severus
and Harry were displeased with the hour they woke up, they dressed quickly: T-shirts and
shorts. Harry was slightly cold but refused Severus’s help -he didn't want to be carried in
arms anymore.
“We dressed lightly so as not to sweat where we arrive,” said Severus. “This discomfort will
last very shortly.” Lily went out with her thin dress too, they locked the door. Lily took out
her wand and they left Spinner's End behind.
Sirius’s directions were accurate, they appeared at the bottom of a dumpster. There was a
narrow street and continuous buildings. “We are looking for number 94,” said Lily. “You
aren't cold anymore are you Harry?” she said in a low voice.
“No, it's hot now,” said Harry. “90, 91...” They heard a whistle sound in the dark and their
first reaction was to look back. Then when they heard it again they lifted their heads.
A curly-haired woman waved at them from the window on the second floor of the building
two doors down.
“Through the brown door.”
Severus and Lily looked at each other. Who was she? Harry headed to the door then Sirius
came out of the door. “Yaay you came,” he said with a joyful voice. He hugged Harry tightly,
then Lily.
“Girl, don't walk around with this dress without your husband by your side, they’ll kidnap
you.” Lily blushed terribly but Severus seemed to approve on the contrary.
While going up the stairs, due to the late hour, they couldn't ask Sirius anything to avoid
making noise but their questions found answers shortly. They entered the flat through an
antique wooden door. Before she even introduced herself, a bronzed young woman with curly
black hair at shoulder length kissed Lily.
“This is Martina,” said Sirius. “Medical student.”
Meaning Muggle. Both Lily and Severus noted. Martina kissed Severus this time. “Ah, you
are very cold,” she said with a slight accent. Her English was fluent.
Seeing Harry, she turned to him. “What is your name, sweetie?” Harry hid behind Severus.
“That’s Harry. He is shy,” said Sirius.
They went inside.
Martina was full of energy, directed the guests to the living room. While Sirius headed to get
something from the kitchen, she slapped his bum. Lily and Severus were getting more
surprised every second. Moreover, Martina called Sirius ‘amore’. Severus bit his cheeks not
to laugh.
When Harry fell asleep immediately, Lily laid him on the sofa. Martina showed the second
bedroom to Severus too.
“There are another friend’s belongings in this room, that friend is on holiday. But Sirius
changed the sheets. You can sleep comfortably, it’s clean.”
“Is this place a student dormitory?” asked Severus. It was surprisingly warm and organized
for a dormitory.
“We are tenants,” said Martina. “We share with other students. Sirius isn't a student but no
one needs to know.”
“Meaning…” said Severus, as if walking on a minefield.
“A difficult childhood, a difficult family,” said Martina immediately. “Yes, he told me
everything. Ran away from high school and such…”
Ah. So not everything.
Martina giggled suddenly. “You should have seen the look on your face. I was joking.” Then
she winked. “Of course I know you are wizards. Actually, Sirius needs to tell you how he told
me this but…” She shrugged. “Tomorrow is better.”
Severus and Lily lay on the borrowed bed and Severus couldn't believe the hot weather
despite the open window. There was no need to cover themselves at all. “Take off your T
shirt if you want,” whispered Lily. Severus applied what his wife said and lay back on the
bed. Lily hugged him immediately, threw her leg over him.
“Is Harry roasting too?” asked Severus. A sound like mmm came from Lily, she had fallen
asleep immediately. Severus fell asleep while watching the ceiling too.
When morning came, Lily and Severus saw the household and Harry had woken up before
them.
While Lily took out new summer clothes for her son, Harry yawned. “I woke up at one point
last night, and it was very hot.” Severus rolled his eyes. This was the state of everything that
could be said having been said.
“I will have Sirius buy a fan for you, cucciolo!” said Martina cheerfully. Harry blushed shyly
again, then went to the room to get dressed.
They went to the cafeteria down the road to have breakfast. The menu was simple, coffee and
croissant on the side. “There are other local delicacies too,” said Sirius pointing. “What
would you like to get?”
Then, with plenty of grammar mistakes and sprinkling English words in between, he
attempted to give a semi-Italian order. The man at the register laughed at him first; Sirius
responded without taking offense at all. Then a few more sentences passed between the two
that Lily and Severus couldn't understand and the laughter extended.
Sirius turned to his friends and smiled. “Tell me, how is life without me?”
Lily hit his arm. “You should have told us about Martina,” she said but her voice didn't sound
angry. “I had such bad scenarios in my head that I was sure you were unhappy here.”
“First of all,” said Sirius, grinning, “I chased after her for a long time. Us being here right
now wasn’t exactly easy last year.” He took a bite of his croissant and winked at Severus.
“This poor sod chased after you a lot too. He’ll understand me.”
Lily had softened a bit more. “So what’s the second thing? Sea, sand, sun?”
“That’s the third.” Sirius became serious for the first time. “Lily, I don't think I’ve ever done
anything for myself until I found myself here. Except for becoming an Animagus and running
away from my family home, of course.” After a short pause, he added: “For the first time, I
am living for myself. I have more normal responsibilities.”
“Black has grown up and is learning to take responsibility,” said Severus, indifferent but with
a faint smile.
“That is so true I won’t even get angry at it,” said Sirius.
“Martina also mentioned something she said would be more appropriate for you to tell us,”
said Lily. “About how you told her about yourself.” Sirius giggled. He was so relaxed that all
of Lily’s doubts evaporated in an instant.
“It was about three months ago. After your wedding,” he began. “I was still telling Martina
about airports, trains, flights and such. Just straight-up lies. She sees a jacket on me, asks
‘did you just buy it?’ I say, ‘It’s a gift.’ After all, I’m on a limited budget, you know.”
Lily and Severus rolled their eyes simultaneously. Sirius burst into laughter.
“Turns out I got an owl back in March while I was attending your wedding. Martina takes the
post in confusion, opens the letter… The Italian Ministry of Magic is reminding me that I
need to renew my residence permit. She thought it was a joke at first, but she didn’t throw the
letter away. Then she basically started espionage.”
The expression on Sirius’s face became serious for the first time. “She realized there were no
flights from London to Catania on the day I arrived. She put everything in front of me. I was
genuinely scared at that moment.”
He continued after a short pause. “I couldn't lie anymore. She had come with proof. ‘I am a
wizard, it is true,’ I said. Doesn't she turn to me at very moment and say, ‘then turn this water
into wine’? I said, ‘Martina, am I Jesus?’”
Severus had just taken a sip of his coffee when he sprayed it onto the table with laughter.
Sirius was laughing both at his own situation and at Lily’s attempt to wipe Severus clean with
a napkin.
“‘No, I want my wine,’ she said. So instead, I took another glass and turned it into a table
lamp. She just froze there. I thought she forgot to breathe. Then it suddenly clicked and she
shouted, ‘are you really magical?!’ Good thing we were alone at home.”
“For the love of Merlin,” said Lily. “Quite chaotic.”
“Don't ask,” said Sirius.
“Then I started explaining slowly. My family, Hogwarts… The hardest part was admitting
that I lied to her constantly. I mean, she had the right to leave me a hundred times over.”
“So what stopped her?” asked Lily.
“His handsome face,” said Severus with a dry expression. Sirius rolled his eyes but smiled at
both of them.
“It has nothing to do with looks,” he said. “Our relationship wasn’t exactly built on solid
foundations either. But what kept us together was that, for the first time in that moment, we
were on the same page. Martina might not have lied to me, but… we are both very alike and
very opposite, Lily. That isn't a bond you can always catch.”
He shrugged, his voice softening. “That’s why we talked and decided to continue. Now we
have one single rule: No matter the reason, never any lies.”
After finishing their coffees, they went back to the house to get ready and spent the rest of the
day at the beach. The sea was calm, and the sun was scorching. Lily applied cream to both
Harry and Severus’s exposed skin and back, and Severus applied cream to her just as
generously. Sirius said he wouldn’t go into the sea, taking only a hat for his head.
While Lily and Severus observed the surroundings under the umbrella on the beach, Sirius
and Harry were playing on the shore. Sirius, who said he wouldn't go in, was wet through and
through, and his clothes were sandy. They were splashing water at each other with Harry.
“Shall we jump in from the pier?” said Lily, pointing ahead. Severus nodded, and they left the
shade of the umbrella.
But Lily’s intention was different. A few people walking ahead of them had run and thrown
themselves into the water from the end of the pier. Once Lily was sure of the depth, she
pushed Severus with all her might; that wasn't enough, she kicked him too. Severus really
lost his balance for a moment. Then, by reflex, he grabbed Lily by the waist and lifted her up.
A breath of laughter, a short scream, and they fell from the pier into the sea together.
Six days passed very quickly. They needed to Apparate back to England, to home, at an
ungodly hour of the night. As they were leaving, Sirius hugged Harry and Lily, and shook
hands with Severus. Martina kissed all three of them.
“Please come at Christmas too,” said Martina. “It doesn't snow, but it's fun here too,” she
said, winking at Harry.
“We’ll come back as soon as possible,” said Lily.
They said their goodbyes at the door, then the family of three walked towards the dumpster
and within a few seconds, vanished from sight in the street.
Martina snuggled slightly towards her boyfriend. “You have such a blessing in your hand,”
she said, tilting her head to the side, “and we are still looking at connecting flights. Madonna
mia, Barcelona. Ten seconds from right there.”
-----
Harry’s fourth birthday wasn’t much fun because he was ill that day. Still, Lily and Molly had
planned to throw a small party for both Ginny and Harry on the same day, thinking it would
be quite cute.
On a sunny Sunday in the second week of August, the two families met. Instead of The
Burrow, they had come to the lake Severus and Lily loved. The children went into the water
as soon as they took their shoes off; apparently, they had no intention of coming out for
hours. The adults relaxed by the lake shore.
When it came time to blow out candles on the cake, Harry laughed when he saw three
candles.
“I was already three,” he said to his mother in a cheerful voice. “It should be four.”
They probably hadn't thought of this detail amidst thinking of everything. “Is it okay if you
blow out three candles this year?” said Lily. “Ginny will be three.”
“Okay no problem,” said Harry. Ginny was giggling while watching them too.
Two children blew at the same time and laughed. It was a very sweet and unforgettable
moment.
-----
In September, Harry got sick again and this time Severus panicked. They went to a Muggle
ER at four in the morning. An IV was attached to his small, thin arm; they were told it was a
stomach bug.
After the IV treatment, they returned home and Severus went to the Ministry after sleeping
only an hour.
“Let me know if he gets worse again,” he said to Lily by the fireplace. His voice was tired
and joyless but sincere while hugging his wife. Lily confirmed with a nod and saw Severus
off.
Harry didn't get worse again; he recovered within a few days with supportive potions.
In the first week of October, Lily successfully sold her father’s car: a young Muggle man
bought it. He didn't care that it had been in an accident before either, his aim was to modify
the vehicle and use it like that.
Lily still looked at her father’s old, grey Vauxhall Viva for a while. Of course, she had nice
memories with the car. But Severus was insistent that she and Harry needed to travel in a
safer, better-equipped vehicle.
Lily returned home without a car, by Apparating. Perhaps the emotional weight created by
the vehicle she gave up had left her weak. The house was quiet. Harry had gone to work with
Severus today. Actually, this was for his registration in the Young Quidditch Talents
programme run by the Ministry. Lily wondered how he would describe his day to her when
he returned. He could speak fluently without pausing now.
She had taken her clothes off, was about to put on her pyjamas. She needed to sleep a bit. But
suddenly her stomach cramped, a bitter taste rose up to her throat. When she swallowed, it
didn't go back. There was only one thing to do now. She ran to the toilet and emptied
whatever was in her stomach.
Severus found his wife asleep on the sofa in the living room, wrapped around a hot water
bottle. After gently silencing Harry, who was explaining something loudly, he sent him to his
room.
He woke Lily by stroking her hair. The water bottle counted as lukewarm.
“How was today,” she said in a sleepy voice. Severus, however, was looking worried.
“It was fun for us but... let's not deal with that bug illness again if you want. Let them give
you an IV too.” But Lily refused and preferred Severus’s supportive potions. In reality, she
felt fine the next day too.
Severus didn't want to leave it to chance. “These are less curative but preventative potions-”
he said showing her his stock. Lily already knew, so she cut Severus off with a kiss.
Severus melted immediately, didn't let go when the kiss ended either. “I trust my own skill
but I trust Muggle medicine too. If you are sure you are really fine-”
“I am sure,” said Lily.
Severus still threw a tiny pocket vial from the shelf into the pocket of her robe. “Just in case.”
After dropping Harry off at The Burrow, Lily and Severus reached the Ministry via fireplace
again and went to their own separate floors. Arthur wasn't working in the office that day
either.
During the lunch break, Severus and a female colleague from the Department of Magical
Law Enforcement came to Lily’s office. While they were gossiping together, Alice
Longbottom came too. The small office had suddenly become lively.
“Neville gets sick all the time, we don't even worry anymore,” said Alice sympathetically.
“They were born on the same day, right Lily?” Lily nodded.
“But Severus is very attentive,” she said, winking at Severus. “He made me drink two vials
of potion and everything passed.”
“I still say let's go to the doctor,” said Severus indifferently. “A bug was found in Harry.
What if you have it too?”
“I think this happening is a higher probability,” said Severus’s colleague. Alice looked
thoughtful. She said the nausea might be due to an emotional moment.
The subject quickly shifted to pregnancy, from there to intestinal worms, and finally to
anemia. Severus’s colleague for some reason didn't have an opinion of her own, apparently
finding whoever spoke to be right. Shortly after, everyone left the room except Severus.
“Shall we go to the cinema tonight?” said Lily suddenly.
“Let's go tomorrow,” said Severus. “I think observe yourself at home tonight too.”
They hadn't made such an ordinary plan for a long time. That was exactly what Severus loved
silently anyway. They kissed and parted and Severus went to his own office.
Lily scratched her head while struggling with the Muggle notary documents of the newly
established flying broomstick company Nimbus. Such a thing wasn't in the regulations; she
couldn't understand why they had gone to so much trouble. She wrote a short flying memo
about the subject and sent it to the Department of International Magical Trading Standards.
Just at that moment, another memo came from the Department of Magical Law Enforcement.
A report she submitted last month was needed for a case being opened. Lily opened her
agenda. It wasn't in the September pages. She went to August. She must have missed it.
She scanned August and September again. At that moment, a reply came from Trading
Standards. Lily caught the flying memo in a single move without closing the agenda and left
it on the table.
Looking carefully again, she found it. She had sent it in August.
She brought the folder containing the report to the table with a Summoning Charm. Took the
file out of the folder, packaged it, prepared a new flying memo to forward it. She needed to
mark the date in the agenda too; she looked again.
At that moment, she stopped as if something had hit her head. Her agenda was private; she
marked most records with small symbols only she could understand. She turned the
September pages in panic, looked for the same mark.
But she already knew the answer.
Oh no. Oh no. Lily kept the vial in her pocket. Because there was only one thing these
potions were harmful to.
And Lily hadn't bled at all last month.
Lily decided it was best to handle her work first instead of panicking and sent the quick
memo. Then she wrote another quick memo to Severus. ‘A bit urgent. Let’s meet at the exit.’
Then she hung a sign on the door saying she wouldn't be in the office for the rest of the day,
took her cloak and left.
As she thought, Severus was waiting for her, he had arrived at the exit before Lily. “What
happened?” he asked, looking as if he were searching for a finding on her face. Lily gave him
a ‘not here’ look and they entered the nearest fireplace together.
They arrived home. Lily threw her cloak and bag onto the armchair, held her head with her
hands, exhaled through her mouth, but couldn't calm down. Severus was watching her. Lily
looked at him and decided to be direct.
“I didn't bleed at all last month.”
There were moments when even the sharp-witted Potions Master, analytical Severus, was
stumped because he was a man. “What? Why? Did you get injured?”
“Are you serious?” Lily spoke in a shrill voice unintentionally.
“Lily, I… first a note came from you-”
“We are going to have a child, Severus. A baby. I just realized, while looking at the agenda.
And what if the potion you gave me harmed the fetus? What can we do right now, I don't
know, what if I did something to it?”
When Lily started hiccuping, Severus’s shock passed, replaced by professional seriousness
and deep affection. He quickly closed the distance between them. When he held Lily’s hands,
Lily had closed her eyes tightly. “I hope there is no risk Sev, because I am scared.”
Lily let herself go completely to him. Severus wrapped his arms around her tightly, as if
wanting to hide her from all the evils in the world. He buried his face in Lily’s neck, kissed
her hair. His face was wet; Lily was crying, maybe Severus too.
“First of all,” said Severus in a hoarse voice, “This medicine is completely pure, not a single
harmful toxic substance in it. I prepared it, remember? I would never do anything to harm
you or… us.”
This meant at least the potions were safe. Lily visibly relaxed. She hugged Severus tightly
again. And then, the weight and happiness of the truth finally permeated Severus too. “A
baby…”
They stayed like that for a while, in silence. Then, with that unusual calmness settling into
the house, both of their eyes involuntarily started looking for Harry in the house. Whereas
Harry was spending time with his friends at The Burrow. It was a bit strange that they missed
the noisiest member of the house so quickly; as a matter of fact, the source of noise in this
house wouldn't be limited to a single person in just a few months.
When Lily wanted to get ready and go to The Burrow to pick up Harry, Severus had already
fallen into that familiar dilemma. If Lily said she felt well, she was really well, wasn't she? Or
was Severus being overprotective again? So they went together.
They didn't say anything to Molly yet because both knew she would get overly excited. Harry
was riding a broom with Ron in the garden. He got excited when he saw his parents.
“You came early!”
“Because we missed you very much,” said Severus laughing. Harry was laughing at him too.
All the worries in Lily’s mind disappeared at that moment. She wouldn't trade this state of
Severus for the world.
-----
Weeks passed without hurry. Apparently, all their friends were waiting for the baby news as if
they had reached a silent agreement, everyone was happy and excited.
Sirius had taken the matter seriously so his favourite godson wouldn't feel sidelined in this
new period. While promising to take Harry to Italy frequently, he adorned this not with big
words but with details: long walks by the seaside, small harbours where he would teach him
engine sounds, ice cream shops no one knew… Harry had smiled when he heard these words,
he hadn't said anything but it was clear from how tightly he held the sleeve of Sirius’s jacket
that he put his words in his heart.
Molly, having a large family, had said she would help if they were stuck on any subject. Even
Petunia had wished well wishes and her note had a telephone number (Lily and Severus were
undecided about joining this new fashion trend).
The strangest but most magnificent gesture came from Remus, even Severus was left open
mouthed. Against the health problems Lily experienced while expecting Harry, he had sent
five kilos of pickles to Lily, cucumber pickles with plenty of garlic and dill sealed with an
Unbreakable Charm. Lily was almost going to cry but while she was pulling herself together,
Severus had taken a small plate to taste. He popped one in his mouth, and gave one to Lily
with his hand too.
While Severus chewed the pickle, a slight sour expression mixed with disgust appeared on
his face, as if he had tasted the worst potion he had ever drunk in his life. The vinegar ratio
was so high it burned his throat. However, while Lily ate that pickle she took from her
husband’s fingers, there was pure peace on her face. She had closed her eyes, smiling as if
eating the world’s most expensive chocolate.
Severus sighed but didn't object. He could endure five kilos of pickles for this pleased
expression of Lily.
While the Christmas holiday was being arranged, Severus shook his head silently from side
to side while watching his colleagues’ race to avoid overtime. In everyone’s eyes Severus
was a workaholic but he was just aware of his duties and did his work with maximum care.
Finally everyone was sure they had enough holiday days and the dry noise in the office didn't
end. A birthday was being organized for an Auror in the corridor next to them and just as the
elderly witch Sophia was about to close the door with a weary expression, a grey-brown owl
dived in and landed on Severus’s desk.
“Shoo shoo,” said Sophia. “God, there is such a thing as an owlery!”
Severus untied the note on the animal’s leg, his heart tightened when he saw who the writer
was. The owl took off immediately and flew from the direction it came.
“Bad news, Severus?” asked another witch girl. A new graduate with short blonde hair who
came for an internship, speaking with an accent because she was of foreign nationality. The
only reason Severus tolerated her was that she dedicated herself to tasks more than the other
employees.
“No,” he said, folding the letter and putting it in his robe pocket. He had to go to Lily, he
stood up and left the room.
When he entered Lily's office, he saw her and Arthur giggling at facing desks. The tableau
was cute but if the same environment was in his own office, no work would ever get done.
He greeted them and asked Arthur for permission to kidnap Lily for ten minutes and took his
wife to a more deserted corridor.
“Dumbledore found the location of the third one,” he explained whispering, as soon as Lily
cast the Muffliato spell. “But there is a problem... he wants you too.”
“What?” said Lily, surprised for a moment. She involuntarily took her left hand to her
stomach. The baby hadn't grown yet.
“He suggested holding a meeting. Tonight, at Hogwarts. We put Harry to sleep and go
immediately, what do you say?”
“Could be,” said Lily, evaluating the options. “Of course I will come to destroy a Horcrux
Sev but,”
“That isn't possible Lily,” said Severus, his voice soft but clear. “If you enter possible danger,
I-I-”
He couldn't complete his words. He would go crazy, lose his mind. Maybe he would take his
own life, but he couldn't leave Harry either. He didn't even want to think of this possibility.
The same night, twenty minutes before midnight, they travelled to Dumbledore’s office
through the fireplace. When Lily and Severus entered Dumbledore's office, the air was heavy,
all three adults were stressed. Fawkes was fidgeting restlessly on his perch. Dumbledore was
sitting behind his desk with his usual calmness but that familiar twinkle wasn't in his eyes.
"Thank you for coming," he said, motioning for them to sit.
Severus didn't sit. "Why Lily?" he asked directly, his voice like a sharp knife. "Horcrux
hunting isn't a job for a pregnant woman." Lily hit his arm slightly hard. There was no need
to get angry at Dumbledore right now, evidently the man had a reason.
Dumbledore took a deep breath and clasped his hands together. "The place I called you to
isn't an ordinary hiding place, Severus. What guards that cave in the north of Wales isn't a
spell. An ancient creature, a dragon."
Then he continued. “It has immunity to magic and you can't be fully protected from its fire
with a shield. However, I will help Lily here." He pointed to the cloak standing by the table.
“Recognize it?”
Lily’s eyes widened. James had shown this to her once: when they were newly married. This
was an Invisibility Cloak and it explained all the Marauders’ mischiefs. He had a childishly
bright smile on his face and had said the cloak would belong to their children in the future.
“Professor, how did this come into your possession?”
“There was something I wanted to test, purely academic. I intended to return it but... then I
never found the time.”
Severus squinted, looking suspiciously. “This is an Invisibility Cloak, meaning it only renders
one invisible.” He touched the fabric, it was definitely delicate, not something that would
wear out easily. “It won't hide Lily’s solid form. So it’s not resistant to fire either.”
“That is true,” said the Headmaster. “Ancient dragons perceive ‘threats’ entering their
territory by scent. Testosterone, adrenaline, and aggression... The moment two adult male
wizards step in there, it will turn us to ash before we can even draw our wands. To it, Severus
and I are merely two rival males. However... the pheromones emitted by a female, especially
a mother, suppress the dragon’s hunting instinct. The dragon will not wake. Or even if it
wakes, it will not attack.”
The room had turned to ice. Severus looked at Dumbledore, unable to believe what he heard:
“How can you expect me to say yes to using my wife and unborn child—as a living shield
against that creature? This is madness! Never!”
“It is a necessity, Severus,” said Dumbledore, his voice still calm but with sadness in his
eyes. “This is the only way to get the Horcrux out of there.”
“Severus,” said Lily calmly, putting her hand on her husband’s arm. Severus was trembling,
mad with rage.
“I can't, Lily,” said Severus, looking at Dumbledore. “I can't put you in there.”
“If that Horcrux isn't destroyed, neither Harry nor this baby will ever grow up in a safe
world,” said Lily. Her voice was resolute and Severus fell silent. She took the cloak and
draped it over her shoulders. It worked, her head seemed to float in the room, her feet were
invisible too.
-----
February 6, 1985
Lily and Severus had to consider every possibility, even though they hated it. They had to
keep the possibility of failure in mind too. The blood magic proceeding from Petunia was
active, the Dursleys had been informed about this too.
But that night, Sirius came to pick up Harry. They only told him his mother and father had
very serious business. They didn't say goodbye very differently, it was more appropriate to
leave the impression as if Harry was going to spend the weekend in Italy. Sirius didn't act
very jokingly. He didn't speak emotionally with Lily and Severus either. He took the very
excited Harry and left via the fireplace again.
As the night brightened into morning, they met Dumbledore in Hogsmeade again. The small
and sweet village was under snow. Lily had loved this place since her childhood. Severus
hugged her, kissed her hair and slipped the cloak he was holding over Lily’s head, covering
her thoroughly. Lily was invisible now.
The baby kicked inside. This step was important for its future as much as for Harry. They
knew what was at risk. Lily took her hand out and held Severus’s hand. Despite the cold air,
his hand was warm.
“Since there is no fireplace where we are going, we need to Apparate to even colder climates
in this cold weather,” said Dumbledore, with a mischievous air in his voice. Severus frowned
but Lily smiled.
“We are sure of the plan, right?” asked Lily one last time, pointing to Severus holding the
cloth bag. Dumbledore confirmed with a nod.
Severus looked at the bag on his shoulder too. “I can feel the darkness,” he murmured. “Not
very strong but... in panic.”
“Horcruxes sense their end coming,” said Dumbledore. “They aren't just solid objects... they
are alive.”
Dumbledore took them to the depths of a desolate, rocky mountain or a forgotten cave. It was
likely they were outside England. Severus checked Lily’s cloak again, her feet weren't
visible.
Dumbledore whispered: “Ordinary dragons love gold. But this... This Ancient One loves
purity and hates darkness. It will sense all three Horcruxes we have.”
They entered inside the cave. The air was hot, smelled of sulphur. Severus was walking in
front, followed by Lily who was both invisible under the cloak and within Dumbledore's
protective shield. Dumbledore was walking beside Lily.
They found that giant dragon, whose scales looked like they had turned to stone, sleeping
three hundred metres away in the deep darkness. Inside the treasure the dragon guarded was
the Hufflepuff cup.
Lily summoned the cup with a Summoning Charm but all three knew this wouldn't work.
“Wouldn't hurt to try our luck,” said Lily. Severus scanned the treasure with hawk-like eyes,
and saw the cup.
At that moment the dragon opened its eye. Lifted its giant head. Its eyes locked directly onto
Severus. Just at that moment, Lily, whom the dragon couldn't see but could smell, took a step
forward, the dragon paused. Its nostrils flared, perhaps a calm scent coming from Lily spread
in the air... The pheromones had confused the dragon. Its eyes closed.
Severus: “I am going to get the cup.” No need for other words, Lily and Dumbledore would
protect him of course. No need for Lily to say be careful either.
When the dragon opened its eye again, its pupils were like a well of fire. Dumbledore held
Lily’s arm, it was surprising he knew where she was despite her being invisible. It wasn't safe
for Lily to walk further. The dragon growled, the ground shook. It wasn't breathing fire yet.
Dumbledore raised his wand signalling them to stay calm. Severus was locked on his target.
Severus stepped forward with the cloth bag in his hand. He was 3 metres from reaching the
cup. Something in the bag, most likely the locket, was trembling and heating up due to the
dragon’s presence.
When Severus took the cup in his hand, he lifted it in the air. Now the ancient dragon was
very angry. Lily took a step back. Severus looked into the dragon’s eyes and shouted: “Is this
what you are looking for?” The dragon sensed that intense, sticky darkness lying in the goblet
in Severus’s hand and the other objects in the bag. Severus put the cup in the cloth bag
without taking his eyes off the dragon.
The Horcruxes were practically groaning. It threw its massive head back. The scales inside its
ribcage started glowing with orange and white light. The air in the cave heated up to an
unbreathable point.
“Severus! NOW!” roared Dumbledore. His voice had drowned out even the dragon’s growl.
In the tenth of a second the dragon opened its mouth and released that blinding white flame,
Severus threw the bag in his hand—containing the locket, cup and diary—into the air, right
into the center of the flames.
Immediately after, he threw himself back, behind the silvery shield Dumbledore created.
BOOM!
When the ancient flame met the three cursed objects in the air, a shrill, metallic scream broke
out shaking the cave. This sound was of a kind the human ear couldn't withstand; as if three
different souls were screaming in torment at the same time.
Inside the flames, pitch-black smoke appeared first, trying to form shapes resembling a snake
and a skull, but the Dragon Fire was so pure and strong that it consumed the dark smoke
within seconds.
The shockwave emitted as the Horcruxes were destroyed shook Dumbledore’s shield. Rocks
had started falling from the cave ceiling. Lily held onto the cloak but it was useless. While
the dragon shook its head with the effect of the flame it spat and the satisfaction of the
“cleansing”, its tail was demolishing the cave walls. If they didn't get out immediately, they
would die here.
“WE ARE LEAVING!”
Dumbledore grabbed Lily with one arm, and Severus, who was getting up from the ground
with a soot-covered face, with the other. Just as the cave was about to collapse on them, the
familiar Crack! sound was heard.
Seconds later, they came to their senses with the cold wind hitting their faces. They had
landed on a quiet, snow-covered hill near Hogsmeade.
The sky was grey but there was no sulphur in the air, the Horcruxes weren't screaming. They
had succeeded, they had escaped. Lily was the first to laugh, took the cloak off herself, threw
herself onto the snow.
The sleeve of Severus’s robe was burnt and his hair smelled of smoke but his eyes were
shining. “It’s over,” he said, looking at the sky. He started laughing too, this was a release of
nerves. He hugged Lily. “It’s all over. They are gone.”
Dumbledore took a deep breath while putting his wand in his pocket. He looked tired but he
had stood up. “It was perfect timing, Severus. I am sure Regulus has found peace.”
“We are at peace now too,” said Lily. Then she suddenly flinched. When Severus noticed, he
immediately straightened up in the snow. “Lily? Are you okay? Are you hurt?”
There was a strange, bewildered expression on Lily’s face. “No I’m not hurt. It passed,” she
said breathlessly. She had taken a hard baby kick to her ribs.
“Since you don't have the Horcrux matter over your heads anymore, maybe not doctor’s
orders but, I assume you need to rest for a few weeks Lily.” The Headmaster smiled looking
at the two young people.
“Definitely, this order will be obeyed at home,” said Severus, half-joking half-serious.
-----
PART 3: For What Remains
Lily POV, July 1978
Lily did not deny Severus’s allure. She just didn't say it out loud. Sometimes she realized she
was holding her breath while Severus spoke. And sometimes when Severus fell silent, she
wanted to speak senselessly.
Still, Lily Evans saw not admitting that she loved Severus Snape as a form of mental hygiene.
Some truths lost their function when explicitly named. They would disperse, lose their effect.
Severus’s existence was like this. She applied this not as a defense mechanism, but as a
method of organization. Not admitting it kept it under control.
But Lily was hormonal. This, too, was undeniable. She noticed Severus’s walk. The way his
shoulders were slightly hunched forward. The low but unwavering tone of his voice when he
spoke. People withdrew when looking at him. Lily, however, wanted to get closer. This was
biological. It was illogical. It was embarrassingly obvious. Being near an attractive man
wasn't new for Lily. But her defenses were low when she was with him.
The last time they went to Hogsmeade, Lily watched Severus while sipping her beer. This
time without hiding it. The moment his lips touched the glass, his swallowing, the shadow of
his eyelashes. Her mind produced no logical deduction. She was just watching.
"You are looking at me," said Severus.
"Yes."
"Are you analyzing?"
"No," said Lily. "Not this time."
It wasn't analysis, it was curiosity. She had always been a student hungry to research and
learn. But school was over now. The option to live together in the same flat in central London
at an affordable price had suddenly fallen into their laps; they hadn't planned this. The house
was very big anyway, a duplex flat and two living rooms? But they accepted and moved in
within three days.
Building a life in the same environment was not like studying at the same school. Even
though they had seen each other with the messiest hair and in pyjamas countless times... this
house was enchanted in a different way.
Two weeks after moving in, Lily felt the need to better organize the thoughts in her head.
For this reason, when she realized she couldn't cope with the desires she felt for Severus, she
got herself a notebook. Hardcover. Plain. Identity-less. Keeping a diary wasn't like Lily, but
this was a situation that needed to be recorded. Severus’s name never appeared in the
notebook. She had chosen this deliberately. Instead, there were descriptions: "Tone of voice."
"Feeling of proximity." "Shoulder distance." "Morning silence."
Lily wasn't romanticizing her desires. She was examining them. She noted at which moments
they intensified, by which words they were triggered. Especially in the evenings. The times
when Severus was without his cloak.
She wrote about his stance in the kitchen. His not leaning against anything. His trying not to
take up space. His moving as if he were asking permission from the house.
August 1978
Seven weeks had passed since they moved in. Seven Fridays had passed together.
Lily’s shift had ended early. The other witches—meaning the non-interns—were going to
drink Butterbeer. Lily did not join. The day had been heavy. In the morning hours, she had
gone to the funeral of her old schoolmate James Potter’s mother. The death had been sudden;
from the flu. James, who always looked cool and nonchalant, seemed truly lost for the first
time. Lily was unaware that James’s father had also died just a week after graduation.
The James who showed off to impress Lily seemed to have died with them. He was no longer
a child either.
For this reason, she thanked god that she could call Severus ‘my closest’ once again. She
wanted to go home in the evening and fall asleep talking about the novels they were reading
together. Lily had just started Dracula by Bram Stoker. She had experienced a brief horror
when she learned Severus had finished the book twice. Severus wasn't just a nerd when it
came to Potions or the Dark Arts. In fact, he was a terrific bookworm.
But Severus wasn't home when she arrived. It wasn't a problem; they worked in different
departments. But Severus still wasn't there. It was 10 at night. And Lily realized this clearly
for the first time: Severus Snape’s absence was not silent.
She started getting angry, even though she knew she had no right to be. If Severus wasn't
going to say where he went, why was Lily trying? She had hidden nothing from Severus until
that day. But Severus was taking no steps at all.
Being rejected… Being rejected even though she hadn’t chosen to strive for a man’s attention
wasn’t what hurt her. But she had left doors open. She had cast glances. Those glances were
the glances of a woman in love.
Severus didn't see. Severus was blind. Lily wasn't. She had touched. Hugged. Wanted.
Desired. Fantasized. She had even fantasized about him in bed. She knew Severus. His
shyness, his smile, his feelings unspoken.
When Severus came home at midnight, he was a mess. Lily vaguely remembered asking him
where he had been. The look Severus gave her was ice cold.
Severus said he had work. He was lying. Lily was offended but reminded herself: ‘His
business is not my concern.’ She only asked him how he could stay out of the house for hours
in such a dark period, at a time when safety was so important. She wanted an explanation.
That look again. Lily felt a physical chill, a shiver. “Just because we have separate lives, we
can’t make this work without letting each other know,” she told him.
Severus stared as if Lily had slapped him. For the first time, an emotion crossed his face.
Sadness. His lips parted. “Are you saying this?” he said.
Lily couldn't understand. For the first time, she couldn't read him. She had hurt him and didn't
know the reason. Then something even more senseless happened. Severus kissed Lily for the
first time. All the questions Lily was going to ask him flew away. She put her hands on his
chest. She didn't push him away.
Her heart was beating fast. It was real. Then they broke apart. Lily’s feelings were making
her dizzy, the ground didn't feel safe. Severus took a step back. He was surprised too. Lily
knew those looks too, she knew him. If his skin touched hers a little more...
“I shouldn't have done that,” said Severus.
“Which one?”
Severus chose silence. Lily continued. “Ignoring me,” she said. “That wasn't protecting me.”
She lifted her head. This time there was no ice in Severus’s gaze. Hot anger was in his eyes,
but it wasn't directed at Lily.
“Because you see me like this,” Severus said, “I doubt myself. I am not an impulsive person.”
He took another step back unconsciously. “Nor am I used to,” he said, “someone choosing
me.”
There it was. The catalyst. “You weren't chosen,” said Lily calmly. “You were already here.”
And Severus would always be with her; Lily would not mislead him on this.
December 31, 1978
On New Year's Eve, the house was semi-lit by the mixed light of candles and gas lamps.
There was no snow outside, but the cold was like a sharp reminder seeping in through the
windows.
Lily had looked at her notebook while preparing her own gift. A small time machine where
she kept records of love and desire. He would be with her as she read. She scanned the small
box and tied a red ribbon on it. It wasn't showy, but it represented deep love.
They met in the kitchen at midnight. Her lover was waiting for her.
Severus was sitting in the kitchen with a small, rectangular package. It had a black ribbon on
it. Choosing the gift had taken him hours. He had weighed it with his logic, taken care that it
was functional. But beyond functionality, he wanted to leave an unexplained impact on Lily’s
life.
Lily could see him clearly now, as he was, not from behind a curtain. She could understand
him now. Severus had shared some, if not all, of his dreams and fears with her. Lily’s gift
created a brief hesitation in Severus’s eyes. There was a note hidden between the pages of the
book:
Trust yourself. Sometimes the fear of losing prevents winning. You can achieve this.
Severus felt as if he were on a small bridge built between emotion and logic.
(End of flashback)
Martina was in a rush.
Christmas, 1990
Her twenty-hour shift had just ended. She had less than two hours to stop by home, shower,
and then reach the Portkey with Sirius, but… the trains were on strike.
Luck was on her side. Paolo, her shift colleague who was an anaesthesiologist, was walking a
