few metres away with a motorcycle helmet in his hand. He was a bit sticky, but lately, he
hadn't been hitting on Martina.
Because last week, Sirius had come to the hospital with a sandwich he made at home, giving
everyone the message that “Martina is taken.” When Martina returned home, they inevitably
argued; Sirius had admitted his cunning, with a glint of both determination and slightly
irritable love in his eyes. If she hadn't been sure of his good intentions, she might have sulked
at her boyfriend, but she didn't. Sirius truly cared about his loved ones.
“Paolo, how are you?” she called out, waving. After a short chat, Martina hopped on behind
Paolo. The engine started, and the city blurred with Christmas lights. Paolo dropped her off
right in front of the house. “I can't thank you enough,” said Martina. Paolo smiled. “If you
treat me to a pizza, we're even.”
Martina entered the flat laughing. Sirius was in the living room considering whether to take
his guitar or not. “Take it,” said Martina. “Definitely take it.”
Before entering the bathroom, Sirius called out: “If I had known there was a strike, I would
have come to pick you up.”
“Paolo dropped me home, on his motorbike.”
“WHAT?” Sirius’s face appeared at the door instantly. “Martina, that boy is a total leech—”
there was an expression of disgust, not anger, on his face. Martina turned on the shower so as
not to escalate the argument, but Sirius had already entered the bathroom.
“I don't like that man. He's too close with you—”
“Amore, enough please,” said Martina. “You look like an ordinary nagging oaf.”
“When it comes to Paolo, yes I am. He wants what is mine.” Martina pouted. “You are very
jealous,” she said, but she was grinning.
Before the argument dragged on, Sirius returned to packing the suitcases. When Martina
dried her hair, two small suitcases were ready in front of the door. “Let's carry the guitar by
hand,” said Sirius. Martina gave a thumbs up.
When they arrived at the street where the Portkey was located, Martina tensed up a bit. She
wasn't against Apparating, but Muggles sometimes experienced nausea. She belonged to that
group.
A few seconds later, they were in front of Andromeda’s house. They had reached the familiar
garden in one piece, and both of their stomachs were intact. A slight tension appeared on
Sirius’s face.
“I hope she likes you,” he said. “If I fall out with my only relative too, we’ll spend all
holidays with yours.”
“They wouldn't mind. Besides, you like my family,” said Martina.
“You are going to make me obese; the scale shows over 80 already, amore.”
Martina giggled. “I’ll prepare a diet list for you, amore.”
Ted Tonks opened the door. They introduced themselves and went inside. Sirius hugged his
cousin first, then her husband. “We haven't seen Andromeda for four years,” he said, turning
to Martina. “Last in '86—” “—just before Dora left for school,” Ted finished. “You had come
with Harry.”
“I guess Moony hasn't arrived yet,” said Sirius; he had hung up his girlfriend’s coat and was
scanning the room. “For the first time, we are arriving on time for a visit,” he added,
grinning. Andromeda rolled her eyes at her cousin.
Just then, the door opened again. “It’s so cold, I’m freezing—oops!”
Tonks, whom her mother and father called Dora, had kicked the vase used as an umbrella
stand while taking off her boots. She bent down and fixed it, then noticed the arrivals.
“Sirius! You got here so fast!” She took a packet out of her pocket while taking off her coat;
Sirius had arrived in the interval she went to the shop to buy chewing-gum.
Sirius introduced his niece to Martina. “This is Nymphadora, but don't call her by her name.
She is determined to become an Auror this year,” he said, grinning. “A job that is a mix of
magical detective and security guard.”
Tonks smiled brightly at both of them. She turned to Martina. “Call me Tonks, please,” she
added.
When Tonks went to the living room to her parents, the door rang again. Remus had arrived.
Martina opened the door for him, then they hugged.
“Amore,” she said to Sirius, “your friend is an ice-man!”
Remus smiled and came inside. He hugged Sirius too. Andromeda and Ted seated him by the
fireplace; Remus warmed up in a short time. The conversation shifted to daily life. Martina
and Tonks were talking at the kitchen entrance about which wine to open.
“I wish we had brought that Tuscan wine,” said Martina. She had pouted her lips like a child.
“I brought it,” said Sirius, laughing slightly. “In the blue suitcase.”
While Remus followed Sirius into the kitchen, Martina had dashed upstairs to get the wine.
Tonks, who was directing the glasses to the counter with a Summoning Charm without
breaking them this time, suddenly froze and stopped. She had seen the guest who arrived last
at the house, and Remus had noticed the young Auror at the same time. Sirius clapped Remus
on the shoulder.
“Here is my best friend, Remus,” said Sirius, elbowing his friend when his sentence finished.
Martina had already come downstairs, she gave what she was holding to Sirius, and Sirius
handed the bottle to Tonks.
“What happened, sweetie, did you see a ghost?” he teased Tonks affectionately.
“Ah… no,” said Tonks. “Thanks, Sirius.”
Glasses were filled, and everyone found their favourite spot. While Martina was talking
about wine pairings with Tonks and Ted, Andromeda was in the corner talking to Sirius about
Death Eater activities. Sirius didn't hide anything from Martina, but he softened the details a
bit when telling her. Since there were so few wizards in Italy, let alone having trouble with
Muggles...
“People have started to get scared again,” said Remus. “Everything is like it was ten years
ago.”
Tonks, noting that Chardonnay was the best pairing for the baked, rich fatty salmon, turned
on the radio with a happy expression, and the gloom in the room dissipated. The young Auror
candidate possessed many Muggle hobbies: she was most interested in the culinary arts. She
sat by the window. After a while, Remus came over to her.
“It’s nice to see you in the daylight… Remus,” said Tonks, averting her eyes slightly.
“Platinum blonde hair suited you,” said Remus, then paused. “I apologize. My mind went to
the past.”
“Mine too.”
(Flashback) May 1989
The Tonks family lived in a town with both wizarding and Muggle populations. Although
both sides were acquainted, relationships remained superficial due to the Statute of Secrecy;
especially because of the re-emerging anti-Muggle hatred.
Tonks, like every nineteen-year-old adolescent, had left the local swimming pool within the
framework of her limited freedom. She was leaning against the hood of Candice’s car with
her friends Ezra, Candice, and Isabel, sipping her beer while Isabel told them her latest dating
story uncensored.
These were adult-specific details Tonks hadn't experienced yet, but she had no intention of
revealing her inexperience to these cool girls. She rested her head on Ezra’s shoulder and
handed him the beer bottle. While laughing at what the girls said, she paused for a moment;
she noticed Ezra’s look at her. Ezra was her friend, and giving him false hope would be cruel.
She lifted her head, smiling gently.
Then Candice started complaining about her tall basketball player boyfriend. Apparently, all
he did was undermine the girl’s self-confidence. When Isabel and Ezra hurled advice like "We
told you twenty times to break up with him" and "Don't let him treat you like this," Candice
bowed her head. It was obvious she was upset. Tonks felt deep sympathy for her; human
relationships were sometimes even more complicated than magic.
Without understanding what was happening, two shady types approached the four
entertaining youths in the empty parking lot. Tonks recognized trouble. These were the
neighbourhood muggers. They hurled empty threats for a few minutes. Verbal harassment
continued until Ezra, the only male in the group, took a punch. While one held Candice by
her hair, the other waved his knife at Isabel. Tonks intervened.
“Is the car yours, beautiful babe? Give me the keys,” said the man holding Candice’s hair
roughly.
“You'll see what happens if I make you swallow those keys. Let the girl go.”
The man pushed Candice but this time walked towards Tonks, grinning. “Look at this... The
blondie is going to show us what for, Pete.” Tonks gripped her wand in the back pocket of her
denim shorts with her left hand. To hell with Ministry rules, she thought. “My last warning to
you.”
While Isabel was sobbing, Pete’s attention suddenly shifted to the darkness behind. They
were no longer alone. Everything happened in a few seconds, at a speed the human eye
couldn't follow. Blood suddenly gushed from the mouth of the man walking towards Tonks.
The mugger seemed to be drowning in his own blood. Then a wound opened in his chest.
Tonks didn't even blink. The man fell to the ground. Like a puppet with cut strings. He was
dead.
A man appeared behind the corpse: An angry face, messy dark hair, a large, animalistic nose,
and yellow eyes glowing in the dark. Tonks had seen many drawings of what a werewolf
looked like in Defense Against the Dark Arts classes. It didn't take long for her to realize that
the man in front of her was in that dangerous phase stuck between human form and wolf
form.
The emotion she should have felt ought to have been horror in the face of witnessing sheer
brutality. Her friends were screaming, Ezra lay unconscious. But Tonks... she did not feel
horror. Tonks felt excited. It was as if an electric current were coursing through her veins.
The pool of blood on the ground hadn't turned her stomach. After the man threw aside the
heart he had ripped from the mugger with his bare hands, he lifted his head and looked
directly at Tonks, pausing for a moment. Perhaps he hadn't expected this blonde young girl in
front of him not to scream, but to look at him as if enchanted. He snarled, his chest heaving
rapidly.
Pack-affiliated werewolves who transformed outside of the full moon did not change as they
did during the full moon; they retained human characteristics. Except for their keen hearing,
sight, and sense of smell.
Of course, Tonks would learn this later, while researching and trying to understand what had
happened to her.
Candice and Isabel were crying. The man’s back was turned to them; they couldn't see the
corpse. But they realized something bad had happened. Ezra had fainted; Isabel was prodding
him. The second stranger had killed Pete as well. Candice and Isabel were probably crying
about that too. Then the werewolf duo ran away, plunging into the wooded terrain.
By the time the police arrived, the crime scene had already been sealed as a "Wild Animal
Attack." Andromeda and Ted were very worried and angry. Tonks didn't enter into any
arguments with her parents that night; she went straight up to her room and tried to forget this
brutality. Her parents forgot.
But Tonks... She couldn't. She researched. She read. She learned.
(Now)
“Blonde is a nice colour, but I like purple for now,” Tonks added. Remus smiled at her. He
was about to say something, but Ted and Andromeda called out to him from the living room.
After dinner, when Remus finished the third glass of wine Ted offered, he stood up. There
was an imperceptible rush in his movements as he straightened his jacket. “I should ask for
your permission to leave now,” he said politely. “My flat in the city centre isn't quite—”
“Don't be ridiculous, Remus!” Ted Tonks caught the man’s objection in mid-air and crushed
it. “It’s freezing outside; we won't send you to that cold house at this hour. The guest room is
ready.”
“Really, to cause inconvenience—”
“Sirius and Martina are staying upstairs,” said Andromeda, with that 'Black' intonation that
accepted no objection. “And you will stay in the guest room downstairs. We only see our old
friends once in a decade anyway; I have no intention of letting you escape.” Seeing the
authoritarian affection in Andromeda’s gaze, Remus dropped his shoulders; he had
surrendered.
As it was approaching two in the morning, everyone dispersed to their rooms. There was only
one guest room on the ground floor. It looked like a cross between a bedroom and a study;
there was no sound from upstairs, not even snoring could be heard, but Remus’s brain wasn't
quiet enough to sleep.
He was lying on his back, staring at the dark ceiling. His mind kept reverting to May 1989, to
that bloody car park. Dora. Tonks. Nymphadora Tonks. She should have been disgusted,
terrified. But the way she looked at him at the dinner table... Those challenging, curious looks
terrified Remus. God, was the girl from that night Tonks? Did I rip someone's heart out in
front of my best friend's niece?
Just as he was about to close his eyes and force himself to sleep, his wolf senses sharpened.
There were no footsteps in the corridor. First came the scent, but very faint; the sound of a
breath, almost as silent as a ghost, stopped in front of his door. A sharp, ice-cold scent of
menthol. Remus sat up in bed, holding his breath. Then, a piece of white parchment slid in
through the gap under the door. And along with the parchment, immediately following it, a
heady and sweet scent of jasmine filled the room, softening the sharp menthol.
Without using a wand, with only his night vision and the pull of that jasmine scent filling his
nose, he picked the paper up from the floor. Even the paper smelled like her; as if her hand
had touched her hair while writing.
That sentence hurriedly written on it: “Come to the kitchen balcony. I have cigarettes.”
Remus stared at the note. His logic said, "Go back to bed, lock the door, and flee first thing in
the morning." But his body had already stood up.
He put on his cardigan. He slipped silently into the kitchen. The balcony door was ajar. When
he stepped outside, he saw her.
On the balcony, which was colder than the other rooms of the house even though its windows
were closed, Tonks was placing a cushion on an iron chair to sit. Tonks turned her head at the
slight click made as Remus closed the door. She stood there in a loose T-shirt and plaid
pyjama bottoms. Her hair had now taken on a silvery purple colour in the moonlight.
The expression on her face was unreadable. “I thought you wouldn't come,” said Tonks, her
voice like a whisper.
“You should have been sleeping,” said Remus, his voice raspy. He stood at a safe distance in
the doorway. “Don't you have work tomorrow?”
“Christmas holiday.” Tonks held out the pack of cigarettes to Remus. Remus smiled but
declined.
“I can't believe I forgot the Ministry is on holiday,” he said to himself. Tonks handed him a
cushion this time. “Come sit.” Remus sat on the other chair. How was Tonks not cold? Remus
wanted to wrap himself tighter in his cardigan. The scent of menthol and jasmine was felt
more clearly from this distance.
Tonks looked at the cigarette pack in her hand, “Actually, I quit, you know?” she said. Then
she fixed her eyes on Remus’s amber eyes. It was the exact same look as in the car park.
“That night,” said Tonks, not beating around the bush. “Life became too meaningful to
poison oneself like this. So I quit. It happened quickly and just like that.”
Remus took a deep breath. He had started to sense where this was leading. The shiver that
came had nothing to do with the cold. “I'm glad. A bad Muggle habit.”
“My dad and mum don't know. They just think it was an 'attack'. They've already forgotten
anyway.”
“It's better they don't know,” said Remus. “There is no place for monsters in their safe world,
Dora.”
“Don't call me Dora,” said the girl, sharply. Then she stood tall, and Remus now saw a
woman before him. “My name is Tonks. And please let me thank you.” Then Tonks paused.
“That was the most impressive thing I have ever seen in my life.”
Remus lifted his head in shock. “You are crazy.” Then he took a deep breath. “I was very out
of control that night. We were practicing hunting for fun with the Alpha, to control non-full
moon transformations.”
-----
Two weeks passed very quickly. The snow that had fallen melted, and a new year began. With
Lily in the driver's seat and Louie in his child seat in the back, she skillfully parked her new
car in the empty, grassless front yard.
Severus was watching the newly arrived red Hyundai from the kitchen window, with Iris in
his arms. Iris was playing with the top button of his sweater with her little fingers. As the
engine sound cut out, Harry slid down the banister; Severus whipped his head around so fast
it nearly snapped off.
“Harry! You must walk down the stairs on your feet.” It wasn't the first time his father had
suffered a mini heart attack at the boy's recklessness.
“Sorry, Dad,” he said, then opened the door and ran to his mother; his father followed him
out. Iris watched her big brother with curious eyes.
“Whoa,” said Severus, grinning. “Nice. Does it reflect the Gryffindor spirit, or was it just a
coincidence?”
“I didn't like the white and grey models at all, Sev.” Lily got out of the car, and Iris beamed at
her mother, but Lily opened the back door first to unbuckle Louie.
“They didn't look like they did in the catalogue. This is more vivid and sporty.” Once Louie
was out of the car, she took Iris into her arms, kissed the child's hair and cheeks, and then
hugged Harry.
“Is your geography homework done?” Harry nodded. Severus ruffled his hair. “We could say
I did the homework.”
The children went into the house ahead of them. Harry was 10, and Louie was 5. Louie had
wavy red hair like his mother and brown eyes. He was considered tall for his age; in many
stores, he wore clothes for seven-year-olds rather than five. He was very intelligent, talkative,
and curious. But he was too outspoken; when asked what he wanted to be when he grew up,
he had said, ‘I’m going to be a dad,’ causing his parents a few seconds of horror. But now
they laughed about it.
Harry, five years his senior, had straight but messy black hair and his mother's green eyes.
Physically, he had a normal build, though he was lean. He played Quidditch very well and
swam actively at the school gym.
Hair that wouldn't behave no matter how much he combed it was a problem shared by his
biological father, James. Harry was no longer afraid when his father, Severus, told him he
might give him a buzz cut with clippers while he slept whenever he procrastinated on his
maths homework. He could see through the bluff, and besides, he had started to like maths.
Moreover, his father would never intentionally upset him; Lily found these sweet bickerings
very amusing.
Iris was the first (and for now, the only—though Severus had expressed that three children
were sufficient) planned baby. She was a redhead like her mother and Louie, but her eyes
were blue. Since one parent of both Lily and Severus had blue eyes, this was an unexpected
but not surprising development. She was born two years ago in May; she was 20 months old.
Harry had been slightly disappointed when he learned that first-years couldn't participate in
Quidditch tryouts. In his view, a joyless academic year awaited him next year, but according
to his mother, he was exaggerating.
The house at Spinner’s End had also undergone a transformation over time. The kitchen and
living room had been made more spacious with Extension Charms; accordingly, new
furniture had been purchased. Thanks to the new bathroom added downstairs, Harry no
longer had to climb the stairs at night. When Remus put his family home up for sale and
moved to a smaller, more compact house, he had brought the piano to them.
Louie had fallen in love with the instrument; he tried to play occasionally, but all he did was
give everyone a headache. Harry wasn't as passionate, but he played better.
Changes had been made upstairs as well. Severus’s old room from his youth had been
renovated for Louie. Lily hadn't had the heart to let newborn Iris sleep in the same room as
Louie; Louie also needed uninterrupted sleep. For this reason, the Extension Charm was used
for the nursery as well, and Iris had started sleeping in the room allocated to her.
While Louie was telling his brother how they chose the car, in the kitchen Severus touched a
large pot full of water with his wand and the water boiled instantly. While he was preparing
tea for both himself and Lily, Lily suddenly hugged her husband from behind and started
kissing his neck.
“My love, wait—these are very hot.” He immediately placed the cups on the counter; he
didn't want to spill them on himself and get scalded.
Lily took advantage of this opening to look inside, towards the living room. Harry was
playing the piano, so the melody being heard—unlike Louie's attempts—was not grating on
the ears. Louie was beside him too. They seemed to be having fun, but Lily knew that the
time she could spend with Severus without being disturbed was only a few minutes. A little
way off from the piano, Iris was content by herself with her toy blocks.
Severus, having made the kitchen safe, leaned towards her this time and they started kissing.
Severus wrapped his arms around her; Lily ran one hand through Severus’s hair, and with the
other, cupped her husband’s bum.
“In broad daylight? Louie will be horrified again,” said Severus, grinning. Lily rolled her
eyes; Severus kissed her hair at that moment.
“Not for a little while,” said Lily. The next day was Monday; schools were opening, life was
returning to normal. They could make time for each other just as they did on days they didn't
go to work. Every time Louie caught them kissing he gave funny and sweet reactions, but for
Harry, this was normal.
After dinner, while Severus examined a few official documents regarding his work, Lily,
Harry, and Louie teamed up to complete a puzzle. They had been working on it for three
days, and they had shown quite a lot of progress.
While Lily tidied up the mess with magic, Severus went upstairs to put the two small children
to bed. After the last plush toy went into the basket, Harry was coming down the stairs.
“Were you upstairs?” asked Lily; she hadn't noticed Harry going up. Harry nodded; he must
have said goodnight to Severus upstairs too, and he was getting very sleepy. He hugged his
mother and mumbled goodnight to her as well. At that exact second, Severus was coming
down the stairs and entering the living room. Lily had hugged Harry, then suddenly tickled
her son, and Harry ran to his room squealing, his mother laughing behind him. Her eyes slid
to Severus, and Harry closed his room door a bit hard.
“There is a teenager in the house now,” Severus summarized the event.
After the children slept, they moved on to the real annoying agenda. The Horcrux matter,
which they had been seeking for years but which had been interrupted a bit by Iris's birth, had
been dropped into their laps again by Dumbledore a few weeks ago. Through the Pensieve,
Dumbledore had transferred a memory to both of them, and his suspicion that the fourth
Horcrux was the ring belonging to the Gaunt family had become a certainty.
Severus doubted it being this easy. “Which curse shall we research against tonight?” asked
Lily, heading towards the bookshelf next to the piano.
Severus was bringing sage tea in two cups. Finally, Severus had received a diagnosis of
allergic rhinitis at a fully-equipped Muggle hospital. Lily also had to go for check-ups more
frequently due to blood pressure fluctuations. For the last few months, Lily couldn't help
thinking ‘Just like my mother’; her mother had passed away at 52, two weeks after Lily
started seventh year.
Severus spoke very positively that the same scenario would not repeat, and Lily wanted to
believe him.
Severus had concentrated more on poisons that were invisible on an object, hard to detect,
and absorbed through the skin. If the Horcrux was really a ring, let alone wearing it on a
finger, even touching it would be dangerous. While drinking their tea, they both took notes
and compared them, just like they used to do at Hogwarts.
Towards midnight, they turned off the lights and went up to the bedroom. There wasn't much
change in their sleeping arrangements; Lily had switched to a firmer pillow and Severus
preferred a high pillow. While Severus waited to fall asleep, Lily clung to him like a limpet,
and Severus did the same.
Severus and Lily’s birthdays, which followed three weeks later, passed with celebrations at
home. With February, Iris could say a few words, Louie was slowly learning to read, and
Harry had come third in the new swimming race.
“It’s unfair,” he cried; he had been late by 4 seconds. Severus waited patiently for him to
calm down. “You have countless competitions ahead of you,” he said, trying not to keep his
voice devoid of emotion; he understood his son very well. Unfortunately, Harry was a bit
devastated whenever he didn't come in first, because he loved competition.
Harry’s lips trembled; he clenched his small fists to digest the defeat. Then he was persuaded
to take a shower.
Harry was silent in the car too, sniffing occasionally. He didn't speak until they got home.
“This result came even though I trained more,” he said to his father as he closed the car door.
“I should stick with Quidditch, shouldn't I? It seems like that’s my only talent.”
“No, Harry, that is not your only talent. You are good at maths too, your grammar is fine...
Also, you are very successful at being hard on yourself for no reason.”
Harry grinned for the first time since the race. “Dad, come on, don't make fun.” They entered
the house. “I was really upset.”
“If Sirius is home, would you like to talk to him through the fireplace?” suggested Severus.
“No, I’d prefer to talk to you. He just hypes me up and praises me. He talks about James...”
Harry stopped and grimaced. “I can't tell him not to compare us, because he would be very
hurt by that.”
“Ah, Harry,” said Severus, taking a chocolate pudding out of the fridge. He handed it to
Harry. “Let adults think about what and how they will feel. That is not your responsibility. It
really isn't.”
Harry sighed while spooning the dessert. “I think Sirius is sensitive about this. I’m not sure,
actually.” Then he paused. “But don't you talk to him. I should do it.” Severus agreed and
took a dessert for himself too.
“Hey, weren't you on a diet?” asked Harry, narrowing his eyes.
“Let’s say I’m celebrating my son’s third place.” Harry laughed out loud for the first time that
day.
-----
The inevitable balloon burst at Ron’s birthday a few weeks later. The already warm family
atmosphere in the kitchen at The Burrow was even more colourful now. The only Weasley
children not yet attending Hogwarts were Ron and Ginny. Severus was swamped with
Ministry work; it was next to impossible for him to make it to the end of the party.
Conversely, Lily and Arthur were having a relaxed year.
Lily arrived at The Burrow via the fireplace with the three children. Louie was a bit covered
in soot; while Molly was tidying him up, Lily was hugging Ron and Ginny. “Happy birthday,
handsome!” She handed the package to Ron; the boy’s face lit up. Ginny helped as he opened
the package, and the gift was revealed.
“Are these those small stone figures Arthur is trying to collect?” said Molly, laughing lightly.
“If so, these aren't for Ron, they're a gift for his father.”
“Everyone can build together actually, the game has no age limit,” said Lily. The Lego box
that came out of the package contained small, colourful pieces, and each piece was
meaningless on its own. The real point was interlocking the pieces; no glue, no magic, it was
possible to build everything with just imagination. It was also very easy to separate the
pieces.
Ron hugged Lily with joy and then took Harry’s hand, and they ran upstairs to play with the
Legos. Louie was explaining something to Ginny too. While the two mothers sat in the
kitchen watching the children (Harry and Ron had just gone out to the garden), two figures
appeared in the fireplace. Sirius and Remus. Lily, who hadn't seen her friend for months,
hugged Remus.
“Remus is busier these days,” said Sirius, giggling. “I think he is cheating on me. He wanders
around more well-groomed for someone else, Lily, look at this state of him. Clean-shaven,
perfume. A girl has bewitched him.”
“Really, Remus?” said Lily, suddenly curious. Remus was turning beetroot red with every
passing second.
“I can't imagine Remus in the forest with other wolf-girls,” said Sirius. “Ah, wait a second, I
can imagine it.” Then he winked at Lily and added, “But you won't need perfume during the
full transformation, my dear Remus.”
Sirius paused as if measuring the limit of exactly what he was going to say, biting his lips
slightly.
Remus rolled his eyes and mumbled ‘there is no wolf-girl’. Molly also scolded Sirius
jokingly and told him to leave poor Remus alone.
“If this man is poor, then we are miserable,” said Sirius with fake drama; Molly threw a towel
at him.
When Arthur arrived too, everyone went to the kitchen and Ron blew out his birthday cake.
“11 years old means Hogwarts age,” he said with joy. It was obvious he was impatient to go
to school. Harry wasn't that impatient. “I hope it won't be too boring,” he said to Ron in a low
voice. “Apparently, we are forbidden from playing on the Quidditch team,” he added.
Ron shrugged comfortably. “Who cares? We can fly in the garden however we want, no need
to get on the house team.”
Harry wanted to get on the house team, though. Ron patted his back. “You can try in the
second year though. I’ll join the tryouts with you too. Imagine both of us being on the team,
we’d have so much fun.”
This thought had amused Harry. They took the brooms to fly one last time before the sun set.
Lily, getting hot in the kitchen, watched the boys going out and went outside herself.
“Don't go too high,” she called from below. The wind had picked up and the children had
jumpers on. Ginny also glided into the air following them. Sirius came out after Ginny too.
“So Sirius, how are you?” said Lily without taking her eyes off the three brooms. Sirius
started explaining. Everything was fine with Martina, she was expecting a promotion soon.
He explained that she couldn't choose between cancer research and paediatrics. Then, while
he was mentioning that he received an Italian language proficiency certificate and Lily was
congratulating her friend with her mouth open, the three brooms landed.
“We got cold, as you might guess,” said Ron looking at the adults, but there was a smile on
his face. Harry and Ginny were following him too. Lily’s insides warmed while looking at
three happy faces.
“Come on inside, you must be frozen.”
“Oh Harry,” said Sirius with a very soft voice. He hugged him with one arm, and Harry
snuggled up to him. He was almost at shoulder height; in a few years they would be of equal
height. “Doesn't he fly wonderfully, Lily? You are a terrific talent, Harry. You two are
talented too but—” he winked at Ron and Ginny. “—Harry currently [gets] bonus points from
me—”
“Hah, we aren't offended at all,” said Ginny laughing, while Lily was raining sparks on Sirius
with her eyes.
“By the way, talent transfer via genetics really exists. Martina said so and I believe her. I
mean really just like—”
“—If you are going to say ‘just like James’, Sirius, this creates a problem and I am very fed
up with you.” Harry suddenly pulled away from Sirius's arm with a voice as cold as ice.
Sirius was astounded. “What?”
“I am Harry. Not James. Stop looking for your dead friend in me and see me for once.” His
voice cracked with intense emotion. “I don't want to hurt you, I love you, but really, enough
is enough.”
“I don't understand, Harry? What is the problem?” Sirius asked.
Lily felt the need to intervene. With a soft voice, she asked, “Sirius didn't say anything bad,
Harry, why are you reacting like this?”
Harry turned to his mother, his eyes brimming with tears. “I am telling him to stop comparing
me to a dead man. That is my problem. Ask yourself why I am reacting this way.”
Then he stormed inside, leaving Ron and Ginny in astonishment. “It's the first time I've seen
Harry cry,” murmured Ginny, breaking the silence. Lily was stunned; she lifted her head to
look at Sirius, and he was stunned too, looking equally shaken. As Ron and Ginny silently
entered the house, Lily turned towards Sirius.
But Sirius was walking in the opposite direction, towards the garden gate.
“Where are you going?” Lily called out.
Without even looking back, Sirius said, “I... I should go. See you later,” and Apparated as
soon as he stepped out of the protective wards.
When Lily went inside, only Remus, Louie, and Iris sleeping in Remus’s lap were in the
kitchen. “Is everyone upstairs?” asked Lily frantically. Louie was about to fall asleep too.
With a slight sadness, Remus said, “Harry was very... angry, Lily. He went upstairs crying,
the others followed him...” He sighed. “I can't blame him.”
Lily slumped onto the chair next to him. “Oh Remus, no... Harry snapped at Sirius and I
didn't foresee it.”
“I know. But it's understandable,” said Remus sincerely. Just then, Molly and Harry appeared
on the stairs. Harry’s eyes were red.
Trying to stay calm, Lily said, “Baby, are you feeling better? Do you want to talk?”
“Can we go home, Mum?”
Lily nodded, took Iris from Remus, and they went home through the fireplace.
When Harry said he wanted to be alone, Lily told him he needed to control his emotional ups
and downs, but added that he could play the piano if he wished, and took the two younger
children upstairs to sleep. When she came down ten minutes later, Harry was in his room and
had locked the door.
Lily’s patience was running thin. “Harry, we do not lock doors in this house!” she called out
from behind the door. “Either you open it yourself, or I will.”
She counted to twenty; when no sound came from inside, she opened the door with
“Alohomora” and poked her head in. Harry had pulled the duvet over his head, hiding. Lily
hesitated for a moment whether to go to him or not.
Finally, saying, “I am letting you be alone, Harry, but please do not keep the door locked,”
she gently closed the door.
Severus arrived via the fireplace just at that moment. “Molly said you came back early,” he
said, out of breath. “I thought you would stay for at least another three hours?”
Lily threw herself straight into Severus’s arms. “Oh Sev,” she pressed her face against his
chest; Severus hugged her tightly, as if understanding the situation, and started rubbing her
back like comforting a child. “What happened, my love, what is the problem?”
Sobbing, Lily said, “The problem is Harry,” and explained everything that had happened. Her
voice trembled as she wiped her eyes with her hand. “It’s my fault. Maybe I should have
explained mourning and longing better to Harry. Poor Sirius, he just stood there as if
slapped.”
Severus pulled her back slightly and looked into her eyes. “No, Lily. Harry is only ten years
old; you cannot expect adult maturity from him. Besides, I assure you he knows these
concepts.” He hugged Lily again. “Do you remember what he told me on the day of the
swimming race? What he felt that day...”
Lily started crying more violently. “Oh my God... I am a terrible mother.”
“No, you are not.” Severus kissed her wavy red hair, inhaled her scent. It was hard to see Lily
like this, but he needed to stay strong. “I was with him that day and he confided in me. Then
he forgot about it and moved on, that’s all.”
He continued in a soothing tone. “They are uncle and nephew, even if not by blood, Lily.
Moreover, when Harry is this upset too, how can they stay mad at each other? Give them
time, they will both sort it out.”
-----
Around the same time, in Italy.
When Sirius entered the house, he wasn't angry at all; on the contrary, his shoulders were
slumped. Martina was distracted and looked up from the book she was reading. Sirius took
off his jacket and collapsed onto the sofa, next to Martina. He silently rested his head on
Martina's shoulder. This was his way of seeking a "safe harbour".
Martina put down the book she was holding and ran her fingers through Sirius's hair. "Was it
a bad day?"
"It was terrible," murmured Sirius. "Harry and I had an argument."
Martina listened to her boyfriend without judgment. Sirius explained what happened, Harry's
outburst. But not to defend himself, just to share how much he was hurting.
"I just wanted to compliment him, Martina," said Sirius, lifting his head and looking into his
girlfriend's eyes.
"When I look at him, I see James, yes. I can't help it. James was my brother. Harry laughing
like him, flying like him... This makes me happy. I thought it would make Harry happy too.
But Harry acted as if I had insulted him."
Martina took Sirius's hand between her palms. "I know your intentions, Sirius," she said
softly. "You didn't mean that."
"Then why does it feel like he hates me?"
Martina thought for a moment, appearing to choose her words carefully. Sirius waited
patiently. "Maybe it's not hate, but a feeling of inadequacy?" She said, and added, "Just think
about it... Everyone tells him 'Your dad was great, your dad was a legend.' And when you, as
one of the people he values most, say 'You are just like your dad,' he hears this as 'The reason
I love you is your dad'."
Sirius paused. He had never looked at it from this angle. "But it's not like that. I love Harry.
Just because he is Harry."
"I know," said Martina. "I know how big your heart is. Just... James's shadow is so vast that
Harry might feel he is getting lost in that darkness sometimes."
Sirius let out a deep breath, his eyes staring into the distance. "I was unfair to him," he said
silently, whispering.
"You can make it up to him," said Martina, kissing him to support him. "But sometimes your
love is so intense that there is no room left for the other person to breathe."
Sirius smiled slightly; it was a sad smile. "I guess you're right. I should give him some
space."
The atmosphere at Spinner’s End felt oppressive. Harry knew he was in the wrong, but he
was tired of being compared to a man he didn't know and constantly being told he was just
like him, as if a single move of his own could insult his memory. And truthfully, he had been
quite insulting tonight. But Harry didn't care. He thought that if James had a shred of
common sense, he wouldn't like this comparison either.
Yes, he wished he had known him... But life was too short for 'what ifs', James was the prime
example of this. He was twenty years old when he died, and he was the person most entitled
to say 'what if'. Not Harry or Sirius.
He had also made a mistake by locking the door, but luckily his mother was quite
understanding. He deserved a slipper to the head one hundred percent, but Lily was really
wonderful. Why did he constantly upset her? Harry grew sad as he dove into his thoughts.
"Why do I constantly upset her?" he thought in the dark, under the duvet. "My mum can't
even bear to get angry at me, and here I am locking doors."
He could have stayed in bed until morning, but the call of nature prevailed. He pulled the
duvet off his head, sat up, and scanned his room in the dark. Two comic books and a maths
notebook were on his desk. The door was slightly ajar, and he could see the light in the living
room was on. His mother was definitely there. Probably his father too.
He fought a short internal battle between leaving his room and staying in bed. He could stay
in bed to avoid meeting his mother, to avoid an embarrassing stare-down, but his bladder
argued otherwise.
No one noticed him as he went to the bathroom, and after Harry handled his business, he
fixed himself in the mirror. His glasses were left in the bedroom so he couldn't see clearly,
but he made sure his eyes weren't too red. As he opened the door and stepped out, his father
was coming down the hallway. They made eye contact, and Harry was sure escape was
impossible now.
"Did you hear what happened?" He asked in a cautious voice. "I upset my mum too, and
Sirius as well. I might be unfair, but I don't regret it, so if you are going to be angry at me—"
"No, Harry, why should I be angry at you?" His voice sounded too understanding. Harry
swallowed. "Because it always happens like this. I upset both of you."
Without answering, Severus placed his hand lightly on Harry’s shoulder and guided him to
the living room. His mother was there too.
Harry sat next to his mother and looked at both of them. "I am sorry for my attitude," he said
in a low voice.
"It is not your fault, Harry," said Lily, in an equally low but soft voice.
“Harry, I know you know this, but let’s reiterate it anyway,” said Severus. Harry looked at
him with sincerity. When Harry grew up, he wanted to be like him, to stand unshakable in the
face of events, just like him. Severus continued. “Your genes will not change; in fact, I fear
that the person you will resemble more as you grow up will always remind Sirius of the past.
Remus too. But your soul belongs to you, and you are Harry. And the outburst you made
tonight actually made us both proud.”
What? What did that mean? Harry frowned, trying to make out his father's face with his
blurry vision. Had he heard wrong? Lily picked up where her husband left off. “Sirius was a
bit upset, but he will recover. I’ve known him for twenty years, Harry.” Lily rubbed his back.
“He is an adult who has completed enough of his emotional development not to hold a
grudge against you,” she said with a smile.
“Although I still have doubts about that 'maturity' part, I agree with the part about him not
holding a grudge,” said Severus, rolling his eyes.
Harry slumped his shoulders. “Still, I made him feel guilty and caused him to get upset.”
“These things happen,” said Lily. “You terrorize us occasionally too; Sirius had to get his
share,” she said, winking. Harry blushed. “I don't do it on purpose, Mum. I just get very
angry sometimes,” said Harry, ashamed. Lily kissed his hair.
“I know. I wasn't blaming you anyway.”
Then they sent Harry to his room to sleep, and Harry was grateful for it. Once in his room, he
threw himself onto the bed. The heavy stone on his chest had lifted. He felt lighter inside than
he had in a long time.
-----
Cold March transitioned into an April filled with abundant rain. To describe the English
weather merely as ‘gloomy’ would be a blow to its rich literature. But Severus was
experiencing the real blow that afternoon. Reclining slightly on the armchair in the living
room, he could neither take his eyes off the book in his hand nor help hating it with every
fiber of his being.
He turned the page furiously. "Idiot," he murmured. "Utter moron."
Lily giggled. They had posted Harry and Louie to Italy via the fireplace a few hours ago, and
Iris had just gone down for her afternoon nap. If Iris slept soundly tonight, they would be
alone, and Lily was barely keeping the lock on her plans in the deepest dungeon of her mind.
"What are you reading, Sev? The Rise of the Dark Arts or something again? Your face looks
like someone accidentally threw porcupine quills into your cauldron."
Without lifting his head, Severus said, "Worse. American Teen Literature." Lily approached
and saw the cover. Her eyes went wide. "Severus... Is that Sweet Valley High in your hand?"
Severus nodded.
“I bought it because it was number one on the bestsellers list. I thought it was about the lives
of farming families and their children or something. I certainly wasn't expecting this.”
Lily couldn't hold back her laughter. "Oh Sev... You mean you thought this was a pastoral
village novel because of the title? Cornfields, harvest season, and honest farmers and such?"
Severus straightened his shoulders defensively. "'Valley' is usually a geographical term; not a
cesspool where adolescent hormones accumulate."
"I have some unpleasant observations about this girl called Jessica... she creates chaos simply
because she is bored." Then he turned to his wife. “Listen to this. 'When Jessica saw that
Todd wasn't looking at her, she felt as if her world had come crashing down. Maybe the
colour of her headband was wrong? Maybe her life was over?'” He read the sentence from
the book with a serious, icy voice, as if reading the analysis report of a dangerous and
forbidden potion. Lily perched on the arm of the chair, next to Severus. Her shoulders were
shaking with laughter.
"Do you hear that? A headband! The girl's existential crisis hinges on a headband! When I
was this age, I was running from werewolves at midnight, while they get depressed because
they couldn't become prom queen." Lily was a bit calmer now, but she still had a smile on her
face, and her cheeks were flushed.
"If we’d had a prom culture, you would have been the queen though," said Severus, looking
at her with a softer, less sarcastic voice.
Lily reached out, gently took the book from his hand, and placed it on the coffee table, just
out of Severus's immediate reach. Then she snuggled up to Severus, running her fingers
through his black hair. Her voice shifted from that playful tone to an inviting one. "Leaving
poor Todd and the headbands aside, you would have been my prom king too. Also, my love,
right now there is no teen drama at home, no children's voices... No farming either. There is
just us."
Severus's gaze left the book on the table and slid to Lily's green eyes. That "hate-reading"
mood instantly dissipated.
"You're right," he said, holding Lily by the waist and pulling her towards him on the sofa.
"Farming books can wait."
In Sicily, however, the weather was brilliant, unlike England. There wasn't a scorching
summer heat, but a sweet spring warmth that made one relax prevailed.
Sirius and Martina were still staying in that cute student house, but the familiar chaos of an
approaching move could be felt inside. There were half-taped boxes and stacks of books
waiting to be packed all around.
Harry grasped the situation immediately; Martina appreciated this sharp observation of his.
"I am done with my training now, Harry," said Martina, placing books into a box. "After my
residency finished, I had the right to apply to work in different cities. So I tried my luck, but
unfortunately, the option I liked least came up: Rome. We have to move to the capital." She
grimaced and sighed as though she had eaten a sour lemon.
"Where is Rome?" asked Louie.
"A bit further north, in the middle of the country. I am sure those living there like it, but for
me, Rome means chaos and noise. Besides, I am Sicilian; we love the island air."
"Moving to another city must be hard," commented Harry. "But you can be just as happy
there as you are here, right?"
"Of course. That part is up to Sirius. Real estate matters, finding a house, financial crises, or
random philosophical information I need out of nowhere... On those topics, I trust Sirius
blindly." Martina smiled, looking at the children.
“Come on, put on your jackets. Sirius and his friends have taken root waiting. Or do you not
want gelato?”
Harry asked, looking at Louie, a bit confused. “Does Sirius have other friends here besides
us?”
“Brother? Sirius is friends with everyone!”
After putting on their jackets (Martina had dressed Louie), they went down the stairs with
curiosity, and when they emerged onto the sunny, cobblestoned little square, they got the
answer to their questions. Sirius was sitting on the edge of the fountain in the middle of the
square. He was wearing sunglasses, leaning back, enjoying the Italian sun, and he wasn't
alone.
Next to him was Uncle Remus, trying to hold a map in the wind, his nose slightly red from
the sun, and pink-haired Tonks, who was happily eating the ice cream in her hand.
“Surprise!” shouted Tonks when she saw the kids. She raised her ice cream in the air, but the
scoop flew off the cone and fell onto Sirius’s shoulder.
Harry and Louie ran to them laughing; Sirius was swearing in Italian because his top got
dirty, while Remus and Tonks hugged the children in turn, and Martina took out a napkin for
Sirius.
“You can do the thing you do with your wand later when it’s just us,” she said, giggling.
There was a slight stain left on the jacket.
Sirius was used to his niece’s clumsiness, but he said, “I liked this jacket, Dora dear.”
“My name is Tonks,” she said smiling; she hadn't rolled her deep grey eyes. “Martina, what
was that forgetting disease that starts in old age?”
Martina bit her lip not to laugh. “Dementia? Or Alzheimer’s?”
“Hah! That’s the one,” said Tonks, pointing a finger at Sirius. “Poor Sirius is showing early
symptoms. He hasn't been able to learn my name for years.”
Sirius looked at them with his mouth open. Remus was shaking with silent laughter. “This is
insolence!” said Sirius with fake anger. “I am trying to give you a cultural tour here, look at
the treatment I get! Besides, my memory is perfectly fine.”
“Sure, sure...” said Harry, moving to Tonks’s side and supporting her.
Sirius looked at Harry as if betrayed. “You too, Harry?”
As their laughter filled the square, even Sirius grinned and threw his arm around Tonks’s
shoulder. “Let’s get moving, you ungrateful brats. I promised to feed you real Sicilian pizza
before going to Rome.”
After getting the pizzas and devouring the unforgettable delicacy, they came to the beach. It
was crowded; some were walking, some were fishing. There was a nice breeze in the air, it
wasn't cold. Louie was watching the fishermen with interest; Sirius called him and Harry, and
the three of them walked towards the pier.
“The sun will set in an hour,” said Martina. She was giving historical facts to Remus and
Tonks, then her face looked panicked as if she had forgotten something. “Wait for me right
here. I have something very quick to do,” she said, then dashed towards the avenue and
entered a pharmacy across the street.
Remus and Tonks were left a bit confused in front of a beach bar that was inactive due to the
season.
“Look at them,” said Tonks, laughing slightly. Remus turned his head; in the distance, Sirius
was talking about something with the kids and all three seemed to be laughing.
“I love seeing him like this,” said Remus.
“Oh, really?” said Tonks with a mischievous air.
The wolf, accustomed to the scent of menthol and jasmine, caught a new undertone with a
momentary breeze: vanilla. It must be perfume. Remus tried to ignore his heart, which had
done a somersault instantly. “So the wild, scary werewolf is capable of expressing love.”
Remus coughed from surprise. “Ahem—I—I didn't understand?”
Tonks said nothing, but her eyes spoke volumes. Remus averted his eyes from hers and took
a deep breath. The contrast between the bright pink hair and deep grey eyes did not escape
Remus’s notice.
“I didn't imply anything; you understood me clearly,” said Tonks, finally tearing her gaze
away from Remus to look at the beach.
‘She never changes her eye colour,’ a thought echoed faintly in his brain. ‘I love this contrast
too,’ came the second thought. Then he gathered his wits and returned to the present. Tonks
might not be looking at him, but she was an Auror; she was noting everything. Crazy girl.
“I want to live without fear anymore, Dora,” said Remus, his voice weary. He hadn't yet
realized he hadn't addressed her as Tonks. “And I can do that when I’m alone. I mean... it
can't be said that I am completely alone either—”
Tonks turned her gaze to him; a faint, wry smile appeared on Remus’s lips. “—but I don't
have much romantic interaction with my ‘friends’ in the forest, so in that sense, I am alone.”
Tonks giggled, but Remus maintained his seriousness. “Yes, I am admitting this for the first
time: a relationship scares me,” said Remus, avoiding those grey eyes. “To deny this would
be childish. Just because I won't harm you doesn't mean I'm not dangerous.”
“I was already sure you wouldn't harm me, but it's good to hear it from you,” said Tonks. She
reached out and took Remus’s scarred hand. Her fingers were warm. Remus didn't pull his
hand away; his brain couldn't calculate his next move. “And I think my fearlessness is
enough for both of us,” continued Tonks, grasping Remus’s hand tightly. “You have a way
with words, Lupin, but if you think I’m going to let you go, forget it...”
Then she took another step closer, tilted Remus’s chin up slightly, forcing him to look at her.
“I chased this mystery for more than a year. I had pushed everything to the back of my mind,
but I could never erase you... And I’m glad I didn't.”
Remus also involuntarily took a step towards her. The distance between them had closed, and
Tonks’s fresh scent of vanilla and menthol filled his nose. The wolf inside him was pleased
with this scent, wanted this romance, but Remus was resisting. “Where do you get this
courage?” whispered Remus, as if enchanted. “When I don't even have it...”
“What do you mean ‘when I don't even have it’?” Tonks frowned, but her eyes were smiling.
“I don't think so. I don't have a werewolf nose, but I smell something bold and very clear
here: Not indecisive or dull.”
Remus swallowed. His gaze slid from Tonks’s grey eyes to her lips and got stuck there. The
scent of vanilla was sharpening his senses rather than drowning them; he saw there was gloss
on her lips. He swallowed. “Sorry, but I have to confess... I am quite dull when it comes to
romance.”
“Not at all...” said Tonks, her voice now a whisper. “You want to avert your eyes from me
because you are afraid of what you will do if you look.” Tonks noticed Remus’s gaze
wandering over her lips. “And your heart...”
She raised her hand but couldn't touch Remus; she held onto his jacket. “It’s beating as if it’s
going to burst out of your chest, isn't it? But not out of panic that you’ll hurt me. Oh,
Remus...”
Remus seemed breathless. While his logic screamed "stop," his heart and instincts had taken
control.
“Definitely from that,” said Remus in a hoarse voice. It was unclear whether he meant the
panic or the desire, but both were there.
Tonks smiled victoriously. “No, I figured it out, you—” She couldn't finish her sentence.
Because the man who claimed to be "dull" leaned down in a sudden movement and pressed
his lips to hers. This was not a gentle attempt; it was a hungry kiss where years of
suppression, fear, and passion combined. Remus Lupin had taken the first step despite his
fears.
The vanilla was coming from the clear gloss the young woman was wearing. But he wasn't
excited because he had solved a mystery that needed solving.
He felt her hand, smaller and warmer than his own, on his chest; the hand was touching his
shirt, but not to push Remus away. Remus’s hands wrapped around Tonks’s waist. Tonks’s
other hand went to Remus’s neck, then into his dark blond hair. When Tonks parted her lips,
the kiss deepened, then suddenly an exaggerated cough came from behind them.
"Ahem. AHEM!"
Remus and Tonks sprang apart as if electrocuted. Both were breathless, their lips flushed.
Sirius had leaned down slightly, covering Louie’s eyes with his hands.
"You naughty lovebirds!" shouted Sirius with fake horror. "There is a tender mind here! You
have traumatized innocent Louie! Go get a room!"
Harry was laughing at Sirius’s reaction, and Louie was trying to pull Sirius’s fingers off his
face. Finally, he let Louie go, turned to Remus, and grinned. "Finally, Moony! Even I aged
until you two got together."
Both Tonks and Remus were bright red. Then Sirius noticed his girlfriend coming from the
street. "Where was Martina?" Martina had quickened her pace.
"She went into the pharmacy," said Tonks. Just then, the young doctor arrived out of breath
and took two colourful boxes out of her bag. Sirius’s face went slightly pale.
"amore, there are children with us, put them back in your bag." Martina winked, then turned
to the couple whose faces were still red. "Did you get very cold?" she asked, bewildered.
"That’s not from the cold," interrupted Sirius, grinning slyly. "In fact, we can give one of the
packages to Remus. Whichever is the largest size."
Tonks looked like she was about to shoot sparks from her eyes, but Sirius ignored her. The
rest of the cultural tour passed with plenty of laughter but no sharp jokes. Remus, however,
was sure Sirius was waiting for the right moment. They ate pizza in the evening just as they
had at lunch, and when the children got tired, they went home.
It happened exactly as Remus expected. Louie had just fallen asleep, and Harry was brushing
his teeth. Neither Tonks nor Remus objected to the sleeping arrangement Martina had set up
beforehand. Remus and the two children were to sleep in the living room. When Sirius
brought the subject to innuendos at one point, Martina threatened to make him sleep on the
balcony with a half-joking, half-serious expression.
Their other Italian flatmate, Letizia, had said she wouldn't mind Tonks sleeping on an air
mattress in the same room with her. The only problem was that she wondered how Tonks
dyed her hair so often and seamlessly. "All your hair strands are soft and smell wonderful,"
said Letizia admiringly.
Tonks, who was well-versed in Muggle terms, managed to skillfully get out of the issue by
saying "My hairdresser knows his stuff," without prolonging the subject.
The next morning at six-thirty, the whole apartment woke up with noise and fear. It was a
small earthquake, but Louie was crying uncontrollably. Sirius had taken him into his arms but
wasn't very successful in stopping the child, whose voice was trembling. Letizia was
panicked too. Saying she couldn't stay in the house any longer, she left almost running.
Remus relaxed when the Muggle panic in the house started to disperse.
"I’d better take Harry and Louie home," he said. Tonks looked like she might go too, but
since the next day was a holiday and she couldn't resist Sirius’s insistence, she lay back
down. During the day they visited museums and galleries, and there were no other tremors.
Meanwhile, Remus appeared through the fireplace with Louie and Harry, giving Lily, who
was preparing morning coffee, a mini heart attack.
"Mum, do you know, there was an earthquake and Louie got scared," said Harry. Any other
time he would have teased Louie, but he had been scared too. Remus smiled at both children.
"Harry acted bravely, it was already a small tremor. However, Sirius couldn't calm your little
one down," he added, while Lily was hugging her son.
"It’s okay, Louie," murmured Lily. "Do you want to go upstairs and wake your dad?" Louie
nodded and shot up the stairs like a rocket. Harry also headed to his own room, yawning.
"How are you?" asked Lily, narrowing her eyes at Remus. "Going abroad seems to have done
you good."
Remus’s ears turned red. "Something happened," he said, and the two moved to the kitchen
table and took their coffees. When Remus recounted everything that happened yesterday
afternoon, Lily could have truly squealed like a teenage girl.
"That is wonderful news," said Lily. "I was sure you were interested in her, but I wasn't close
enough to Tonks to realize it was mutual." It was very true; Tonks and Lily had only met
twice. Remus smiled.
“It’s wonderful, yes, but what if I mess it up?” he said in a hushed voice. “If she can't be
happy with me and we break up because of that—”
“If you break up, that’s normal,” said Lily. “But I fear your worry goes beyond just not
making her happy. Come on, tell me.”
So Remus explained: How werewolves were still not looked upon favourably, how his secret
couldn't remain hidden, his inability to find decent employment for years, and the blatant
hatred he encountered from time to time. “Let alone marriage, even her seeing me won't be
welcomed by some,” said Remus.
“Wait a second, you kissed yesterday and you’re already thinking of proposing?”
“I was giving an example, Lily,” said Remus, shaking his head. “Just an example.”
Lily grinned. “You know best. If you say she’s the woman of your life, then there’s no
problem.”
Remus blushed again. Lily continued: “Regarding being welcomed, let me add this: Tonks is
an adult capable of owning her decisions, and an Auror. You are underestimating what she’s
like when provoked. No one with a brain would cross Tonks.”
-----
When May arrived, first Iris, and then seventeen days later Louie, celebrated their birthdays.
And when the calendar turned to June, Remus blew out his birthday cake with his girlfriend
by his side. The energy of the new romantic couple could be felt from five kilometres away.
Sirius and Martina hadn't been able to attend these three happy celebrations; they were busy
moving into their new home in Rome.
Lily made a suggestion to Sirius: he could consider selling his family home. She and Severus
went to the house at Grimmauld Place. The house could be cleaned, but the general condition
was terrible. As Sirius’s mother’s portrait rained insults down on Lily, the pair quickly exited.
“That is exactly why it is impossible to sell that house,” Sirius said with a strange sadness
while they were chatting through the fireplace. “Or the ground floor would need to be
completely demolished. I’m not sure, I don't want to deal with it right now,” he had said.
Then he told Lily about Rome and the big city chaos.
“It’s not like living in central London, Lily. This place is truly a metropolis. I understand
Martina better now.” It wasn't without its upsides, and they loved sightseeing and exploring.
Martina also loved her new job: cancer researches. They ended the conversation with the
matter of the ring.
“Severus is going on a reconnaissance mission with Dumbledore tonight, and they plan to
destroy it tonight,” said Lily. Sirius nodded.
“Then I don't need to ask if you’ve taken precautions?” asked Sirius with a serious expression
on his face. These ‘objects’, which had cost his brother Regulus his life, and the others, were
a key issue for Sirius too.
“We have,” said Lily. “Still, I am worried—”
“Don't worry. There won't be any problems. At most, it will turn out that this ring isn't cursed,
in which case Dumbledore and your husband will leave there in one piece.” After saying
goodnight to each other, they disconnected from the fireplace.
Severus came downstairs half an hour later. “I think Iris understands.... I don't know how...
she didn't want to let me go, and every time I put her down she woke up again. Then I’d put
her to sleep again and the same cycle just kept repeating.”
Lily smiled tenderly. “Little children are truly mysterious.” Then Severus hugged her and
kissed Lily. It wasn't a hungry kiss, but the emotion was palpable. Then Severus pulled away.
“I actually don't want to go. But there is a voice inside me that won't shut up: a contrarian
voice saying, ‘If I leave this senile old man alone, he’ll surely get himself killed, and then all
the work will be left to me.’”
Lily couldn't help but laugh. When the clock struck 11:00 PM, Severus stepped into the
fireplace and, with one last look at Lily, traveled to Hogwarts.
The sky over Little Hangleton was inky black. Two silhouettes climbing toward the ruins on
the hill were almost invisible in the darkness of the night. Albus Dumbledore was leading the
way. His aged body moved through the wild weeds and thorny vines with surprising agility.
Behind him, Severus Snape held his wand at the ready, scanning his surroundings with sharp
eyes.
“Are you sure, Albus?” asked Severus, his voice mingling with the howling wind. “This
place is no different from an abandoned pigsty.”
“Appearances can be deceiving, Severus,” said Dumbledore, not even out of breath.
“Voldemort feels a sickly attachment to his roots, especially those he deems ‘noble’. His
mother’s family home is a temple to him.”
When they reached the dilapidated shack, Severus sniffed the air. There was the smell of
damp, rotting wood and... very old, very malevolent magic. “The defenses are strong,” said
Severus, pointing his wand at the rotting doorframe. “Likely several curses.”
“We shall manage,” said Dumbledore, waving his wand gently. An invisible barrier shattered
with a sound like breaking glass.
When they stepped inside, they both saw that metallic glint coming from beneath the
floorboards. A gold box. And inside it, that crude ring with a cracked black stone set into it.
Dumbledore's breath hitched.
“Oh...” whispered the old wizard. His eyes filled with a strange, sorrowful glint. He lowered
his wand. His hand reached toward the box as if moving of its own volition. “Could it be
possible?”
Severus saw that expression on Dumbledore's face. This was not the expression of "disgust" a
wizard would wear while looking at a Horcrux. This was an expression of longing.
“Albus, don't touch it!” Severus warned sharply. But Dumbledore seemed not to hear him. “I
can see them... I can apologize...” His fingers were trembling, mere inches from the ring.
Severus realized the danger. This wasn't just a Horcrux. This was a trap. And Dumbledore,
despite his renowned intellect, was walking into that trap like a defenseless child. Severus
didn't think. He flicked his wand. The ring rose from the box and hung suspended in the air,
but there was an invisible shield between it and Dumbledore. Severus had deemed this
solution more appropriate than flinging the old man across the room. Dumbledore suddenly
realized what he was doing and came to his senses.
“Ah, Severus, what have I done?”
“Nothing. Whatever was going to happen, I prevented it quickly. We cannot touch this; this is
definitely the darkest Horcrux we have encountered so far.”
Dumbledore knew he was right. Severus had taken care of three Horcruxes before, and this
fourth one was aware of it.
Dumbledore was looking at the ring in the air. “This must be another piece of the mystery I
have been working on. Look at fate, Severus. Let us return to the office, I will explain
everything.”
“We need to destroy this right now, Albus,” said Severus, shaking his head. The need to
focus on the mission outweighed everything else. Dumbledore could tell him Tales from the
Thousand and One Nights later if he wished.
Dumbledore sighed deeply. He tore his eyes away from that black stone with difficulty and
looked at Severus. The usual twinkle in those blue eyes had extinguished; it was replaced by
the sorrow of a tired, old man. “You are right, Severus,” said Dumbledore, his voice hoarse.
“Taking this to the castle... would be trusting my willpower too much.”
“So how will we destroy it?” asked Severus, without lowering his wand. “Standard spells
won't work on this. And we don't have a substance as powerful as Basilisk venom.”
Dumbledore nodded solemnly. “There is only one way. A dangerous, wild, but definitive
way.”
Severus’s eyes narrowed. “Fiendfyre.”
“I'm afraid so,” said Dumbledore. “Normally, I wouldn't attempt this in such a confined
space, amidst so much wooden material. But you are here. You can help me control the fire.”
Severus would do this willingly. This was among the counter-spells he and Lily had
practiced. "If you lose control, I will burn you along with the shack, Albus, just so you
know."
"A fair deal," said Dumbledore, smiling slightly. Both wizards pointed their wands at the
ring. Dumbledore whispered, making a complex wand movement. What poured from his
wand was no ordinary fire; they were living, roaring, shapeshifting, magma-coloured
monsters. A serpent and a dragon made of fire lunged at the ring.
The heat in the room instantly rose to hellish levels. Fortunately, both wizards were ready.
The ring remained suspended in the air amidst that terrible heat. And then, a deafening
scream filled the shack. This was no human voice; it was the collective wail of metal, magic,
and a fragment of soul being torn apart.
Black smoke billowed from within the flames. For a brief moment, the smoke morphed into a
writhing, agonized face—Voldemort’s face—before being devoured by the beasts of the
Fiendfyre and vanishing. Severus and Dumbledore cast the counter-curse simultaneously. The
roaring flames were extinguished in an instant, as if sucked away. Only smoke and the smell
of burning remained. The ring fell to the floor; it was now just an old, blackened piece of
gold. The stone set in it was cracked down the middle.
"It is done," said Dumbledore. "One more piece gone." He tore his eyes away from the ring
and looked at Severus.
“Voldemort was obsessed with splitting his soul into seven parts, but he never achieved it.
There are six, Severus. Four have been destroyed.”
“The Diadem is at Hogwarts. What could the other object be, is that at Hogwarts too?” asked
Severus.
Dumbledore shuddered suddenly. “I have a theory... But now is not the time. Besides, I am
sure I am mistaken.” A smile appeared on his face. “Does that stone mean anything to you?”
Severus looked at the cracked stone. Even though it was safe to hold the ring now, Severus
summoned the stone into his palm. “I have never seen anything like it. And it certainly
doesn't feel cursed.”
“Let us return to the office. I will explain.” It was well past midnight when they returned to
Hogwarts, to the Headmaster’s office.
Dumbledore went to his desk and, with trembling hands, pulled an old, worn book from his
drawer: The Tales of Beedle the Bard. Severus placed the cracked stone on the desk, in front
of Dumbledore. "Children's tales, Albus? Are you serious?"
"Tales are the safest disguises for the purest form of the truth, Severus," said Dumbledore,
gently opening the book. He found The Tale of the Three Brothers and touched the symbol at
the top of the page with his finger: A triangle, a circle, and a line. "You recognize this mark,
do you not? Grindelwald's mark."
"The Deathly Hallows," said Severus, his voice thick with weariness. "The Invisibility Cloak,
the Elder Wand, and..." His eyes slid to the stone on the desk. He paused. The mocking
expression on his face froze. "...The Resurrection Stone."
Dumbledore gave a sad nod. "Cadmus Peverell's stone. For centuries it was kept in the
possession of the Gaunt family merely as a 'family heirloom'. Even Voldemort did not
understand what it was; he saw it only as a display of power."
Dumbledore’s voice cracked. "But I—I understood what it was."
Severus looked at Dumbledore in disbelief. "So, you are telling me that the thing you tried to
sacrifice your life and sanity for in that shack is a fairytale object believed to bring back the
dead?"
"To see my loved ones," said Dumbledore, his glasses misting over. "To apologize. To my
mother... my father... and my sister, Ariana."
Severus fell into a deep silence. It shook him to see the regretful old man lying beneath
Dumbledore's mask of the "all-knowing sage."
On the other hand, he could understand the old man. Yet Severus was a logical and analytical
man; he could compensate for his past regrets through the living.
"The stone is broken, Albus," said Severus, his voice softer this time. "It won't work
anymore."
"Perhaps that is for the best," said Dumbledore, without looking at the stone. "The dead
should stay where they belong." He fixed his eyes on Severus. Severus asked another
question to keep the subject from straying from the Horcruxes.
"The Diadem," said Severus, changing the subject. "You said it was at Hogwarts. Where?"
"I suspect it is in the Room of Requirement," said Dumbledore, returning to his old
businesslike demeanor. "Voldemort was arrogant enough to believe he knew the school better
than anyone. Yet he was not the only one to discover that room. But finding the Diadem is
like looking for a needle in that colossal pile where everything is hidden."
Severus stood up. "Then we had better start searching. But first..." Severus looked at the
broken stone on the desk one last time.
"Hide that thing, Albus. Or better yet, throw it away. I do not want to see you in that state
again. My job is to fight the Dark Lord, not your depression."
Dumbledore smiled slightly. "Goodnight, Severus. And... thank you."
Severus did not answer; he simply bowed his head and headed for the fireplace. There was a
single thought in his mind: To go home, to return to real life. Fairytales and the dead could
wait.

July was passing more slowly but beautifully. Severus and Lily had used their days off
together again and, taking all three children, went on holiday to Andalusia for the first time.
The idea of visiting Sirius in Rome was also tempting Lily, but when Sirius expressed that

Lily laughed out loud. Severus pursed his lips. “Two drunk Muggles, and it’s not even 2:00

The family, not expecting such a welcome, looked at Molly in astonishment. Severus was

Meanwhile, the adults were in a heated discussion in the living room. Lily was sitting up

Severus said no more. It was impossible anyway; for Peter to reach Harry, he would have to
step over the corpses of both of them, as well as Sirius and Remus.
When they returned to Spinner’s End the next day, Lily prepared their favourite dessert—

Moreover, when Harry asked for permission to ride his broom in the afternoon, Lily had told

Louie didn't have an independent life anyway, and Iris had only just started verbalizing her

“It’s unfair!” he shouted, while going to the bathroom to brush his teeth. “As if I gave them
the keys and said ‘get the hell out of prison’! It’s always me who suffers.” He slammed the

Lily didn't get angry at this outburst, but as the day progressed, Harry’s constant huffing and

his room. Fortunately, the door wasn't locked.
Severus returned from the Ministry in the evening. He seemed on edge. He signaled to Lily to
come to the kitchen. “I researched the legal loopholes that would keep you out of prison if
you killed Peter.”
Lily rolled her eyes. “And who looked after things while you were doing your little

“You know that blonde assistant, Damira? I passed them to her. She works like a bee; I adore
her.”
“So, what would save me?” Severus frowned.
“Theoretically, nothing. Even your own life being in danger doesn't mitigate the rules,
meaning self-defense is invalid. But I found a loophole. Only if Harry is in real danger could
that exonerate your murder.”
“Can you elaborate on ‘danger’?”
“There was a case about this in 1952.” Severus grimaced. “A father catching an incestuous
act. He kills his nephew while his daughter is being raped, and yet he cannot avoid prison.
But in another case, 1949, a young wizard who killed his father for abusing his Muggle wife
was arrested and acquitted. His mother’s injuries were severe.”
“I cannot wait for ten of Harry’s bones to be broken to stop Peter,” said Lily.
Harry’s moodiness continued on his birthday too. Lily, who had planned a small family party

“I don't want to celebrate this year. You eat the cake.” Then he sat at the piano and started
playing sorrowful notes. Even Louie couldn't cheer him up.
By evening, he had rebuffed all his parents' efforts to communicate with him. He had also
refused to blow out the candles. Harry only came to his father before going to bed and
handed him a brochure. Severus was looking at a swimming competition invitation.

first even though I trained for weeks,” he said, shrugging. “I never thought I’d miss the day I

Even minutes after Harry went to his room, Severus was still looking at the brochure,
thinking. He didn't want to fall for this emotional blackmail, but his son's words had pierced
his armour. He was thinking about how their bond would be shaken if he went to Hogwarts

Lily left the untouched cake in her hand on the kitchen counter and came to her husband’s

Swimming Championship—Crystal Palace National Sports Centre." The date was the
following weekend. 9 days later.
"He was getting up at the crack of dawn for months for this," whispered Lily. "He even drank
those disgusting vegetable juices just to improve his breathing."
"I know," said Severus sharply, but his anger was not at Harry, but at the situation. "But it’s a
war zone out there, Lily. With Lucius and Bellatrix and the others on the loose, I cannot

"This is Muggle London, Sev," said Lily, her voice starting to come out a bit louder than a
whisper. She was reasoning. “Death Eaters couldn't even find this place on a map.”
"We cannot take the risk."
“Then we take precautions, Sev. Harry wants to achieve something next week, just as Harry,
with his own effort. Even if he comes third again, it will be his achievement. If we take this
away from him, we will have killed his spirit."
He looked into Lily's green eyes. In those eyes, he saw Harry's disappointment from
moments ago. And Harry was right. Even losing a fair race was more honourable than
winning while imprisoned at home.
“I have conditions,” he said finally, his voice sharpened like a knife.
Lily smiled as if she had won a victory, but it was a bittersweet smile. “I’m listening.”

Harry had woken up that morning with his heart pounding in his throat, and the feeling just
wouldn't go away. It felt as though his breakfast wouldn't go down from his throat to his
stomach, as if he couldn't swallow, but in reality, he had no trouble eating. In fact, he had
finished his plate with an appetite.
“Harry,” his mother said in a soft whisper. “Not winning or not finishing the race isn't the end
of the world, you know that, right?”
Of course he knew. He understood now. Ever since a group of murderous lunatics had
escaped from prison, every time his eyes caught the scar in the mirror, he knew that "the end
of the world" had other meanings.
“I’m fine, Mum,” he said with a firm voice. He cleared his throat. He couldn't be having heart
palpitations, could he?
All five of them got into the car. During the competition, Iris and Louie were going to visit a
nearby funfair with Aunt Petunia and her family. His Aunt Petunia was a bit distant towards
Harry, but Iris and Louie loved her. Moreover, last November, Dudley had gotten a baby
sister, Carrie. As much as Harry felt reserved towards Dudley's family, Louie loved them that
much.
His father didn't believe the little ones were targets, but a woman named Bella was apparently
obsessed with his mother. She might target Aunt Petunia just to upset her, but the funfair was
safe. All crowded areas where Muggles were in the majority were considered safe, partly due
to the police presence. Harry’s logic was completely against this situation. Police would
likely never stop someone like Bella. But he didn't want to voice this and frighten his mother
even more.
About forty minutes later they arrived at the swimming pool, and Harry noticed a few of his
friends. He signaled 'see you later' to them from afar and headed to the changing rooms with
his family.
“No way, are you coming into the cubicle too?” he said when both of them entered. Inside
was spacious, perhaps 50 changing cubicles and a tunnel connecting directly to the pool.
There were actually lots of families around, not just fathers but mothers too.
“We aren't going into the cubicle, obviously, you silly thing. Unless you want me in there,
that is.”
“No, Mum, I can pull my pants down myself.”
They found an empty cubicle and only Harry went in. Severus and Lily remained silent,
watching the other excited youths and panicking parents. Harry came out of the cubicle in a

He handed his glasses to his father and took his swimming goggles in return. First, he put the
swimming cap on his head, then the goggles over the cap.
“My goal is to be first, of course, but I won't be upset if I’m not,” he said, looking at his

strongly again, but this time it had descended to where it was supposed to be, his chest.

He couldn't make out anything; there was just a colourful sea of crowds.
The referee’s whistle echoed. "Swimmers, take your marks!"
Harry climbed onto the platform. His toes gripped the textured surface. His heart was beating

It was as if his rapid heartbeat had ceased. Because underwater was considered his safe space.

Last 20 metres. 10. 5.
Harry put all the power inside him into his arms with a final burst. When his hand hit the hard

Potter, 1.
He had come first. He was first. He had won.
