The killing curse Lily sent to that exact spot pierced the empty air as they vanished and
shattered the mirrors behind them.
Lily collapsed to her knees beside Harry. With trembling hands, she cupped her son's wet
face, grabbed his shoulders, and shook him. "Harry? Harry? Open your eyes! Harry!" She
rested her head on his chest to hear a heartbeat, but there was only silence. If her first cry had
been bad, this was far worse.
Severus slowly sank to his knees, as if in a dream. His hand went to Harry’s still-warm
forehead, to his messy hair. "He’s gone," whispered Severus. His voice sounded foreign even
to his own ears. "No, it can't be, no."
Lily turned, straightened up, and grabbed her husband’s collar. She was shaking him, but
Severus was like a rag doll. Limp and unresponsive.
"Listen to me! No, look at me!" screamed Lily, her voice cracking. Severus turned his eyes to
her. "You must have a potion! There has to be an antidote in your bag! Severus, you can fix
everything."
Severus slowly shook his head. "There is no antidote for death, Lily," he said. His voice was
so hollow it was as if someone else had spoken. "Avada Kedavra... there is no return from
that curse."
Lily was sobbing. "No... No, he is too little..." She was babbling, her mind shattered by
shock. "He’ll get cold... Severus, let's take him home, he’ll get cold..."
Severus reached out a trembling hand and touched Harry's shoulder, which was beginning to
cool. A volcano was erupting inside him, but only icy silence was reflected on the outside. I
didn't protect him, echoed in his mind. I wasn't enough. He gently lifted Harry’s head; he
wanted to pull him into his lap, rest him against his chest, hug him one last time. Lily was
clinging to Harry’s other side. A tragedy of three on the cold tiles.
Just at the moment when hope was lost, a violent shudder passed through Harry’s body.
Severus felt it first. A tremor like a spasm, like an electric shock. Lily lifted her head, her
eyes filled with fear and hope. "Severus?"
Then came that sound. That first breath, deep, raspy, taken as if to burst the lungs. Harry
coughed, blinked, and let out a louder wheeze. He opened his eyes and wanted to sit up, but
two adults—his mother and father—had engulfed him, kissing and hugging him tight.
Lily and Severus noticed one more thing. The lightning bolt scar on his forehead split open,
and a pitch-black smoke, like tar mixed with blood, seeped out and mingled with the air. It
was as if a dark soul had been ripped out of him. Harry could breathe now; the coughing
stopped.
“What happened to me—ah.” Both his cheeks were kissed somewhat roughly, and his
father’s arms gripped him tighter. Harry felt cold; when had he decided to move to the tiles
and go to sleep? Even worse, had he fainted?
"Dad?" he asked in a hoarse voice, bringing his hand to his throat. "Why... did I faint?"
This time, it was his mother's turn to cling to him like a limpet. Harry felt a pang in his heart
when he realized they were both crying. Whatever had happened was over, he felt fine now,
and he didn't want them to be upset.
"Just... a bad accident," Severus finally said, his voice still cracking. He lifted Harry gently
from the ground, as if he were made of porcelain. "But it's over. It's all over." He shook his
head and reached for Harry’s forehead with trembling fingers. The scar was no longer red and
swollen. Only a thin, white line remained.
He thought about the pitch-black smoke like tar. The thought that crossed his mind made his
hackles rise. Could it be?
Lily sniffled and stood up, summoned Harry's towel from where he had hung it before all this
happened, and draped it over her trembling son's shoulders.
"We are going home," Lily said firmly. Her eyes were still teary, but her voice was in that
tone that brooked no argument. "Right now."
Severus’s mind, however, was occupied with the dark magic he had seen. If what he was
thinking had really happened, they needed to meet with Dumbledore urgently, but his priority
was Lily.
Harry quickly took off his swimsuit and put on his clothes. The crowd was still by the pool;
music could be heard. He put on his glasses, and his blurry vision instantly corrected.
“Love, can we lift the confusion spell now?” he asked Lily, and after Severus nodded his
approval, they lifted it. Meanwhile, the three of them were already heading towards the car.
Harry only came to his senses when he was about to sit in the car. He adjusted his glasses and
frowned. “Dad... that man was Peter, wasn't he?”
Severus didn't answer the question; he hugged Harry as if wanting to take him under his
wings. "No questions, darling. It's passed now. They can never harm you again." Then,
minding his head, he helped Harry into the vehicle.
While Severus was closing the door, Lily cast a Patronus Charm in broad daylight; even
though Severus was surprised, he didn't twitch a muscle because Lily wasn't someone who
would do magic in Muggle areas.
“Who did you send it to?” he asked, even though he knew the answer very well. It had to be
Petunia because their other two children were with her.
“To Petunia,” Lily confirmed. Then she took a breath. “Now we are going to do something I
never wanted to do, Severus... We are going to Privet Drive.”
Severus was surprised by his wife’s demeanor again. “Blood wards, yes, wise.” He moved to
the passenger seat, and Lily sat at the wheel. “Going home would have been good too.”
“We will be safe at my sister’s too. About the house... Sev,” she started the engine and turned
to her husband. “I don't think we should go home,” she said. She couldn't say ‘home doesn't
feel safe’ in front of Harry.
“Do you have a plan for afterwards? We will need to meet with Dumbledore too,” said
Severus, changing the subject. Meanwhile, Lily drove the car onto the avenue; traffic was
heavy. “I’m sure the Dursleys have a fireplace, but it isn't connected to the network.”
“We can Apparate or—” then her personality changed 180 degrees. “Damn you, you fucking
Ford! Give way!”
Both passengers flinched. Lily honked the horn as if to blast it and made a very rude gesture
at the driver in the adjacent car. “Take this and shove it, okay?”
“LILY! Do you want me to drive?” Severus was in shock. What was happening to his wife?
“I DON'T. Shut up, or fuck you too, Sev.” Severus fell silent when he got his share of this
anger.
“Mum...” mumbled Harry. Severus turned his head back. “Sorry, darling. We are a bit on
edge, but it’s not because of you.” We are just experiencing things we can't control, that's all.
We are fine.
“It’s okay, Harry, yes, we are just stressed,” said Lily, not taking her eyes off the road. They
had finally entered the motorway. “It will all pass. I promise.” She seemed to be speaking
more to herself.
They arrived at the house at the same time as Vernon’s vehicle. Petunia ran to them with
Carrie in her arms and holding Iris by the hand; Dudley and Vernon were behind them. Louie
also ran and hugged his father.
“Auntie said it was an emergency,” said Louie. His mother took Iris into her arms. “But we
still had fun today.” He saw the medal on Harry’s t-shirt and his pale face. “You won!”
Harry hugged his brother without a word. He had thought he would have a somewhat normal
day, but instead, he had been attacked at the pool and had fainted. And his parents were
acting very strangely.
Then, something even stranger happened: his aunt hugged him.
As they entered the house, Petunia handed little Carrie to Vernon, while Dudley and Louie
were telling Lily about the roller coasters. Severus checked the fireplace under the pretext of
examining the new wallpaper. The fireplace really wasn't connected to the network, but they
could Apparate. To home or to Hogsmeade, that was the biggest question.
Although Lily appeared more relaxed on the surface, Severus realized she wasn't letting
Harry out of her sight. Louie could play games with Dudley, but Lily offered to solve a
puzzle with Harry. They ate dinner with minimal conversation; the two Muggles were in no
rush to hear the answers to their questions. Most likely because they weren't ready for them.
While Petunia and Vernon were busy with their own children, two Patronuses arrived in
quick succession. Arthur was frantic about their whereabouts; according to him, the Ministry
was in chaos. The Department of Magical Law Enforcement, in particular, was being
pressured again regarding their ‘former Death Eater colleague Snape’. Lily sighed; it meant
the undercover Death Eaters were indeed still influential there and were stirring up trouble.
The second one had come from Dumbledore, telling them to leave Spinner’s End. Lily and
Severus looked at each other. Furthermore, they could only come to the Hog's Head after
midnight.
“Peter and Bellatrix left their work unfinished, of course,” said Severus with a grim smirk.
“Now it’s our turn.”
Lily took a deep breath. “I don't know, Sev. Until we reached the car, my mind was set on
home. But then something bothered me.” She looked around, at the walls. It was a nice
house, but grumpy and oppressive. Yet, it was safe.
“It’s either Hogwarts or here,” said Lily.
“Should we warn Sirius?” asked Severus.
Lily shook her head. “He’ll find out tonight anyway. Or tomorrow.”
They fell silent again. Louie and Iris were exhausted from the day at the funfair. While Lily
gave warm milk to Harry and Louie, Iris was talking to her father. There were no spare rooms
at the Dursleys', but Lily and Severus weren't planning on sleeping anyway; they would settle
the two younger children in the living room.
“I miss Piggy. He won't have nightmares without me, will he?” said Iris.
“I am sure he won't. Piggy is a brave little pig,” answered Severus. Then Iris closed her eyes
and fell asleep quickly.
The other person resisting sleep was Harry. His father and mother couldn't bring themselves
to be angry with him tonight.
-----
When the clock struck 2 AM, word came from Dumbledore that it was now safe to Apparate
to Hogsmeade. Lily wrote a note to her sister and left it on the kitchen table, so she would
read it and not panic if they weren't there in the morning.
With Harry, they arrived in Hogsmeade, where the chill of the August night cut through their
thin jackets. Severus drew his wand, and Lily immediately stepped in front of Harry.
“This way,” said Severus, his voice barely above a whisper. They entered that familiar,
dilapidated pub with a severed boar’s head on its shabby wooden sign. The interior smelled
heavily of stale beer and sharp goats.
Aberforth Dumbledore was behind the bar; he didn't even look surprised to see the group
enter. His blue eyes—identical to Albus’s—slid first to Harry, then to Severus.
“So he made it out alive,” said Aberforth in his gravelly voice. “Well done. You’re a tough
lad. Albus is upstairs, pacing back and forth. He’s worn a hole in the floor.”
“Is it safe?” asked Lily, placing her hand on Harry's shoulder.
“Nowhere is safe except here, girl,” said Aberforth, pulling a bottle from under the bar.
“Firewhisky?” They nodded.
Before they could even sit down, familiar footsteps were heard coming from the back stairs.
Albus Dumbledore had come down; this time the cheerful twinkle was absent from his eyes.
Instead, there was pure, scientific curiosity and deep concern.
“Harry,” said Dumbledore. His voice filled the room. “Lily, Severus. What happened?”
Harry started; it only then occurred to him that his parents hadn't told him what had
happened. His parents froze as well and looked at each other.
“I was alone in the changing room,” Harry began. “My memory is still very blurry. But I
recognized a man: that crazy Peter who escaped from Azkaban. If he fell down the stairs right
here, I’d give him a kick too; a pathetic sort. I also remember his high-pitched voice like a
rat. Then I must have fainted and my mum and dad got me out. That’s the story.”
Then he turned to his parents. “You are afraid Peter will come to the house, but I think I wish
he would. Is he the dangerous one?” He pouted and looked at the old professor. Dumbledore
was smiling at him. Harry smiled back.
“Harry... Your memory must still be very fragile and maybe the trauma is making you
forget,” his mother began. “However, Peter is not someone to be underestimated at all. Nor is
he foolish enough to come and attack alone. Do you remember the other woman?”
“I don't remember,” said Harry honestly. Now he felt a bit bad for not remembering.
“Harry, you didn't just faint,” said Severus. Saying this seemed to cause him pain. “We
thought we lost you, and for a short while, the world stopped turning for us. And when you
came to, we tried not to project that onto you,” he finished.
But Harry’s eyebrows shot up. “I understand, but it still feels like you are hiding something
from me?”
“Harry, in the wizarding world, there are three malevolent curses with heavy penalties. One
of these is the killing curse; it instantly severs the person's connection to life and has no
return. You survived this curse once, when you were just one year old.”
“Yes, the dark wizard Voldemort. A total nutcase.”
“Both a nutcase and a genius,” added Dumbledore. “What an interesting concept, isn't it? He
is expected to be very successful and has a bright future, but the person makes the wrong
choices.”
“A pure, unadulterated idiot then,” said Harry sharply. Severus was always proud of Harry
for speaking his mind, but tonight it hit the ceiling. Harry and Dumbledore had never
communicated with such mutual, clear dialogue before.
“The gist of it is this, Harry,” continued Severus. “You deflected that same curse in the pool
today. Or rather—” he sighed. “You actually died, Harry. You were truly dead, for a few
minutes.”
“What? How is that possible?”
“There are a few theories,” said Dumbledore thoughtfully. “Of course there are. Dark details I
didn't think were probable, that I didn't even want to bring to mind.”
“What do you mean? Could Harry really have been a Horcrux?”
“Yes, Severus. Although I never wanted to accept this possibility, it was in the drafts. Harry
was a Horcrux he never intended to create. A piece of him latched onto Harry that night. He
didn't choose you, for example, Lily. I thought about this a lot. A more adult body, not a baby.
Also a competent witch. Why it was Harry, I cannot answer that.”
“Just help me understand this and it will be enough, Professor,” said Lily, finding a new
energy and adopting a more vivid attitude. But it was an energy Severus didn't recognize. “If
what happened today hadn't happened, and even after we secured the Diadem, we would still
be searching for his pieces—his objects, right?” she asked.
“Even with Harry by our side, would we still be floundering in this impasse?” she added.
Sadness passed through Dumbledore’s eyes. “No. Instead of hiding this from you and if
necessary defeating Harry myself, or worse, allowing him to find himself in danger? No,
Lily.
“I had risked turning you against me, becoming enemies. Of course, I was going to tell you
both that one day you would need to kill your only son, that you would need to put him
forward like a pawn in these unpleasant scenarios. It is hard to continue living by ignoring
the facts that are plainly visible, Lily. Believe me, I know best.”
“I know now, Professor,” said Lily in a voice as hard as ice. “Then let tonight be our new
beginning, because for Harry and for us, it certainly is. From now on, Harry is not a soldier of
the Order, a pawn on your chessboard, or a sacrificial lamb. He is just a child. And we are
just his parents.
“And you will help us. At the Ministry, in the press, at school... Merlin knows we will need it.
However... do not ever dare to gamble with my son's life again. Because then, you will find
me against you, not dark wizards.”
Harry and Severus had listened, holding their breath. And now, neither mystery nor trust
remained.
Human relationships sometimes worked in reverse. Just like in school corridors... It was
naive to cram hundreds of young people into a building and expect them not to compete or
hold grudges against each other. In that environment, there were mostly those "unattainable"
and sharp personalities you admired from afar, found convincing, or even envied. At first,
they are striking and dizzying.
But as you get closer, as you really get to know them, the spell breaks. As the masks fall, the
chasms between become visible. Admiration gives way to disappointment, and eventually,
those bonds sever.
Right now, they were exactly on the last page of that story. School was out. They were in that
place where all masks had fallen, trust was gone, and only naked truths remained.
-----
EPILOGUE (end of Book 1)
AUGUST 29, 1991
The biting frost of the early morning cut through the mist over the river like a knife. The
massive stag descending to the water’s edge was not skittish. In his movements held not the
panic of prey, but the wary self-assurance of a hunter. His hide had turned into a map of scars
from the passing years: a wolf’s claw mark on his left flank, faint burns on his neck...
The stag lowered his head to the icy water. The moment his tongue touched the frigid water, a
lightning bolt flashed in his mind. Cold. Water. Bubbling cauldron. Grey smoke. Flames. His
mind jolted as if waking from that hazy, instinctual slumber that had lasted ten years. The
stag’s pupils dilated. Animalistic urges (flee, hide, eat) were replaced by something much
more complex and painful: Guilt.
The stag’s hind legs trembled. Bones shifted with a terrible crack, as if shaking off the rust of
a decade. Fur receded, hooves turned into fingers. Seconds later, on the muddy bank of the
river, a naked man with matted hair and beard, his ribs visible, collapsed onto his knees.
James Potter took a trembling breath. His lungs were burning. He looked at his hands. His
fingernails were overgrown and packed with dirt. The muscles in his arms possessed the
hardness of a wild animal rather than the softness of a wizard.
"I’m sorry, Lils," he whispered, his voice coming out raspy like a rusty hinge unused for ten
years. He tried to stand, but his head spun. "Just... just to fix—" His eye caught a piece of
newspaper fluttering in the wind at the base of the rocks nearby, likely left by campers who
had passed through the previous week.
He was cold. Not just because he was naked, but because of a bad feeling inside him. He had
to check the house too, but he didn't know where he was right now. He picked up the wet,
muddy paper from the ground with trembling fingers. His eyes searched not for the headlines,
but only for the date. He squinted to clear his vision.
His eyes focused on the top right corner of the page. James held his breath.
THE GUARDIAN AUGUST 25, 1991
James blinked. He shook his head. "No," he murmured. James Potter’s knees gave way in the
middle of the desolation as he clutched the newspaper in his hand.
He wanted to return home, but how? Was everything as he had left it? He didn't know. But he
was returning. In his round hazel eyes, there was not the bewilderment of a human, but the
dangerous determination of a leader returning to his pack.
END OF BOOK 1
BOOK 2: Retrograde: A Solar Return
12 years ago today: August 29, 1979
James Potter, having woken up to an exceedingly normal morning on an exceedingly
normal summer day, returned to bed upon realizing it was earlier than his usual waking
hour.
What roused James from his bed, after he had managed to fall back asleep, was the
sound of knocking on the door downstairs. The Muggle watch on his wrist showed that
only forty minutes had passed. He stretched and reached for his glasses. Hoping his
perfectly normal day wouldn't change, he lazily padded downstairs; the person at the
door seemed intent on breaking it down. Grumbling to himself as he opened it, he saw
Remus, Remus's parents, and Lily standing outside. He flushed to the tips of his ears;
what else would he have slept in on a warm summer day besides a pair of shorts?
Stammering, he invited his guests inside, and... the state of the house was just as
humiliating as his momentary state of undress. After shooting Remus a meaningful
look, he excused himself, dashed upstairs to his room, and locked the door.
What he really needed was a cold shower, but instead, he threw on a shirt and trousers
that could be considered relatively clean and devoid of the smell of sweat. He
remembered Lily's presence downstairs at the very last second, just as he turned the
doorknob. Taking two steps back, he grabbed the aromatic bottle sitting in front of his
mirror and spritzed himself five times.
When he went back downstairs, the four newcomers had taken their seats at the table.
Remus, wand in hand, was directing the freshly washed dishes into the cupboards. "Ah
mate, what are you doing? I would have done that—take a seat at the table," James
said, but the chore was already finished. Remus smiled at him. "No worries, brother."
Then, he took his seat at the table as well. He and Lily locked eyes. The smile on her
face was genuine. Her eyes were green, a rich shade that reminded James of the forest
and fresh grass, and there were faint summer freckles across her nose. The red hair that
James had grown so accustomed to over the years, and which he thought suited Lily
perfectly, had been cut rather short a few months ago and was now just brushing her
shoulders. They had been dating since June, and James...
He had truly chased after Lily for years, and just when he had the least hope, Lily had
suddenly told James that 'there would be no harm in giving it a chance, in trying.' And for
two months, they had been in this trial phase. He didn't know what Lily was planning,
but James had thoroughly enjoyed this trial phase.
Loving and being loved aside, James truly felt he needed someone. Not because he was
weak, but because he was lonely. He was finally ready to build a life with someone;
being alone in this house left him with no desire even to wash the dishes, even though
his hands would never actually touch the soap. Life had stopped for him. He didn't
know when he had last eaten a proper breakfast; when his parents were alive, James
used to love preparing breakfast for them and eating while chatting.
"There has been another attack," said Remus's father, Lyall Lupin, getting straight to the
point.
James's smile faded. "Who? On whom?" he asked, his voice trembling. Lily's smile
froze.
"Our classmate, Mary. Fortunately, she wasn't home," he added, and James breathed a
sigh of relief.
"Investigations into who did it are ongoing, but one of them has been tracked down,"
said Lyall. "Greyback."
The tension at the table was palpable, like an electric current. James turned to his
friend. "You need to move in here. Urgently."
"Calm down, James. We came here both to inform you and to discuss a proposal we
have for you," Remus said calmly. When his amber eyes shifted to Lily, James realized.
"What is it?"
"Our house might be being watched," said Hope, Remus's mother. "Even though my
husband is certain no intentional harm will come to us, for the past few weeks, a highly
talented and threatened young woman has been living in our guest room."
Lily tensed slightly at the compliment but managed a smile. "You are truly very kind,
Hope." Lily had resigned from her job at the Ministry about a month ago and essentially
vanished into thin air. She could only go places by Apparating from just outside the
house; even with James by her side, she always wanted to stay far away from magical
London.
James, meanwhile, was staring at the Lupin family in bewilderment. "I think I
understand. It won't be a problem. Lily and I—" he looked at the girl "—are together, and
wherever you want to move, I'll support you."
"That's exactly the issue," Lyall said. "Lily also wanted to move, but her family's old
house was sold and—"
"—I'm not struggling financially," Lily finished. "I can move into a more secure
arrangement."
"Alright. I'll help you find a place, too. What about Muggle London? You're used to it
there—" James reasoned. But the smile vanished from Lily's face, and she shook her
head in a definitive no, prompting James to cut his sentence short.
"We woke up very early this morning and discussed this, James," Remus began. "Lily
gave her approval as well. Of course, you two will talk about it again, but... if you also
agree, Lily is open to moving in with you today."
Wait, what? Had he heard wrong? He looked at Lily.
"Move in? Of course you can come. I mean, I agree too. It's just..."
"True, it's very sudden," Lily said. She was smiling again. "But instead of talking to my
sister and getting support from her—"
"Merlin's beard, you're right."
Lyall and Hope turned to James. "It feels as though we've suddenly dropped a massive
responsibility on you," Hope said. "But when Lily mentioned that you get along well and
that you are serious, my final doubts vanished."
James made a great effort not to widen his eyes in shock. Had Lily meant it in the sense
of a 'serious relationship'? Was Lily truly planning her coming years with James? They
would have to talk about this.
As soon as the guests left the house, James began to overhaul the place, wand in hand.
He had set up the spacious, east-facing guest room for Lily; he only repaired a few
small things and swapped the sheets for clean ones. The sun hit this room directly;
fortunately, Lily was a morning person.
He still kept his parents' room locked; weeks had passed since he had last cleaned it.
As he walked past the door, that familiar sense of grief gripped his heart, and he
immediately stepped away.
He was still sleeping in his childhood room. The only things that had changed in the
room over the years were an aging wardrobe and that wide bed his father had moved in
for him a few years ago, saying 'you're becoming a grown man now,' when he had
suddenly gone through a growth spurt and could no longer fit in his old one.
When Lily arrived in the afternoon with only a backpack and a single trunk, James was
surprised by the sheer lack of belongings. He recognized the trunk; it was the one Lily
had used at Hogwarts. Without allowing Lily to bend down, he grabbed the trunk,
floated it upstairs with magic, and placed it next to the bed. Lily set her backpack on the
bed; the seams of the canvas Muggle bag were straining slightly. It must have been
quite full. Once freed from the bag, Lily breathed a sigh of relief and tucked a stray lock
of hair behind her ear with her left hand.
James recognized the earrings. The silver earrings he had bought in their sixth year to
get in Lily's good graces, hoping 'maybe I'll steal her heart,' and gifted her for her
birthday in January. When Lily looked at him, James came back down to earth.
"I saw the earrings. I'm glad you haven't lost them." Or thrown them in the trash.
"I wear them sometimes. I mean..." she laughed softly. "They match the rest of my
jewelry."
James had told Lily not to touch anything food-related and that she could make herself
comfortable and listen to the radio. Then he brought a few novels and placed them on
the table in front of her: he knew Lily loved to read, but they were mostly Muggle
literature. The way Muggles handled normal life didn't appeal to James's tastes; they
wrote about either love triangles or troubled, psychopathic, sometimes even
murderous men.
He prepared a hearty steak with potatoes, making perhaps a bit too much. He packed
up the leftovers; maybe they would eat it tomorrow, or if Sirius came by at night, he
would eat it.
The conversation flowed effortlessly; they laughed constantly. Although taking a breath
and laughing for a few hours during this tense time, at the very height of the war, felt
good, remembering it later felt no different than committing a sin.
"Thank you for everything, James," Lily said, standing in the doorway. Her voice was a
little thick. "Really..."
James shrugged, his hands in his pockets. "Don't mention it, Lily. This is your home
too."
Lily paused for a moment, her eyes tracing James's face. Then, with an unexpected
step, she closed the distance between them. Her hands found the collar of James's
shirt, her fingers gripping the fabric, and James's hands reflexively found Lily's waist,
pulling her against him.
Truth be told, James's heart was beating as if it would shatter his ribcage. The moment
he had dreamed of for years was right here, right now, in his arms. But something... was
missing. Or perhaps it was too much. Summoning all his willpower, James did the
hardest thing of his life and slowly pulled back.
Lily, bewildered, whispered, "James?"
"Hey," he whispered, his voice tender. "We're in no rush."
Lily swallowed hard. "I just..."
"I know," James said, not letting her finish her sentence. He rested his forehead gently
against hers. "You're tired. And your life changed today. I'm right here, Lily. I'll be here
tomorrow, too. And the day after."
Pulling back, he looked deep into her eyes, wearing the softest, most disarming version
of that famous James Potter smirk. "Tonight, just sleep. Sleep knowing that you are
safe. We have plenty of time."
Lily's shoulders dropped as if an invisible weight she had been carrying had lifted. She
understood what James was doing. And in that moment, the gratitude she felt for him
gave way to something much deeper, much warmer.
"Goodnight, James," she whispered.
"Goodnight, Evans."
The next morning, Lily woke up to aggressive sunlight hitting her face in her new east
facing room. James must have purposely left the curtains wide open. At that moment,
Lily wanted to squint her eyes and bury her head under the pillow. But from downstairs
came the clattering, the sounds of a fork scraping against a pan, and a cheerful, off-key
whistle. James was awake, probably preparing a breakfast fit for kings, having already
forgotten yesterday's rejection (or rather, interception), and had already entered "new
day, new luck" mode.
As Lily stared blankly at the ceiling, she felt that familiar, aching emptiness inside her. If
it were Severus... If it were Severus, he would never display such noisy cheerfulness at
this hour of the morning. There would likely be absolute silence. When Lily woke up, she
would find him at the kitchen table, black tea and a complex Potions article in front of
him. Instead of making small talk, he would make a biting remark about the theory he
was reading.
With James, everything... they hadn't even been dating for two months. We still have
time. It's normal for me to feel like I haven't gotten used to it.
Lily sat on the edge of the bed and rubbed her face. She couldn't help but feel
ungrateful. Downstairs was James, ready to set the world on fire for her, having opened
his home to protect her. But Lily's damn mind was searching for the depth of that
difficult boy with the anthracite coal eyes, the one who had broken her, who had lied to
her.
Lily, in truth, loved the storm and the lightning; James, on the other hand, only promised
a sunny, cloudless day. "Step by step, Lily," she whispered to herself. "Just get up and
have that breakfast."
She slipped on her "grateful and happy girlfriend" mask, took a deep breath, and
descended the stairs toward that "easy" life.
When she reached the bottom of the stairs, the smell of toast and coffee wafting from
the kitchen had almost dispelled the melancholy atmosphere in the house. James was
behind the kitchen island. He wore a white t-shirt, and his hair was—if possible—even
messier than usual.
Hearing Lily's footsteps, he looked up. There was pure, unadulterated morning energy
on his face.
"Good morning, Evans!" he said, giving the spatula in his hand a slight wave. "I couldn't
remember how you like your eggs. Tea?"
Lily felt a lump in her throat as she sat in the chair. "Scrambled," Lily said, her voice
coming out quieter than she expected. "I like them scrambled."
James smiled, serving the hot eggs onto her plate. "Noted."
-----
By the time the calendar reached mid-September, Lily felt they finally had a routine.
Getting used to James was easy; the hard part was lying to her own heart. Waking up—
which they had been doing together for the past week—was followed by the breakfast
ceremony, the arrival of one or all of the Marauders (the house was like a headquarters
for the four of them), and afternoons spent either discussing the current state of affairs
or just making idle chatter. Then came going outside for some fresh air—James would
make sandwiches at home, and they were quite delicious—to either a park or a forest
favored by Muggles, perfect for a picnic. Returning home after sunset, having dinner
and relaxing, and finally falling asleep around midnight.
Lily was content, actually. Her answer to the question of 'what is the problem?' would
be—"Shall we get married?"
Lily suddenly started. She tilted her head down and stared at the hazel-eyed young man
resting his head in her lap.
"What?" was all Lily could manage.
"Not today, of course," the man across from her said with a laugh. It was an unhurried,
relaxed laugh. His lean but fit body was stretched out on the grass. He was wiping his
glasses on his t-shirt, but his piercing hazel eyes saw no one but Lily.
"James... we've only been dating for three months."
And Lily was trying her absolute best to make her heart beat for the man in front of her.
But something just... wasn't working.
James, to put it in the most superficial way, wasn't someone unlovable. He was
average-looking, not as tall as Severus, but he had an incredibly big heart. He had made
Lily his priority even before they started dating. He was always asking if she was okay,
as if constantly hoping Lily would ask him for something just so he could spend time
with her.
If she told other girls, Lily would probably get a solid slap in the face. "The man is like
winning the lottery, what are you waiting for? Say yes!!" Or, "The girls who want the
attention you complain about try so hard, while you get it effortlessly..." She kept
playing out scenarios like this in her head.
When no response came from Lily, James continued. "I don't know, Lily. Forget I said
anything if you want, it's for the best. The time will come, of course," he said, putting his
glasses back on. "I just... I just said it because we're both orphans. Of course, Sirius is
like a brother to me, but—"
"No, I understand what you mean," Lily said. She truly did understand.
"Ah, now I'm embarrassed; I was just talking nonsense." He looked at Lily and grinned,
then lifted his head from her lap and sat up. He chuckled as he reached for his jacket.
"Good thing you didn't panic and run away from me."
"You're right, James. Let's get married."
James's jaw practically dropped to his stomach. "Seriously?!"
"Yes," Lily said with a laugh. James was right, wasn't he? They could become family to
each other through the bond of marriage. Lily had never had a problem with Sirius, for
instance. If James saw him as a brother, Lily could too.
That evening, James invited all the Marauders, Remus's parents, and Peter's mother to
his family's home, and the two shared the glad tidings. They announced they wouldn't
wait long, planning to get married in a chapel within two weeks. Remus happily held
Lily's hands and congratulated her. Peter did the same, looking even more excited than
Remus. Sirius, however, hugged Lily as if he were trying to break her bones.
"I can't believe you're marrying this idiot, but I'm so happy!"
October 1, 1979
James was trembling, but it had nothing to do with the autumn frost. "How so?" was all
he could manage, staring at Peter. "Snivellus?" he whispered with disgust.
Peter nodded, in an absolute panic. Just moments ago, in a tone as casual as if he were
remarking that it was raining outside, Peter had said, "Did you know who Lily was
dating, and even living with, last autumn? Snape. Severus Snape."
Those words stabbed James's heart like a dagger.
"Mate," Peter said to James, placing both hands on his chest as if to stop him, though
James had no intention of going anywhere. "Now is the time to think logically, James.
Please look at me. James." Peter forced him to look at him. James looked, his hazel
eyes completely frozen.
Peter's ice-cold green eyes seemed to be pleading. "Haven't you and Lily talked about
the past at all? Mate, don't do anything rash. That girl loves you and wants to marry you.
For God's sake, be reasonable; today is your wedding day!"
"Then why did you give me this news, Peter? What kind of malicious pleasure are you
getting out of this?" He took two steps back, flames practically shooting from his eyes.
Peter's shoulders slumped. "Never. Never. I thought you knew too—"
"If you thought I knew, is this how you would have said it? And now you're apologizing—
what right do you have!"
"James, no mate, don't—"
"You intended for me to act rashly, Wormtail. You're so banal and cheap. Don't you dare
call me 'mate'."
"James, would I ever wish harm upon you or Lily?"
James repeated Peter's sentence in a high-pitched, mocking girl's voice and scolded
Peter, telling him to go back to the others. When the door closed, he punched the table,
then regretted it because his hand hurt. As he rubbed his hand, a sense of calm washed
over him.
Lily hadn't stayed mad at Snivellus for a single day during their seven years of Hogwarts
education anyway, and contrary to everyone's fears, Snivellus had never been seen with
the Death Eaters. The young man was ugly, cold-skinned like a vampire, stiff, and
thoroughly unpleasant. Plus, he came from a poor family, and on top of that, a
Slytherin. His not hanging out with Death Eaters felt almost contrary to the laws of
nature.
Taking a deep breath, he felt himself accepting the painful idea. Maybe Lily had truly
found something in that stiff, unpleasant boy and loved it. Or she was going through a
period of mental lapse, and Snivellus had seemed charming—James laughed angrily.
James had made highly illogical mistakes too; in fact, he was ashamed when he
remembered them now—he'd had one-night stands. But even with those women,
James hadn't lowered his standards. Worse than Lily having lived in the same house
with Snape was the thought of the possibility of Lily even looking at him...
The door opened again, and James spun around to look at the newcomer; if it had been
Peter, he was going to hurl a few curses at him, but it was Sirius. He looked as though
he was about to say something, but he saw James's face and closed the door behind
him.
"Sirius..." was all James could manage. How could he explain this? "This wedding isn't a
mistake, is it?"
Sirius perhaps was genuinely expecting something else. He rolled his deep grey eyes at
James, a smirk settling on his face. "Let me tell Remus to take Lily out of here, and you
can marry me."
James laughed. "We'd divorce in two days, brother."
Sirius joined him, and they shared a laugh. Once they composed themselves, Sirius was
straightening James's bow tie.
"I don't like these things. This nonsense called formal wear. I wish grooms could wear
whatever they wanted when getting married."
"What would you wear?" James asked, slightly afraid the answer he received would be
rated strictly for adults.
"My pajamas. T-shirt and trousers if we needed to take photos. If I got married on the
beach, a swimsuit."
Sirius tugged the bow tie one last time and stepped back. The mischievous expression
on his face slowly faded, replaced by that rare Black seriousness and a brotherly pride.
He clapped James hard on the shoulders.
"Jokes aside," Sirius said, lowering his voice. "Her eyes smile when she looks at you,
James. She looks at you as if you're the only man in the world, as if the sun rises solely
because of you."
"You're right," James said. His voice was no longer trembling; instead, it was taking on
that familiar James Potter arrogance. He looked in the mirror one last time, pushing the
broken boy in his eyes deep down.
Everyone was waiting for them in that makeshift little space, adorned with magic to
resemble a palace. James took his place at the altar, next to Remus. When the music
started and Lily appeared on the path, James's breath caught in his throat. She was
beautiful. Heartbreakingly beautiful.
As Lily walked toward him, James made a silent, dark vow to himself: 'I don't care about
your past, Evans. Nor that you loved that boy. Because right now, you are walking to me.
And I will be such a husband that your dark past will remain nothing more than a faded
memory. I will love you so much that you will be forced to forget him.'
When Lily reached the altar, she smiled at James. There was pure excitement and trust
in her eyes. James reached out and took Lily's hand. "I'm ready," Lily said.
The ceremony and their being pronounced husband and wife before God didn't even
take ten minutes. Remus caught Lily's bouquet, and everyone, including his own
parents, briefly teased him. But then again, there were no other single female guests
expected to catch the flowers. James smiled at his friend.
"The next wedding is yours, Moony. I promise it will be wonderful, I'll take care of
everything. You just get the girl," he said.
Remus looked at the bouquet of white roses in his hands with a sorrowful, almost
apologetic smile.
"In my condition?" he said quietly, in a tone only James could hear. "Asking a girl,
'Would you like to live the rest of your life in poverty and fear with a werewolf?' isn't
exactly my style, Prongs. But thanks for the thought."
James squeezed his friend's shoulder and hugged him, telling him that wasn't true.
Remus was one of the most honorable men he knew, and the girl who loved him would
be lucky. And Remus, too, of course.
The friends talked and chatted for another half hour, then it was time for everyone to
head home. James had a few tropical honeymoon destinations in mind, but Lily had
smiled at him genuinely and said they would have plenty of time to travel later. In light
of today's new information, James was relieved that they weren't in a rush to go
anywhere.
'God, what am I going to do? What kind of poison is this that my mind can't think of
anything else?' he wanted to scream. Instead, he thanked the guests, hugged them, and
wished everyone a good evening.
-----
The house he lived in had ceased to be the Potters' home for a while, after his parents
passed away, but now it was their home again. James hadn't completely emptied his
own childhood room, but for a long time, he had been staying with Lily in what he used
to call the guest room. When they went upstairs, Lily giggled and reached for her trunk
in the room.
"Remus gifted this last week," she said, holding a bottle of champagne in her hand.
Apparently, she had found a free moment to grab two glasses from the kitchen and hide
them in the room. She handed the bottle to James and took the glasses, and James
opened the bottle with magic.
"It's very good," James murmured as he took a sip from his glass, even though he didn't
understand anything about drinks.
"Muggles apparently love this. Remus saw it in that hypermarket and bought it for both
his family and us." Lily took a sip too, but her face scrunched up. "It has too much
sugar, though."
He didn't know she was familiar with Muggle drinks. Had she drank champagne and
made love with Snape too?
When the glass shattered against his forehead, James didn't know what hit him. As if his
face and shirt getting soaked wasn't enough, his head was throbbing. Had glass gotten
in?
"Get out! GET OUT!"
James slammed the door and left the guest room. He hadn't realized he was thinking
out loud. And damn it, his head hurt. After healing himself, he went to his childhood
room to sleep.
For the next three days, Lily didn't say a single word to her 'new husband'—neither good
morning nor thank you. Her sweet face was a stark contrast to her lips, which were as
motionless as a wall. James had tried talking a bit, but then Lily started finding notes
around the house.
"Lily, my one and only love. I swear, the moment I uttered that sentence, I wanted to rip
my tongue out. I just felt inadequate. I know it's not an excuse, but if you let me explain, I
will tell you. I love you."
Or the next day, there was another note next to the packed sandwich. "How many more
days are you going to punish me? At that moment, I just thought I didn't deserve you,
and the thought that you had a deeper bond with him drove me crazy. It's all Peter's
fault. Please give us a chance."
The Peter Pettigrew detail infuriated Lily. She had been willing to go another ten days
without speaking to James, but:
"Get out of bed, you asshole! I said GET UP." It was seven in the morning. She poured a
glass of water over James's sleeping face. "Does Peter know too? Did you tell him, 'my
wife slept with Snape'? Explain yourself!"
James squeaked, spitting out his words as he wiped his face with his pajama sleeve.
"Merlin's beard! No. No, he told me. He said you two used to date!"
There it was. That was it. Lily took a step back, scrutinizing James with the empty glass
in her hand. "I never thought this would be a problem. What a slippery man you are,
Potter."
"No. I'm never like that. No, something else made me angry." They were conversing for
the first time since the champagne incident. James threw off the duvet and got out of
bed. "Look, I don't like that man. Truly. From the moment I first saw him, I didn't want
him in my life, anywhere near me. Even you being friends made me tense when we were
at school. He was bringing down your quality, Lily. You are so beautiful."
Lily, for some reason, seemed to be looking at him with disgust. "Oh my god. It's not
that I had a relationship with just any man; it's the fact that I had one with Snape that
destroys you."
"You hit the nail on the head."
"Then drop dead from your misery, Potter. Let alone calling me a whore, he used to
choose his words so carefully that—"
"If he was such a wonderful man and treated you so well, why did you break up?" James
asked. Lily took a step back. "I'm not asking to hurt you, Lily. I'm genuinely curious."
Lily could have told him the truth, but she knew this would result in Severus's name
being immediately banned by the Order as a Death Eater. But Severus wasn't going to
choose them. Lily had begged him in her last letter to him.
Severus wouldn't choose them. And Severus had promised.
"That's none of your business," Lily said, her voice softening. "We couldn't work it out,
and we broke up. That's all." To James, this wasn't enough, of course, but press for
more? No, because Lily was talking to him for the first time in a while. "Would you like
some breakfast?" he asked Lily hesitantly.
"I ate the sandwich you left out last night," Lily said. "Why don't you invite that
gossiping, sneaky Peter over one evening and see what other filthy things he knows
about me. Let him say it all so you can relax, Potter."
Then she walked out of the room.
October 7, 1979
James wanted this marriage to work, so he invited all three of his friends over in the
hope that 'if everyone gangs up on Peter, we will make up,' and everyone arrived in high
spirits. Remus had brought drinks again, the exact same champagne at that! ("Lily said
you loved this," he said, and James took the bottle with a bit of trepidation. "You
shouldn't have, my dear.")
Lily also welcomed the guests. When that absolute idiot Peter told her, 'you've become
the fifth Marauder,' James wanted to nail him to the wall with his fist, but he kept quiet
because Remus laughed.
Sirius, on the other hand, was scrutinizing James with a serious and anxious expression.
Peter was relaxed, telling a joke, oblivious to the scolding he was about to receive.
James called Sirius into the kitchen on some pretext. "What's wrong, brother?"
Sirius said quietly, "Some things happened yesterday, classic Black drama. Bellatrix
and Andromeda ran into each other in Diagon Alley..."
"Oh no. Don't tell me wands were drawn."
"No, but it was a close call. Just a verbal argument," then he looked toward the living
room. "I'm just upset about it. I'll be fine."
"Sirius, whatever problem you have, you can tell me, you know that, right?" Sirius
nodded and smiled.
Peter opened the champagne, and everyone raised a glass. "May I raise my glass to my
new sister, Lily?" Sirius said with a chuckle. The others laughed too. "When things were
better between me and the esteemed nuisance Walburga Black—age four—I asked her
for a sister, and she refused, saying, 'We already have Regulus.'"
Remus and James spat out their drinks. "Then let me raise my glass to you, Sirius; you
manage to make the atmosphere even weirder with a single memory, even in the
weirdest moments," Remus added.
They laughed again. "Then let me raise my glass to my wife, Lily," James said. He locked
eyes with her, and he was serious about his next words. "I am lucky that you chose me.
If I forget, you can remind me by hitting me over the head. I love you."
While the other three men burst into fits of laughter, Lily raised her eyebrows. "What if it
hurts?"
"I'll endure it," James said with a smirk.
"Peter, you raise a glass too," Remus said. Peter flushed slightly. "Then let me raise my
glass to James. You are a wonderful friend. You all are. I really landed on my feet in life."
Sirius, Remus, and Peter burst into laughter again. This time it was Sirius who spat out
his drink. "For Merlin's sake, this is disgusting," he said to Remus, still laughing.
"Because it's a very sugary champagne," Lily said.
Sirius turned to Lily. "Who are you going to raise your glass to?"
Lily closed her eyes as if thinking. "Mmm. I suppose I'll—" She opened her eyes, and she
was looking directly at Peter. "Let me raise my glass to Peter. He implied to my dear
husband, ten minutes before our wedding, that I was Severus Snape's whore. Cheers,
Pete."
Silence dropped onto the table like a bomb. The glass in Sirius's hand remained
suspended in the air. That roguish smile on his face slowly faded, replaced by that
unsettling, dark expression unique to the Black family. His eyes turned to Peter slowly,
in a mechanical movement. Remus, meanwhile, had gone completely pale. His gaze
darted back and forth between Peter and James, trying to fit what he had just heard into
a logical framework.
"What did you say?" Sirius said. His voice was like a whisper, but it made everyone in
the room shudder. "Peter? What did you say to James?"
Peter sank into his chair, his hands trembling. "I... I didn't use that word! I swear! James,
tell them! I just... I mean, I just wanted him to know the past!"
"Ten minutes before the wedding?" Remus asked. His voice was calm but filled with
disappointment. "Ten minutes before, you implied to James that Lily had slept with
Snape? As if there was no other time, just like that?"
Peter couldn't answer. His eyes welled up with tears; he looked at James with pleading
eyes. All eyes turned to James. James had never felt so small in his life. He hadn't
thought it would happen like this; he assumed Lily would call Peter into the kitchen later
and talk to him. He looked at Peter squirming like a slug, and at Sirius's sharp gaze. He
could deny it, but why? James bowed his head. "Yes," he said in a muffled voice. "He
said that. And I... I believed it."
Sirius suddenly slammed his hand on the table. "You idiot!" he shouted, but he was
looking at James, not Peter. Then, in a flash, he grabbed Peter by the collar; Remus
stepped between them. James remained frozen. As Sirius shook Peter, he let go at
Remus's insistence.
Lily didn't even budge. Staring at the empty glass in front of her, she smiled faintly. "Let
him go, Remus," Lily said in a calm voice.
"Let him explain. Let everyone see what kind of a man James calls 'mate'." Her voice
was ice-cold.
Peter started to cry, his feet dangling in the air. "I just wanted James to be happy! That
Snape... He's a bad person!"
"Let's say it fell to you to judge who is bad," Sirius roared, shoving Peter toward the wall.
"Did you think about what this thoughtless behavior would result in? What if they had
fallen out?"
Sirius let Peter go. Peter collapsed to the floor like a sack. Lily and James looked at each
other. It's complicated there, Sirius... Peter left the house that evening, practically
chased out. Sirius and Remus shot James such a look that James was sure this
wouldn't just end here. But some words needed time, and if spoken in haste, they could
be very hurtful.
Everyone was gone. The house had once again taken on that graveyard silence. Lily was
in the kitchen.
"Lily?" James was at the door but couldn't bring himself to go inside. "I... I'm sorry.
Truly—"
"Herbal tea?" Lily asked. James was slightly taken aback, then nodded okay. Lily spoke
as she poured the hot water. "Apologies don't change anything. I saw what Peter is, and
how easily you are provoked. I will learn to live with this reality."
James swallowed hard. "I just want us to go back to how we were. Today is the fourth
day. When you are silent, even the hours don't pass."
"You didn't just break me, James. You made me feel that you wanted to break me." She
handed one of the cups to James. "This situation won't last forever, of course. Honestly,
I don't regret marrying you."
James breathed a sigh of relief at these words. Lily continued. "You're going to make me
a promise," she said. "You will never, ever question my past or my decisions again. We
will trust each other. And I will entrust you with everything of mine and respect your
decisions. Some things are only said once, James. If I hear it again, I will walk out that
door and you won't find me. Do you understand?"
"I understand," James said, as if his life depended on it. "I promise."
Lily placed her tea on the counter and let herself fall into James's arms. James had set
his cup down at that exact moment, wrapping both arms around Lily. He buried his face
in Lily's neck. "I love you so much," he whispered.
Lily didn't respond; she just remained in his embrace.
-----
December 27, 1980
In the morning hours, people usually feel freshly awakened, energetic, and ready for the
day. Ever since the baby arrived in the family, James missed feeling energetic and ready
for the day.
He was a victim of his feelings; no, no, James would die for Harry. To view his existence
merely as the continuation of the bloodline or the fruit of the love belonging to him and
Lily would be an insult to Harry. Lily and Harry were now his reasons for living, mostly
Harry.
But he longed for morning sleep. Since Harry was born, he cried every night and every
morning. He spent his afternoons sleeping; they would wake him up to feed him, and
during this, Harry would scream his displeasure to the world. The happiest hours were
heralded by the calm that descended on the house after the sun set. If one of them or
Sirius was home—and he came by every other day—they both got a little sleep.
Whatever Sirius was doing, Harry absolutely never cried around him.
"I threaten him. I tell him if he cries, I'll turn into a dog and bite his bum." James wanted
to believe this joke. Sirius could also put him to sleep and even change his diaper.
James and Lily could trust him.
Remus was in other worlds. Hope Lupin had gotten cancer, and the Muggle hospital
had recommended palliative care for her to spend her final days at home. James was
devastated by this news; Remus and his poor father seemed to be living in the rehearsal
of an impending funeral.
The terror of the Death Eaters was breathing down their necks now. But James and Lily's
perspectives had clashed. James, if he had his way, would take his little family and go
to the depths of hell for protection. Lily, as if she had lost her mind, talked about fighting
back. This change might have had something to do with maternal hormones. James
sometimes found her watching sleeping Harry. At first, James respected this
contrasting perspective, but then Lily started making potions. It began with small vials
that brought healing to people; tiny, hope-sprouting vials she managed to pass on to
their friends in the Order through Sirius, Remus, and Peter.
James could accept this, even support it. Potions had always been the subject his wife
was most talented at in school anyway, and James was sufficiently jealous of this
aspect of her.
Later, Lily expanded the work and began researching potions. When James opened his
mouth about this, the atmosphere would immediately grow tense, and Lily would start
blaming him. As if James were responsible for Muggle-born wizards being under threat.
"Grow up a little, James! This is what real life is like, it's not fair. You think we're still in
the school corridors, looking for a dueling partner," Lily finally said. She had exploded
too. James had left Lily in the basement that evening, struggling at the cauldron to
perfect the Wolfsbane Potion, and found himself next to the other person in the house
who loved him.
Harry was awake, lying in his crib, oblivious to the world.
Lately, even pureblood members of the Order were being subjected to such violence
and murdered by torture. James had a nightmare last month about his head being
presented to Voldemort on a silver platter. When he told Lily, she had hugged him so
tenderly... He had told his wife the entire nightmare, except for the detail that Severus
Snape was the one carrying the platter. He no longer wanted to sabotage himself by
bringing up that man again.
They had spent Christmas at home with Peter and Sirius. Peter had chosen to stay away
from James and his family for a few months after his massive overstep last year. This
distance had felt good to James and Lily too; they wanted the waters to calm a bit. After
some time passed, both Peter and Lily learned to live with this emotional distance.
Furthermore, the new couple had to digest an unplanned pregnancy and the reality of
an approaching birth. Waiting for a baby's arrival and preparing for it were different
things. Lily was considered as untalented at knitting as she was bad at cooking. But
what worried them both wasn't the lack of clothes, but health issues. James was very
happy now to have gotten through those days.
And now, that morning, James was pacing the living room with Harry, who was making
the room echo with his cries, in his arms. It was obvious the little one had a problem,
but James couldn't figure it out. He had tried to give Lily time to sleep, but the
approaching footsteps announced that the young woman had woken up.
"James," Lily said. Her voice was neutral. "He's hungry. That's why he's crying."
She didn't add, 'I wish you had woken me up'; there was no need. In moments like
these, James couldn't stop himself from feeling like a useless spare part. Lily was
Harry's only source of nourishment.
Then he went to the kitchen to prepare breakfast and ate alone; Lily didn't come to
breakfast. He thought about making his wife a sandwich, but for a while now, those
sandwiches had been left for Sirius in the evening. Lily was preparing and eating simple
things for herself. James was constantly worried her health would deteriorate again, but
even voicing this worry ignited the fuse of a fight.
Harry was crying again, but this was a cry of crankiness. A minute later, his voice faded,
and then he fell silent... Being the parents of a baby who resisted sleeping by crying
because he was tired was like this.
At noon and in the late afternoon, Harry slept; he even continued sleeping into the
evening hours. If they didn't have to, they wouldn't have woken him up, because he was
sleeping so beautifully. By six in the evening, Sirius hadn't arrived either; he hadn't
stopped by yesterday either. James figured it would be too late for him to come now.
Lily and Harry were in the living room; when James peeked his head in, Lily looked at
him with a smile. It was the sincere and warm gaze James sometimes longed for. "Let
me sleep for a few hours," he said to Lily. Lily nodded, saying nothing.
James went to the room. His body was truly sleepy, but his mind was not at ease.
Bothered by something he couldn't quite understand, he got out of bed. He was
shocked when he looked at his watch; four hours had passed. He must have fallen
asleep without realizing it.
When he couldn't see Harry and Lily in the living room, a small wave of panic washed
over his soul. There was one more place he could check: the basement. It could also be
called the potion-making cave. Both of them were indeed there, the radio was on, Lily
was dancing with Harry in her arms, and the empty cauldron sat in the middle. She
looked at James.
"Another failure..." she murmured. But her voice was cheerful. "I can't make a skin
moisturizer infused with Sluggish Giant Powder," she said.
Sluggish Giant Powder? While Harry was in this basement?
"Darling, that's a very dangerous active ingredient. It wasn't right for you to work on this
while you were down here with the child."
Lily pouted. "You're right. But I was following the recipe," she said, gesturing to the top
of the table with her head. "It didn't work anyway." She shifted Harry to her other
shoulder. "But Mummy will figure it out soon, won't she, little Harry?"
James smirked. "Mummy will figure it out, of course. When pigs fly. When no one is
home. And the cauldron is in the forest."
Lily laughed out loud. "I don't believe it. Your father doesn't trust my skills, Harry. Good
thing I fell in love with him despite his joking manner." She winked at James with a sweet
expression. James, however, couldn't reciprocate with the same enthusiasm. He would
come to regret this later.
"As long as you go off-recipe making dangerous potions with a child three meters away,
darling, even trolls wouldn't trust your skills."
Lily's eyebrows furrowed. "Wow. I guess your father hasn't had enough sleep, Harry."
"Maybe so. Or while I'm losing my mind trying to keep the three of us safe, while our
best duelists are being hunted like flies out there, I become the bad husband for
warning my wife so my son doesn't get second-degree burns, is that it?"
"James!" Lily hadn't yelled, but her voice was sharp. "I don't understand you. Would you
look at the cauldron? There's no fire under it, I destroyed it all." She pointed at the
cauldron. James's ears, Lily's eyes were burning.
"That doesn't mean anything," James said. Still, Lily was somewhat right. James shifted
gears. "I don't know. Look, I came at you—" his shoulders slumped. "—Because you
never see how hard I try for you two—"
Lily took a step back. "No. That's a lie. I see your efforts, James."
"I try really hard around the house too, for instance. I just can't calm Harry down when
he cries." He shrugged as if giving up. Lily stepped a little closer to him.
"Because you don't have breasts that produce milk," Lily said in a low voice, laughing a
little, trembling a little from nerves. She wiped her eyes; her emotions had overflowed
again. James rolled his eyes. Harry was only a five-month-old baby, but he was
watching them both. Lily chose her next words in a careful tone.
"James, I love you, but... School is over, yet you're still bickering with me as if we were
students. I know you're trying to protect me, that you're putting in a lot of effort, but you
don't listen to me. And once again, what you want is what happens."
James's eyebrows disappeared beneath the fringe falling over his face. "What does that
have to do with anything? Who else would you want me to protect if not you? Go tell
that student analogy to Snape, you can share your memories from school while you're
at it. And go ask him if the next attack is going to happen here."
Lily flinched, but her voice was surprisingly calm even to herself. "What does he have to
do with this? How would Snape know that?" James paused. He shouldn't have played
this card, but it was too late now. "You don't know? I thought Marlene told you in your
last owl correspondence. I thought you heard it from her."
Because Marlene was also in hiding like Lily, they only saw each other from Christmas
to Christmas. But because Marlene wasn't a mother, she was active in the Order. A
shiver ran through Lily. "I don't know what fantasy world you're living in, but what you're
implying never happens between us." Lily longed for a girlfriend she could tell all her
troubles to, and Voldemort had taken that from her too.
"Then hear it from me, darling. Your ex-boyfriend went and became Lucius Malfoy's dog.
If only we had sent him a card for Christmas to see if he'd gotten the Dark Mark."
Lily turned to ice. "No. He wouldn't. I can't believe it." Her eyes had filled with tears, and
her gaze had grown numb. James noticed the change in three seconds. "You must be
mistaken," Lily said, looking at James. "Because he promised me."
"Did he promise you this when you were breaking up?" James asked. Lily shook her
head, hugging Harry. "No. Before that." There was doubt in those grass-green eyes now.
"How sure are you that this is true?"
James shouted in bewilderment: "Does it matter? The man is a Death Eater!"
"Keep your voice down! You're frightening Harry!"
"Lily, damn it, I'm scared too! Do you understand? I'm terrified of not being able to
protect you, and you—you—" He was looking at her with an expression akin to disgust.
"Don't hold back, darling. Go write a letter to your ex-boyfriend and ask him personally.
Don't forget to include our home address so they can come and kill us!"
"James, are you an idiot? Where do you even come up with these things?" Baby Harry
had started his cranky crying. Lily was trying to keep him calm, rubbing his back with her
hand.
"Am I an idiot? These things never leave my mind! By the way, I hope that moisturizing
cream can protect us from the Unforgivable Curses."
-----
It was a terrible night. Lily locked herself in their room with Harry in her arms, and for the
first time, Harry wasn't crying at night. Lily shed tears for him instead.
Why had she married James? Why hadn't she spoken to Severus directly instead of
writing him that letter? Severus had most likely crumpled that piece of paper in his hand
and thrown it into the fireplace. So that not one more memory of Lily would remain.
Words were always a more effective weapon, and Severus had always maintained this.
If she had stayed with Severus, she would be married to him right now, and he wouldn't
be running around with Death Eaters. A knife pierced Lily's heart, and a tear fell onto her
pillow. Damn it, she was still in love with him.
On the other hand, James had a great deal of a point. They were on two different fronts
now, and writing to Severus saying, 'hey, happy new year, is what I heard true?' would
be a massive irresponsibility. There was no way to resolve this, either. One day in the
future, if she fell into a trap during a grueling Order mission, Severus would be forced to
curse her. Or she would have to take care of Severus.
By the time Lily realized she couldn't lie in that bed any longer, it was three in the
morning, and James hadn't come to her. She left sleeping Harry on the bed and went to
the room where James might have been sleeping. The bed was neatly made, and James
wasn't lying on it. Lily went downstairs to the living room. James wasn't there either. The
last place that came to mind was the basement. Lily went down the stairs again and
arrived at the door. She turned the handle, but it didn't open.
The door was locked. The basement had no door leading out to the garden, and
Apparating inside the house was impossible. Lily knocked lightly on the door, thinking
James would hear. Not a single sound came from inside. He was either sleeping or
ignoring her.
Lily sighed. She had noted down her new findings regarding their relationship and
planned an appropriate breakup speech. I can't write it on a note again, she thought.
Writing a note apparently didn't give the right effect.
If Lily didn't love James, her job would be ten times easier. In Lily's situation, James was
building a wall against her. She really needed to explain to James why they needed this.
She was thinking about the money she had been keeping aside for a long time. Maybe
she could buy a one-bedroom apartment; she didn't need financial help from James.
Lily had figured everything out in her head. As she went upstairs and packed her things,
the question of 'am I making a mistake?' came to her mind again. No, it wasn't a
mistake. The mistake was them wanting this when neither of them was ready to sustain
a marriage.
Lily looked at the sleeping child. Harry had been able to sleep peacefully, oblivious to
everything. Why tonight, she wondered? Lily simply pinned a note to the kitchen door
saying she was going to Remus's and that they needed to talk tomorrow.
-----
December 28, 1980
For Remus Lupin, who had survived the most grueling full moons time and time again,
whose bones snapped out of place and reset every full moon, the sensation of pain had
lost its physicality. He also had the comfort of knowing what the full moon would bring,
but heartbreak was on an entirely different level for him.
"Do you really think there is no other way, Lily?" he asked for the tenth time. Lily shook
her head with the same patience. On the opposite sofa, Sirius had lifted Harry into the
air and was determining the direction the boy would fly with his wand (because he
needed to get used to flying), and rolled his eyes. He spoke without taking his attention
off Harry.
"It's great, actually. When you separate, you enter a phase of getting your acts together,
and this usually has two outcomes: you either continue to stay apart, or you make up."
"Harry is just a baby, Sirius," Remus said in disbelief.
"The sooner the better," said Sirius. "I mean, it would have been better if mine had never
married in the first place."
Remus rolled his eyes. "Then who would have given me a headache?" The men laughed.
Lily was silent. Remus turned to her.
"You know best what to do, of course. We are by your side. And James's, too," he
added. "Still, don't rush this... divorce letter. Put it off until after the new year."
Lily smiled at him. "My decision is final, Remus. But you're right, after the new year."
At that moment, Hope and Lyall also woke up, and when they came down to the living
room, they noticed the guests. Lily didn't have the energy to explain it to them too; she
hadn't slept until morning anyway.
Remus took Lily to the guest room she used to sleep in and told her she could sleep
there. He and Sirius would gladly take care of Harry.
When Lily lay down on the bed and closed her eyes, Severus entered her dream. Harry
was in the dream too; he was older and flying on a broomstick in an unfamiliar garden.
Severus was on the ground, watching—no, supervising—Harry as he played Quidditch.
Lily felt surprised but delighted. When Harry did a flip in the air, Severus flinched but
then visibly relaxed. He was looking at Lily and saying something, but Lily couldn't make
out what he was saying and kept looking at him. This Severus was both a memory and
different. His hair was shorter, the wind whipped it into waves, and the sunny weather
warmed Lily.
Suddenly, the dream ended and Lily woke up. From outside the door—no, from
downstairs—came the sounds of crying and shouting. Lily put on her cardigan and went
downstairs. The noise was squeezing her soul. Something was wrong.
Lyall practically blocked her path and tried to keep her from entering the living room,
but Remus, noticing Lily's arrival, signaled his father with his eyes. Were his eyes teary?
"What happened?" Lily asked. She had just noticed Peter sitting on the floor in the living
room, his shoulders shaking. Sirius slammed the door and rushed out; Lily flinched.
"Thank Merlin you're here," Peter whispered.
"Peter, what is going on?" Lily asked.
"J-J-James..." Peter said, the waterworks starting again. "The house is destroyed, Lily.
The cauldron exploded. The p-potion." He sobbed.
Remus was no longer hiding his tears either. An elephant sat on Lily's chest. "Where is
James?"
Peter groaned. "H-h-he-" he started crying out loud. Lily's eyes shifted to Sirius, who
had just rushed out the door, but only Hope walked back in.
"I couldn't stop him. He kept saying he was going to look at the wreckage. He was in so
much pain."
"I don't understand," Lily said. She turned to Remus. "What would James be doing with
a potion cauldron?" As soon as the sentence left her mouth, Lily understood. James
had tried to prove himself and—
"No." Lily murmured. "He can't be dead, Remus. No. I can't believe this. Remus, say
something. Harry is too young, Remus... What am I going to do?"
Two gentle arms enveloped her. Hope, and Remus, who had gotten up from his seat,
sat the crying Lily down on the sofa. Lily was saying incomprehensible things, words
starting with either 'it can't be' or 'Harry is too young'. She denied it for two weeks.
Denying the truth creates a false bubble of reality. It's as if the more a person says 'no, it
can't be, it's impossible,' the more this bubble grows. Where the bubble bursts is where
acceptance begins, which was the heaviest and most shattering part. Lily didn't like
funerals, young or old. The dead should be buried peacefully, and people should have
the freedom to continue crying after them for as long as they wished.
She didn't see Sirius until the funeral. She didn't know what she was hoping for; perhaps
he would stand before Lily and say, 'If you had been home, James wouldn't have made
this careless mistake.' Worse, he could ignore her and act as if all their intimacy was
built solely through James. But he did neither; he was quiet and sad, exhausted, sniffing
and talking to Peter. When he noticed Lily, he hugged her tightly with one arm without
saying a word to her.
Lily would understand later: James was his only family, and now there was only Harry
and Lily left.
-----
When James Potter woke up in the cold basement that morning and realized he had a
stiff neck, he cursed his own ineptitude. Finding the door locked made him twice as
angry. When he couldn't find a trace or sound of Lily in the house, his anger gave way to
panic, until he saw the parchment stuck to the kitchen door. He couldn't manage to do
a single thing right.
He missed Harry. Around this time yesterday, he was ready to pull his hair out because
he wouldn't stop crying; now the crib was empty. It was 7 a.m. Should he go to Remus's,
or have breakfast?
Unaware that he would endlessly regret this choice, he headed for the kitchen. After
breakfast, he went down to the basement. His intention was actually to tidy up, but
when he found the recipe Lily had been holding yesterday, he couldn't resist reading it.
James was far from being a Potions Master, but this recipe was easy. How had his wife
gotten so confused?
But the basement was cold and stifling. James brought the cauldron to the living room.
Yes, it was against the rules, but banishing the smell that would seep into the living
room was as easy as a flick of his wand. The trial and error began. He had brought the
ingredients to the living room as well. First, he obtained a skin softener; he tested it on
his arm, and the reaction was different. It wasn't supposed to burn, but James's skin
was already sensitive.
Still, James didn't give up; this small failure couldn't stop him. This was too easy. He
added the same ingredients in different measurements. It was as if he were making
soup; besides, Lily either hadn't written down how much of certain ingredients to add,
or she didn't know. This was dangerous too; Lily hadn't known what she was doing
yesterday.
However, when the liquid in the cauldron took on a threatening purple hue instead of a
healthy pink, James realized something had gone wrong. He had probably overdone the
Sluggish Giant Powder a bit. What he called "a bit" was a whole handful.
He turned off the flame under the cauldron, but it kept bubbling and then overflowed.
Before James could reach for his wand, the cauldron exploded with a BOOM, and
James flew five meters backward.
James was hurled five meters back against the living room wall. The hot, sticky liquid
seeped through his t-shirt; his neck and torso instantly caught fire. The pain was so
sharp and sudden that he didn't even have the breath to squeal. He didn't even have
time for that. He had to get out—outside the house. The human body was too fragile;
human skin was not resistant to fire. But a stag... Its thick fur, its tough hide could
protect him.
His mind screamed, "Transform!" The last thing he remembered was running through
the flames toward the hallway. If asked whether he made it or not, James wouldn't be
able to say, "I think I succeeded." The ceiling had collapsed, the floors had caved in on
each other, and the stag remained in the rubble.
August 15, 1991
He had only lain down for a moment on the towel he had carefully spread out on the
sand, closing his eyes just for a second in the shade of the umbrella. He wasn't
expecting to dream. Waking up with a start, he realized this wasn't a dream, but a
nightmare-like memory he had actually seen and lived through. His flinching must have
startled those around him; two hands touched his shoulders and held him tightly. Harry
didn't need words. His parents were trying to keep him grounded in the present
moment.
The Hogwarts letter had arrived at Privet Drive last week, on the morning the three of
them returned from Hogsmeade. Vernon and Petunia no longer squealed when they
saw owl post, but they still weren't used to it. Harry's school shopping list was long, but
according to his parents, it could be sorted out in a few hours. Harry intended to keep
his mind occupied with the shopping list and spend time with his siblings and cousin,
but then Sirius arrived, and a fight broke out in the house.
Calling it a fight would be an exaggeration; Sirius wasn't saying anything wrong. Lily
didn't want to stay with Petunia until the end of the summer either, but Severus wasn't
keen on moving into Sirius's family home. Fortunately, Uncle Vernon had gone to work
while the adults were shouting in the living room. Petunia sent the children upstairs,
too.
About half an hour later, Lily and Petunia spoke in hushed whispers; though Harry
strained his ears, he couldn't hear them. Then the two sisters hugged tightly, and Lily
came to them to deliver the news: they would spend the rest of the summer in Rome.
Lily had held Harry's hand very tightly during Apparition. The shock of yesterday seemed
likely to last a while longer.
Martina and Sirius's new house was very beautiful. From the outside, it looked like a
three-story apartment building, and from the front door it appeared so too, but when
you went up the stairs to the first floor, there were two doors. Flat number 1 wasn't
actually a flat. There was a magic trick involved, like a sweet shortcut where one would
find themselves on the top-floor terrace. Flat number 2 was their space, a two-story
duplex apartment. The top floor had a terrace door too, depending on who preferred
which terrace door.
As soon as they arrived at the house, Sirius hugged Harry as if to break his bones; they
hugged for perhaps minutes. Harry felt guilty that he had found himself in danger again;
he hadn't thought about this when he wanted to enter the competition. He now
understood why his parents hadn't wanted him flying in the garden: what if Peter had
been waiting in a treetop for the right moment to curse Harry?
Five days in Rome had passed quickly; they were eating gelato and pizza every day,
wandering outside, and one afternoon when Martina wasn't working, she took the
children to the Colosseum to show them the historic arena. For Harry, these little
cultural trips were fun, but a part of his mind was back home. His father and Sirius, or
his mother and Sirius, but mostly his parents were spending more time talking to each
