other in hushed voices. They weren't including Harry in these dialogues, and knowing
that everything was about him made it heavy for Harry to bear.
-----
Today they had decided to come to the beach, an activity that would relax even Lily and
Severus, who disliked the sun, and their three children, who possessed skin that turned
red instantly. Louie was unstoppable, of course; Severus applied sunscreen to Louie
generously, without holding back. Then Louie ran toward the sea after Sirius and
Martina.
"Who else wants sunscreen?" He didn't wait for an answer, turned, and squirted the
bottle onto Harry's shoulders with a squelch.
Instead of going into the sea, Harry watched his surroundings and listened to the noise
of the people, then wanted to spread his towel on the ground and rest a bit. It was as if
everything had been kept under lock and key in his mind until now, but as if the right
moment had arrived, Harry watched that memory again. Taking a shower, the feeling of
being watched from the shadows, Peter and Bellatrix's entrance into the locker room,
Bellatrix taunting Harry like playing with food on a plate before eating it, his parents'
arrival, and the duel. And that moment, the cold green light that felt like ice.
But Harry had been able to open his eyes, he had returned to life. Beside him were his
parents, crying in grief and regret; he was already in his father's arms when he woke up.
"Did you have a dream? Are you okay?" Lily said. Harry nodded.
"I'm fine. It was a dream." He wasn't fine. It had all actually happened.
Sirius and Louie, soaking wet with saltwater dripping from their hair, came over to them.
"Don't you want to go into the sea at all?" Sirius asked, feigning offense.
"Louie wants his armbands so he can swim." What followed was pure comedy. Sirius
was horrified when he realized the armbands had to be inflated by breath. He couldn't
just pull out his wand and cast Engorgio in front of a hundred Muggles.
"Why didn't we sort these out before leaving the house?" Sirius grumbled, right before
blowing all his breath into that little plastic hole. His face had turned bright red. Louie
was laughing at both of them; Severus frowned. "If we had done that, Louie wouldn't
have been able to get his arms in."
In the end, Severus was the one who inflated them the fastest. Lily took off her t-shirt
too; Severus sent Louie after Sirius. "Aren't you coming?"
"The sun is still too scorching right now, maybe in a few hours," Severus said. Lily took
off her shorts as well and handed them to Severus. The burgundy bikini she was wearing
was something she had bought a bit hastily yesterday, and its design featured strings
Lily wasn't accustomed to. She tugged at her top, but the Muggles were wearing much
bolder tops.
"Hey. You look very beautiful, alright. Go swim in the sea." Severus sent her to the
shore, too. Harry was playing with Iris and seemed to be in a good mood; all his
attention was now on his son. Harry lifted his head and looked at him.
"Tell me your dream."
Harry tensed slightly, trying to gather his words.
"Well... You know how I was saying I didn't remember. I remember now. I mean, I didn't
have a dream." His voice trembled a bit on the last sentence. He took a deep breath.
"I'm so sorry. For everything. It was a stupid competition. I was giving you an attitude,
but it was for nothing. I don't know, Dad, it's all my fault."
Severus couldn't say a word, but he hugged Harry. Harry continued, his eyes welling up
with tears. "Now you're trying to protect me again. We can't go home. Now you'll always
have to talk in hushed voices in a panic, all because of me."
"No, Harry. Why would it be your fault?"
"Because I was stubborn. I thought I would achieve something, prove myself. That
competition didn't matter at all. It wasn't more important than you." Harry was crying
now. Severus calmed him down and explained that none of this had anything to do with
him, that Bellatrix or the others would have wanted to lay an ambush regardless. To a
calmer Harry, his words made sense. Bellatrix was a lunatic who had lost her mind. And
Peter was a traitor.
"What about Lucius Malfoy?" Harry asked. In last year's 'Young Quidditch Talents'
tournament, Harry had handed his peer Draco Malfoy a massive defeat, but the blond
boy had taken it well and had struck up a brief acquaintance with Harry.
"The fact that he is Draco's father shouldn't affect your friendship," Severus said. "But
don't go getting any ideas about sleepovers at Malfoy Manor. Your mother would skin
me alive."
-----
A surprise was waiting for them when they arrived at the house in Rome that evening.
Remus and Tonks had arrived.
"We were expecting you tomorrow," Martina said, hugging them both. Harry sensed a
very happy and positive aura from both of them. Remus’s light brown hair was a bit
longer, and Tonks had short black hair this time.
"I didn't want to attract attention among Muggles this time, that's why my hair is this
color. But it's so boring. Don't take offense, Martina, you are a complete brunette
beauty," Tonks said, chirping cheerfully.
They went inside. Iris and Louie were both exhausted from the beach and had full
stomachs. Severus put them both to bed and went out to the terrace. The open air was
warm; the sky was now a deep navy blue. Even the breeze was warm. Tonks and Remus
were sitting on the porch swing, telling them what they had been up to while waiting.
She had dropped her gelato before she could even eat it, and Remus had ordered the
same one again, but the vendor hadn't taken any money for the second cone.
After Tonks finished speaking, Remus said, "I have two pieces of news for you. Actually,
I don't know the second one either, but... Anyway, first of all, I found you a house." This
was very surprising. Lily and Severus hadn't even started looking yet, but Remus was
smiling.
"Don't decide without seeing it. I'm convinced it's very suitable for you. Moreover, it's in
Hogsmeade. Although train journeys are fun, Harry, imagine, you'll be at school within
20 minutes."
Harry couldn't say anything. This was good news; his parents would be closer to him,
but the final word belonged to his mother. He smiled at his Uncle Remus. Remus pulled
a letter from his pocket and gave it to Lily. Lily and Severus realized it was a letter from
Dumbledore. "I met with Albus this morning. We... took a few decisions. My mission has
ended as of now; well, the result was a failure, but it was worth a try."
"You didn't even tell us what your mission was," Sirius said, rolling his eyes. "Anyway,
we better celebrate this. Remus is free now, just like the old days—except for the full
moons."
"The mission to distance the werewolves from Voldemort changed so much over time...
I was successful in that regard, though. Where I failed was that they will never, ever join
the Order."
"That was to be expected, though," Severus said. "Werewolves have never been treated
well in the past," he added. Tonks turned to Remus, leaning in slightly to touch his knee.
"Tell them the other thing." Remus’s ears turned red.
"What happened?" Lily asked. She still hadn't opened the letter. "What other thing?"
"During this mission, I met an Alpha," Remus said. "He is directly responsible for my
positive development, actually. Even though he insisted at first that this 'curse' was a
'gift' to be accepted rather than fought, we achieved something. I’ve been in the same
pack with him for the last three years, and I don't intend to leave. I mean, I’ve made
some strange friends. It means I can be free during full moons now, Padfoot," he said in
a bashful voice.
"As long as you don't sell out my friendship, it works for me, Moony!" Sirius said, while
the others laughed. Lily was looking at the letter.
"Dumbledore gave it to me when he heard I was coming here. A post owl would have
gotten exhausted, of course. You can open it; I didn't, but Dumbledore mentioned to me
that it wasn't personal."
Lily was now thoroughly confused. She tore the seal and opened the letter. Severus
watched her eyes scan the parchment. As Lily read, her eyes first widened, then her
eyebrows furrowed. No one could make sense of the expression spreading across her
face as her eyes moved rapidly over the lines. Shock? Joy? Horror?
"Lily?" Severus said, a slight touch of apprehension in his voice. "What is Albus saying
now? Is it something about the Room of Requirement?"
When Lily looked up from the paper to Severus, her eyes were shining. "He... He’s
offering me a job," she said, her voice still low as if she were trying to process it.
Harry suddenly turned to his mother, and Sirius quickly swallowed the cookie in his
mouth. "What job? Nursing? Is Madam Pomfrey retiring?"
"No," Lily said, her voice clearer this time. She placed the letter in the middle of the
table so everyone could see. "Potions Professor."
As Severus reached for the letter, Lily's smile widened. "It seems Horace Slughorn is
fully retiring. I could also become an instructor, sharing the role with the current
professor—if I want to."
"I think you should want to," Harry said immediately. The others turned to him. "I don't
know, it's very cool. Who else's mother is a professor at the school?"
"Professor Lily Snape," Severus murmured. There was a ring of pride in his voice.
"What is it they say, 'the circle is complete'."
Sirius burst into laughter, leaning back in his chair. "For Merlin's sake! The poor
students... There won't be a single Gryffindor left in the Hogwarts corridors who hasn't
had points deducted."
"But what about the children?" Lily said, with a mother's reflex. "Harry, Iris, Louie?"
"I'll already be at school. And you guys can look at the house in Hogsmeade. If you don't
like it, there's a 'for rent' sign in the building across the street," Harry said, giggling.
Sirius reached out with a mischievous attitude and ruffled Harry's hair. "When did you
even notice that?"
Lily, meanwhile, turned to Severus with a laugh. "Could it really happen? Would we be
happy?"
Severus nodded and smiled at her. "I am truly proud of you. You are going to be a
wonderful professor."
Remus and Tonks were going to go home via the fireplace this time (when Sirius moved
into the house, discussing this with the Italian Ministry of Magic had been his first order
of business), but Martina and Sirius didn't let them. Martina had come up with a great
idea, and she didn't need to go to work tomorrow either. Harry, Lily, and Severus,
however, were heading to sleep. Sirius gestured to them; Lily turned to her friend.
"We're planning to go out for some fun. You should come too, unless you're too sleepy,
of course." Lily weighed the idea but gave it up a few seconds later.
"I don't know, Sirius. We really are tired. Otherwise, the children could sleep here
peacefully." Lily was serious when she said this. It was as if Sirius had bought the
furniture for the house thinking, 'Lily and the kids might choose this room'. There were
two wide beds, it got sunlight during the day, and she absolutely loved the wooden
wardrobes.
Sirius didn't take offense, but he said, "I won't let you escape another time," and wished
them a good night.
The four of them got dressed and went to a nightclub; this wasn't one of those stuffy
bars teenagers went to, it had a terrace and a pool. They could see the city at night.
Sirius picked out black t-shirts with rock band logos for both himself and Remus.
Martina had given Tonks a colorful crop top and shorts, and she herself wore a short red
dress.
Sirius bought colorful cocktails for all four of them, then started dancing next to the
girls. Martina and Tonks (whose hair was pink again) were on the dance floor, blending
in with the others. Remus was also moving to the rhythm, but the wolf didn't like these
environments. All his attention was on Tonks, bordering on extreme protectiveness.
Right at that moment, a stranger introduced himself to Tonks; Tonks sent him away.
Then came a second, and even a third.
Tonks and Martina were laughing out loud; Tonks dismissed all her 'suitors'. Sirius,
meanwhile, was rating the three strangers; according to him, number two was the most
handsome.
Tonks set down the cocktail she had finished, and Remus, disliking the taste, had
tossed his own drink minutes ago; suddenly he found Tonks right next to him. His
girlfriend immediately wrapped her slender arms around his neck, closing the distance
between them. Just as a slow song was playing...
They arrived home as the sun was rising. Instead of Apparating, they had walked (2 km),
and their drunkenness had worn off on the way. (Remus hadn't drank, but even if he did,
he didn't get drunk.)
Sirius brought his finger to his lips, making an exaggerated "Shhh" sign, but Martina
struggled to hold back a giggle. When they saw Iris and Severus in the living room, all
four were surprised.
"Busted," Severus said, a hidden smile on his lips. "Iris always wakes up around this
time." He also gave Remus a nod of greeting. "Congratulations on bringing them back
safe and sound as the only sober person, Lupin. From the noise at the door, I gathered
you had trouble finding the keyhole."
Tonks approached Severus with a giggle, reaching her finger out to Iris's chubby little
hand. "Oh, come on, Snape! We just had a little fun. Look, even Iris is glad to see me;
she's squeezing my finger!"
Severus gently but firmly pushed Tonks's hand away. "My daughter's immune system
isn't quite as diverse as your hair colors yet, Tonks. Go wash up, get rid of that 'smoke
and regret' smell on you, then you can hold her."
Martina threw herself onto the sofa next to Remus with a laugh. "You're so cruel,
Severus. But you're right, my feet are killing me." She collapsed onto the couch,
intending to stretch out her legs.
The following day passed quietly. Lily and her family Apparated back to England in the
evening; they didn't leave for home before insisting that Tonks and Remus take a slightly
longer vacation.
October 1985
Outside, autumn rain was lashing against the windows, accompanied by a howling
wind. Lily woke up to the cold emptiness on the left side of the bed and opened her
eyes. The clock on the nightstand showed 3:40 AM.
"Sev?" she whispered into the darkness. No answer came.
She got out of bed and slipped on her robe. Stepping out into the hallway, she noticed
the deep silence that permeated the house. He wasn't in Louie's room; the baby was
already sleeping soundly anyway.
Where was Severus, then? Lily had an idea. She went downstairs. The door to Harry's
room was ajar; the light wasn't on, but moonlight was streaming through the window.
Although initially only the silhouettes of two people could be made out on the bed, the
moonlight revealed the details. Severus was lying on his back, and Harry's small head
and body were resting on top of him. Harry was clinging to Severus like a baby suction
cup. Severus was holding Harry with one hand, his other hand resting in the boy's hair.
As Lily leaned lightly against the doorframe watching them, she realized that Severus's
eyes were actually open. He had seen Lily too. Stifling a giggle, Lily entered the room
and sat on the edge of the bed.
"I think I fell asleep with him," Severus whispered. "He's not sick, he just had a
nightmare."
"He already has the best medicine. You," Lily said. Severus smiled, tilting his head to
look at Harry. "Look at how comfortable he is."
Just then, Harry mumbled something in his sleep; Lily strained her ears to listen, but
they couldn't understand. With automatic movements, Severus patted Harry's back.
"Shhh. Keep sleeping, munchkin. I'm just going upstairs."
Then, laying Harry down on the bed, he sat up. Lily stood up too, and they went upstairs.
Harry slept uninterrupted until morning that night.
-----
August 29, 1991, 6-7 AM
The stag with large, branching antlers leaped silently over the familiar fence. The scents
filling his nose were wrong. The smell of Lyall Lupin's pipe tobacco was missing. Hope
Lupin's lavenders had withered. Instead, a foreign, metallic, and spicy scent had
permeated the air. The stag took refuge in the shade of the withered apple tree in the
backyard. He transformed into a human.
James Potter fell naked and trembling onto the muddy grass. His clothes from 1981 had
torn to shreds and vanished in the forest over the years. His human skin shivered upon
contact with the cold air. He focused on the first thing that caught his eye: the
clothesline. He staggered to his feet. His legs seemed to have forgotten how to walk in
human form. He pulled down a plaid lumberjack shirt and a pair of baggy trousers
hanging on the line. The fabric itched when it touched his skin. His fingers trembled as
he buttoned the shirt.
Then he knocked on the door. A different wizarding family, not the Lupins, opened the
door from the inside. James felt even more ashamed when he sensed they were afraid
of him.
"I am a friend of the Lupins. Lyall and Hope Lupin. Remus... Remus Lupin." The names
felt foreign to his tongue but were etched into his mind. "I was abroad. For a long time...
A very long time. We lost touch."
The tension on the man's face eased slightly. He lowered his wand a fraction.
"The Lupins? They've been gone a long time, son. Lyall and his wife passed away," the
man said, his voice softening. He seemed to take pity on James's wretched, feral state.
"His son... It was Remus, wasn't it? He sold the house last year. Said the memories here
were too heavy for him."
James held onto the wall to keep from collapsing on the spot. They were dead. And
Remus had sold the house. James's last sanctuary had fallen too.
"Where? I mean... Where is Remus? Do you know his address?" The man nodded.
"I'm assuming you're a wizard; judging by your state, you look like you've had an
Apparition accident. Wait." The man went inside and returned with a piece of
parchment. "He's staying in an apartment in the city center, on the Muggle side."
"Thank you," James said. He prepared to turn and leave. The sun had already risen by
the time he walked out the garden gate. He had a destination now. Remus.
-----
James read the address over and over again. He didn't know this place, but bringing a
map to mind, he was more or less sure where it was in the city: close to Waterloo. He
spun around; he had Apparated right in front of building number 15. This had to be the
address. Fortunately, there were no Muggles around, not that he would care much if
someone saw him.
The apartment door was closed, and James could only see the surnames of the
residents and the buzzers; none of them said Lupin, but it was number 8. As he was
wondering which name it corresponded to, he jumped at the sound of a car horn and
looked around for the first time.
The noise, the parked cars, everything was so different. Not two minutes had passed
when the apartment door opened; a middle-aged man had left the door propped open
as he walked out. James caught it before it closed and slipped inside. Remus was in flat
number 8. He climbed the stairs and set about looking for the right door. He finally
found it. A very plain, old, brown door.
He instinctively pressed his face against it, as if he could catch his friend's scent. But he
no longer had a stag's nose.
His heart was pounding. Trying to prepare himself for the worst—though he didn't know
what the worst was—he knocked on the door four times. James could hear footsteps
from inside. The door was thin.
At that moment, Remus and Dora were in the kitchen—Tonks, that is, but Remus hadn't
called her Tonks for a very long time, and the young woman welcomed this. The only
other two people who called her Dora were her parents, the people who were truly
close to her, who had raised her. The fact that Remus had included himself in this 'most
intimate circle' meant a lot to Tonks.
"'Could this really be?'" Dora asked. She was recounting an event and pitched her voice
higher to make it more dramatic.
She wasn't a morning person, but she was in a good mood. Stepping into the very
personal space of the man she loved—Remus was sitting on a chair—she sat on his lap.
"'Do you think your destinies could truly be written together?'" the young woman
continued, mimicking an awe-struck voice.
"And I told him, 'If I gave you another five Galleons, you'd see us as married.' Oh well, he
was just an old charlatan. We issued a fine," she said, giggling. "When Mad-Eye Moody
left the shop, he swore a few colorful oaths that hadn't seen the light of day, and we
left."
Remus was laughing too. He had wrapped his arms around his girlfriend. "You only
drank tea. Are you sure you're not going to have breakfast?" Tonks nodded and gave
Remus a short, small kiss first. It was a bit like a peck, then neither could resist their
feelings and the contact deepened.
When the sound of a knock came at the door, both of them startled and pulled apart.
Tonks got up from where she was sitting. "Are you expecting Sirius or someone?"
"No," Remus said, drawing his wand. The wolf inside him wanted to tell his girlfriend to
run to the room, but Dora was already a highly skilled Auror.
Remus unlocked the Muggle lock, turning it through the narrow latch, and opened the
door; it only opened a crack. Outside stood a man with long hair, wearing a shirt that
was too big for him, standing slightly hunched over. His face looked covered in
scratches, and there were leaves in his hair.
The wolf whined in alarm. The arrival was no stranger; Remus knew him. But from
where?
Then the man looked as if he was going to say something, but his mouth remained ajar.
Remus's eyes darted to his eyes. No, absolutely impossible.
The wolf objected. Remus hadn't just met this man; he had grown up with him. The wolf
inside him was accustomed to his scent, too. He stared at the hair; along with the
leaves, a single twig was visible. "Moony..." he said; the voice of the man standing
before him was truly his voice!
It was James! And he was alive! He was standing right there in the flesh! He just had a
slightly deeper and raspy voice.
"James? That's impossible..." Remus said suddenly. Perhaps he had thought out loud. A
light returned to the hazel eyes.
"It's me, Moony."
The wolf was in a state of shock, but from joy, not panic. It wasn't asking the what or the
how; after all, the Marauders were its first pack.
However, Remus was a man of logic. He looked at the worn-out man before him; he
could sense Tonks watching him from the kitchen (her wand at the ready). He could
open the door wide and invite his friend inside. But Remus simply Stunned the man at
the door; James hit the ground with a thud.
His eyes remained open, but his gaze was vacant; the first "hello" he received from the
first friend he had seen in ten years was a Stunning Spell.
Remus stood on the threshold, his chest heaving, without lowering his wand. His heart
felt like it was going to break through his ribs. But first, he had to remain calm; Remus
gritted his teeth. "Dora!" he called out, his voice trembling. "Come here. Don't lower
your wand."
Tonks immediately darted out of the kitchen, her hair turning a harsh yellow from
excitement and vigilance. She looked at the wretched man lying on the floor, then at
Remus's pale-white face.
"Darling... Who is this? You Stunned him. Do you know him?" Remus couldn't answer.
He dropped to his knees but didn't touch James. With the tip of his wand, he checked
the pulse on James's neck. It was beating.
"This... this is James Potter," Remus said, his voice sounding as if it were coming from
another universe. "Or something that looks like James Potter, smells like James Potter,
and sounds like James Potter."
"James Potter?" Tonks recoiled in shock. "Remus, that's impossible, he died ten years
ago. Or did he not die? Merlin's beard, wake him up. There must be a way for us to be
sure it's him."
Grey eyes met amber eyes and reached a mutual decision. They moved James to the
armchair with magic; he was like a ragdoll. Meanwhile, Tonks sealed the door with three
different spells. Remus pointed his wand at James again. "Enervate," he whispered.
James woke up with a deep, raspy breath. He blinked at the ceiling, then at Remus
standing over him with his wand, and at Tonks. James didn't move, didn't try to escape.
He just looked at Remus with a bitter smile.
"Nice shot, Moony," James said with that raspy voice coming from his throat. "You're
still faster than me."
Remus instantly pressed his wand against James's throat. "Speak. If it's you, tell me
something only we know. Right now. Otherwise, I won't hand you over to the Ministry; I
will tear you apart myself." James looked right into Remus's eyes, and Remus into his. In
those same hazel eyes lay the exhaustion and wildness of ten years.
"Fifth year," James whispered. "On a full moon, we had gone to the south of the
Forbidden Forest. Sirius had tried to ride a stag into the lake. You... you tried to bite the
stag—me—because Padfoot pulled your tail. The next morning, we woke up with a huge
bite mark on Sirius's ass. He told Madam Pomfrey, 'I sat on a chair'."
Remus's wand trembled. This information wasn't in any file, no one could know it. No
Death Eater could know this. This knowledge belonged solely to the four boys who were
in the forest that night.
Remus lowered his wand. His shoulders slumped. Without even looking at Dora, who
stood behind him watching everything in astonishment, he threw himself at James's
neck, sobbing heavily. Remus's sobs were buried in James's shoulder. When James felt
the softness of his friend's cardigan and Remus's shaking body, instead of the hard,
cold ground he had felt for ten years, the feral humming in his mind subsided.
"Boys, this reunion is wonderful, but we have an urgent situation on our hands. First of
all, everyone thinks you're dead. Really, how are you alive?" Tonks had spoken; Remus
wiped his eyes on the sleeve of his cardigan and let go of his friend, giggling.
Remus, giggling? James looked at the young woman. She was cute, young, and wearing
Auror robes. Her yellow hair turned pink.
James explained the details as best he could. When he mentioned the potion gone
wrong and the collapse of the house, Remus tensed. Then he talked about transforming
to escape and somehow throwing himself into the forest, because when he woke up he
was in the forest and realized the date from a newspaper left behind by campers. "The
explosion caused trauma to your mind. Even if you had been found injured in the house,
it could have taken months or years for your mind to heal at St. Mungo's Hospital," the
pink-haired Auror said.
"By the way, part of the house burned down. When we couldn't find your body, we
assumed you had turned to ash too. You have an empty grave next to your mother and
father's. It was a simple ceremony," Remus said sadly. "I shouldn't have told you like
this, I'm sorry. You're still fragile—"
James let out a soft groan. "Never mind me. I'm fine, I'm not disappearing anywhere."
He squeezed Remus's arm. "Lily... Remus, I need to talk to her, I need to apologize to
her profusely. I truly upset her. I couldn't appreciate her worth, I couldn't love her the
way she wanted."
Remus silenced his friend. "Oh Prongs, my dear friend. So much has changed in ten
years."
"They're alive, aren't they? Please tell me they're okay. I can't live without them, I'll die."
James sat up, a shadow of panic suddenly crossing his face.
"They're alive, don't worry, and they're doing well, too. Harry is starting Hogwarts.
Remus always used to tell me how fast he was growing." As Tonks spoke, Remus
nodded in agreement. Tonks continued. "We're a couple, by the way. You can call me
Tonks."
Remus had blushed; Tonks spoke with a grin. "Merlin, you're so right. We have to give
Lily this news. And Sirius too. How are we going to do all this today?" Her grin faded;
Remus was thoughtful too.
"Well, James, brother... A rather major trauma occurred in the past few weeks. We're
going to have to tell Lily about you very calmly."
James's face went completely white.
"Is it something bad? Did something happen to Harry?" Instead of calming James down
by saying nothing was wrong, Remus and Tonks looked at each other, and fear filled
James inside.
"The whole order of things has changed so much in ten years, James. Where do we even
begin to tell you?"
"Lily first. Please, Remus. You can tell Harry later, maybe—maybe his psychology would
be ruined seeing me. Can we do it that way? That works for me."
"Everything in its own time, Potter. First, let's clear the branches out of your hair and
reveal your handsome face," Tonks said, sincere but serious.
"Moony?" James said with sorrow, turning to his friend. "Please don't hide it from me.
Lily is okay, right?"
When Remus said yes, he was convinced and agreed to go to the bathroom. He
expected to go into shock upon seeing his appearance in the mirror, but there was a
harsh acceptance in the eyes of his reflection.
Tonks was right; he had branches and leaves in his hair. Then Remus came in too, and
with Remus's help, he cut his hair and beard, after which Remus brought a series of
healing potions. Aside from the cuts, James had no major injuries. Remus treated all of
them and, noticing that James had cut his hair a bit crookedly, tried to fix his mistakes.
Remus had not only lost none of his old humor, but he had developed it even further;
James was giggling at his jokes like a teenage girl. He spoke highly of Harry's Quidditch
skills and flying.
"You should see Sirius. Whatever he used to be, he's ten times worse now," Remus
said. Then a look of sorrow crossed his face.
"How is he?" asked James. His voice sounded more like his own now.
"He's fine. He cried over you for a year. All of us did..." then he stopped. "But he's good
now. He left England, he lives in Italy. He fell in love, you know?"
"I don't believe it," James said with a laugh. Remus laughed with him.
"It's probably been seven years." James gave that whistle Remus knew so well.
"Wow!"
Remus noticed his friend trembling. It wasn't a shiver from the cold. He held his hands;
they were cold. "Let me draw a bath for you. You'll feel better with hot water."
He could have stayed and bathed James; his friend needed treatment as much as he
needed the truth, but James said he would manage on his own. On his way out the door,
he saw Tonks at the kitchen table, writing something on a piece of parchment. "Is this
okay?" she asked, showing it to him.
"Sirius and Martina, I hope everything is fine. As you might guess, it's not at all fine with
us. We need you, Sirius, we have to explain face to face. Use the Floo Network, Remus's
house."
Remus sighed as the note ended. "Soft words, but Sirius will read the underlying 'we're
screwed' subtext beneath this. There's nothing else to be done, send it like this." Remus
chuckled. Tonks gave him a 'see you later' kiss and went to work via the Floo Network.
-----
James cleaned himself up for the most part in the bathroom. He had noticed his hands
trembling too, but was it because of the ten-year trauma he had lived through, or
because his friends had moved on without him? And Lily. She must have continued
living too; a sad, lonely, young Muggle-born witch crying over James.
'Please let her have reconciled with her sister,' he whispered into the void. Then he
dried himself with the towel and picked up the clothes Remus had left for him. A slightly
patched but ironed, nice shirt and jeans, which looked as good as new. They were loose
on him; James realized once again how much weight he had lost. He stepped out the
door and saw Remus in the living room. He looked thoughtful.
"We let Sirius know. He will come. And we will talk," Remus said.
"Did you tell Lily too?"
"Us first, Prongs. Between us... we haven't been four—or rather, three after you—for a
very long time, James."
"What do you mean?"
"James... please sit down first." James sat, and Remus continued. "After you 'died', Lily
and Harry started living at my house. My mother and father passed away one after the
other; my father couldn't bear the grief of losing my mother. My mother died in the
morning, and my father in the late afternoon of the same day."
"God. I'm so sorry for your loss, Remus."
"Dumbledore decided that Lily needed to be hidden better, because I couldn't be home
much either—I'll explain that to you later—Lily and Harry moved to Godric's Hollow, and
we placed an ancient protective charm on the house, the Fidelius."
"The charm that works with a Secret Keeper, yes," James said.
"Peter became the Secret Keeper. It was June, six months after your death."
"Why do I feel like a disaster is going to follow this, Moony?"
"Because it did, in a way. Peter sold the house's information to Voldemort, and that
night... Lily survived, apparently Voldemort threw her against a wall, she fainted. And
Harry... a mother's protection charm, perhaps because Lily tried to fight Voldemort with
all her might...
Harry survived that curse, James. He lived, he didn't die. Voldemort was severely
weakened by the rebound and vanished. He fled."
James couldn't process the new information. "That foul-blooded bastard, Peter! How
could he do it?"
"Peter had the Dark Mark, James. All this time, we didn't know. He was a spy."
James was crying. Remus placed a hand on his friend's shoulder. "Tell me he got what
he deserved. Tell me he's in Azkaban."
"He was. He escaped this summer. It was a mass breakout, 22 Death Eaters. We only
recaptured 7 of them."
Tears streamed from James's eyes again. "And then what happened? What did Lily do?"
Remus really didn't want this question to come up. "James, you are in no condition to
put all the old issues aside. If I were sure you would be objective... it was hard for me
and Sirius at first too, but we got used to it. You will adapt in time too, because you have
no other choice."
"Adapt to what? Objectivity about what?"
At that moment, green flames erupted from the fireplace and a man appeared. With a
cartoon character t-shirt, sunglasses hooked onto the front of his shirt, and jeans,
Sirius appeared in the kitchen. Remus stood up; the two men looked at each other.
"Remus, mate! Seems like you guys are having a problem," he said, grinning.
Remus said, "You could say that," in a low voice. Sirius had noticed the third person
sitting in the armchair in the room, his almond hazel eyes bloodshot: James timidly
raised his hand to his friend with a vacant smile and gave a wave.
The room turned to ice. Sirius first fell into a frozen horror, then his eyebrows furrowed;
Remus's wolf could literally hear the gears grinding in his mind. Remus didn't even
intervene; in fact, he took two steps back, while Sirius approached James. As if wanting
to make sure he wasn't a ghost (ghosts are translucent), he first reached out his hands,
wanting to check for a solid body. He grabbed the shirt Remus had lent him and felt
James's solid form.
What happened next developed very fast. Sirius grabbed him by the collar and hauled
him up from the armchair; James fell to the floor, and Sirius grabbed him again and
shook him. He was hearing insults on one hand, while tears wet his face on the other.
But James didn't take his hands off Sirius's t-shirt; in fact, he held onto him tightly. His
hair was shorter than he remembered, soft, and he noticed white strands. He also
smelled of shampoo. James on the floor, Sirius on top of him, they both calmed down
crying.
Sirius got up off James with difficulty. Remus helped James up too; Sirius wiped his
tears with the hem of his t-shirt. His sunglasses had fallen to the floor; he bent down
and picked them up. James's eyes were red, too.
"You... you bastard... I buried you with my own hands!" shouted Sirius, somewhere
between laughing and crying. Then he hugged James tightly again; this time they didn't
fall. James was sure his ribs were cracking.
"Where- where did you come from?" He wasn't letting go of James as if he might
disappear, and his eyes darted to Remus, looking at him as if to say, 'What I'm seeing is
real, you see it too, right?' Sirius reluctantly let go of his brother and sank into the
armchair next to Remus.
"There's an explanation for this, I suppose?" he said, pointing a finger at James.
As he hooked his sunglasses back onto the collar of his t-shirt, James's eyes scanned
him, thinking, 'He's different from my Sirius.'
"He escaped from the forest," Remus said. Sirius let out a laugh that released all his
tension.
"Merlin, 10 years have really passed. Lives have changed. Peter was a traitor, Remus is
dating an Auror—don't let this one go, Moony, really." James hiccupped while crying out
of happiness. "So, Padfoot, who is the lucky girl?"
As Sirius rolled his eyes, Remus laughed, "Oh come on, tell him, Sirius."
"I met Martina on the night of a football match, and I haven't kissed another woman
since." Sirius was praising his doctor girlfriend, he couldn't stop talking about her, but
James listened to him with hearts practically bursting from his eyes. Then Sirius finished
his story, and reality took hold of them again.
"Peter was at the head of those escapees, James. When I find him, I'm going to strangle
him with my own bare hands, he won't be lucky this time," Sirius said.
James nodded, but his mind was elsewhere. "Lily..." James said, his voice more
resolute this time. "I just told Remus, too. You have to take me to her. Or call her here.
I... I can't wait any longer."
"James," Remus said, leaning towards the table. "I was serious when I told you that you
need to adapt, that you need to be objective. Lily... Lily is no longer that twenty-year-old
girl you knew. She is a mother. And she... she did everything she could to protect Harry
for ten years."
"I know," James said impatiently. "She is a wonderful woman. Just let me tell her how
much I love her."
Sirius took a deep breath. He couldn't take it anymore. "James, look... Lily got married."
James's smile slowly froze on his face. His heart felt like it stopped for a moment. "She
got married? Of course... Of course she got married. Ten years have passed." Harry
needed a father. But who had she chosen?
Sirius leaned forward. "That's not all. Harry has been calling him 'dad' for years.
Besides, even though I want you to see your son too, today is not the time or the place."
James stared at him. "How is it not? Will he never know me?"
"Harry is in a very sensitive period right now," Sirius said in a stern voice. "I'm thinking of
his well-being, that's all."
"Who is it then, Sirius?" James's voice had turned into a rasp now. "Who is this lucky
man who stole my son from me..."
Remus held James's hands. "James, calm down. No one stole Harry. Harry knows about
you, but he chose to call that man that himself. He's 11 years old and a smart boy.
Sirius, you tell him, can you force Harry into anything?"
"Never. He is very stubborn and competitive." James smiled, a little relieved.
"You still haven't given me a name. Is he a Muggle or something?"
"James... no, he's not. But you have to understand, that night when Peter betrayed you,
when everything ended... there was only one person by Lily's side. Only one person who
risked everything to protect her and Harry."
James paused. "Mate, no. Tell me it's not the man I'm thinking of."
"Severus Snape," Sirius said, answering instead of Remus. "And we don't call him
Snivellus anymore. Did you know greasy hair isn't hereditary? He gets curls when he
comes out of the sea."
"What curls? Padfoot, are you joking?"
"I'm not joking at all, brother." Sirius had grown a bit stern. "Sorry, we ripped the band
aid off a bit hard, but this is the situation. It's not your fault, it's not Lily's fault, life just
moved in this direction."
James fell silent, but his eyes were filled with pain; he looked as if he would cry if you
merely touched him. Sirius, however, didn't take pity on him; turning to Remus, he
asked, "Do you want a beer?"
"There is no alcohol in this house," Remus said with a smile. "Since I can't get drunk,
and, well, Dora doesn't like it either."
"What kind of youthful fire is this? Your spark has gone out. You've aged my energetic
and breathtakingly beautiful niece, too, Moony."
Remus chuckled at first, and was laughing by the time Sirius finished his sentence.
James was confused. "Tonks is her surname... Moony, she's Andromeda's daughter,
isn't she? Merlin, what's the age difference between you two?"
It was Sirius's turn to laugh, while Remus looked a bit put out, but he quickly recovered.
"First of all, she seduced me. Second of all, it's none of your business." Then he started
laughing, too.
The old friends talked for a few more hours, poured their hearts out, and cried from time
to time. As James rethought what he had learned, he became certain that the thing he
couldn't accept the most wasn't Snape marrying Lily, but Harry knowing him as his
father. Then there was the matter of Peter. He had learned about the pool incident too,
and his insides were now like a blazing fire... If he found Peter, he would kill him without
hesitation.
When he relayed his thoughts to Sirius and Remus, the two Marauders were not
surprised.
"I would personally tear out his heart and leave it for the carrion crows to eat," Remus
said in an emotionless voice. He bore no desire for revenge; he spoke as if this were
simply the most appropriate punishment to be dealt.
When the sun set, Sirius turned to James with a 'strictly taking no for an answer' attitude
and told him he was coming with him to Rome, leaving James speechless. "Wh-wh
where?"
"Rome. I live there. I told you, Prongs." His gaze turned to James. "There is actually so
much I want to share with you."
Remus hugged his friend, who had literally 'returned to the land of the living', tightly
once again. "We'll see each other tomorrow, James. Don't forget that we are by your
side and run off to the forests again." They left through the fireplace with green flames.
-----
BOOK 3: Marching Horses (Italy segment)
The Last Day of August, 1991
Martina was staying with her family in the South at the time. A few days prior, as she boarded the train from Rome, she had kissed Sirius at the station and whispered, “Don’t burn the house down, whatever you do, amore.” Giving a room to a best friend long-presumed dead didn't exactly count as burning the house down.
James was bewildered, though his curiosity outweighed his confusion. It was the first time in his life he had stayed in a house powered by electricity and natural gas. Since his wand had been damaged during the potion cauldron explosion, he was forced to actually flip a switch on the wall just to turn on the light. Even that felt bizarre to him.
Sirius had told him a great deal, yet he remained maddeningly cryptic about the most vital details. “I’ll explain in the morning; sleep now,” he’d said, handing James a peculiar, round tablet of medicine to swallow. James hadn't taken it, of course—not because he didn't trust Sirius, but because... this Sirius wasn't his brother. Not yet. And yet, he walked like him, laughed like him. Just as James had missed Harry growing up, he had missed Sirius’s transformation too.
When morning came, Sirius showed him how to work the shower and brought him fresh clothes and a towel. Twenty minutes later, James emerged from the bathroom looking like he’d just won a war, grinning at his best mate with damp hair—but his mind was on Harry. Tomorrow was the boy’s first day at school; would they even be able to see each other during the term? Sirius, noticing the grey clouds gathering on James’s face, teased him mischievously.
“You need some sun,” he said. They didn't Apparate; they simply walked out to a small café.
Sirius took a bite of his croissant. But it was that bite—the "pre-confession" bite, James had known him long enough to recognise it instantly.
Sunlight hit the table, and Sirius squinted; it looked as though it wasn't the sun bothering him, but his own past.
“Do you like it?” Sirius asked. “Proper Italian breakfast, mate.” James had ordered orange juice instead of coffee. He had no intention of welcoming that mud-coloured Muggle swill into his life (he’d heard from Lily once just how bitter it was). Sirius continued, “James... about seven years ago, one night, I ran into a girl.”
James smirked. He’d seen the pictures of them in the lounge—Muggle photographs—Sirius standing beside a shorter young woman with smiling eyes and dark hair cascading down her shoulders, possessing a natural elegance. Sirius’s taste was undeniably good. “With a car or a broomstick?”
"Nah. My ego."
James let out a bark of laughter—the kind that made the elderly ladies at the neighbouring tables turn to stare.
Sirius held up a hand to silence him, though a faint smile played on his lips. “I’m serious. This... this was something else. Martina.”
James’s eyebrows shot up. “Her name’s Martina? Remus didn't give many details. Is she Italian? Merlin, it’s like pulling teeth with you lot.”
Sirius ignored the jab. “Italian. Medical student. Two years below us, though children born after September don't lose a year here, apparently. And she... she was the first person in my life who didn't give a damn about me. I was floored. I was hooked.”
James’s jaw dropped. “What do you mean? She didn't even look at you? I’ll be honest, Padfoot, she sounds like a clever girl. One could fill an entire encyclopaedia with your past conquests.”
“Go on, have your laugh. but I was burning up inside. I flirted, I joked, I bought gifts... I couldn't win her over.”
The expression on James’s face was one of "this defies the laws of physics." “You? Sirius Black? Couldn't win her over?”
Sirius set down the croissant and took a deep breath. “Let’s go back to the start. That night...”
February, 1983. Outside the bar, youngsters were smoking, beer bottles clinked, and the streetlamps cast a hazy yellow glow.
“Wait a second. You won't understand it without the full story. Look, Prongs... I’m going to tell you something, but you have to swear you won't laugh.”
“I can never promise that. Spill it.” Sirius pushed his croissant aside, leaned his elbows on the table, and leaned toward James.
“Anyway. I walked into a train station in Paris. People were running, shouting, eating sandwiches... I joined the throng. I was supposed to catch the train to Rome.”
“And?” James asked, bracing himself for the punchline. “And... you got on the wrong train, didn't you?”
Sirius shook his head. “Right train, wrong stop.” James’s face lit up. “I got on, I was in my dog form, I was exhausted... I fell asleep. When I woke up,” Sirius said with dramatic flair, “the train... the whole bloody train was on a ship.”
James sat bolt upright. “What?!”
“Yes. A literal ship. In the middle of the sea. I thought I was going to Rome, but it turns out the train was headed for Sicily. To Catania.” James couldn't hold it in any longer; he started howling with laughter.
“Fate, I suppose... I stayed there. Sicily was good. Hot. Loud. Chaotic. Nothing like England. Constant parties, booze, girls... I figured I’d stay a bit longer and then head back to Paris, until...”
James laughed until tears pricked his eyes. “Yes. Until... you ran into a girl.”
As Sirius was passing through the crowd, his eyes had snagged on someone: Martina.
When you locked eyes with her, she had the kind of aura that took your breath away; yet that aura stood in total contrast to her clothes. Every day, she bustled from the lecture hall to the lab, then to the library like a worker bee, clad in oversized t-shirts with a patched rucksack.
She was social and energetic, but Sirius had never seen her smile, not even once.
That evening, her hair was up in a bun. She’d even brought her medical textbooks to the match night; the books wouldn't even fit in her battered bag. She wore an expression that said, “I have things to do, I haven't got time for you.” Sirius immediately donned his famous mask: the grin, the shoulders back.
“Hello. We’ve met before, but you brushed me off. I’m Sirius.”
Martina looked up, and they locked eyes again. She had dark brown eyes and wore no makeup. “Yes, I’ve heard.”
Sirius paused. Usually, that "I’ve heard" would be followed by "Wait, the Sirius Black?!" But Martina just tucked her book into her bag and kept walking.
Sirius followed her, desperate. “Er... would you like a drink?”
“No.”
Sirius’s face fell. But he didn't give up. “And why not?”
Martina didn't even turn around as she replied. “Because I’m going home.”
Sirius sighed as he watched the tasselled hair tie dangling from her bun. “This girl is allergic to smiling,” he whispered to himself.
James was roaring with laughter, nearly falling off his chair. “Someone actually spoke to you like that? Finally! I like your girl already. So, what did you do?”
“I followed her, obviously. I’m Sirius Black.”
“And?”
Sirius narrowed his eyes and tore off a piece of the croissant. “And... I hit a wall. I told you, I crashed. Everything was going perfectly, and then she made a move that completely knocked me off my axis.”
A look of pure delight washed over James’s face. “Tell me. Go on. This is the best part of my breakfast.”
Sirius and Martina were sitting in a café. Months had passed since that night at the bar; June had arrived, and the memories they’d shared were perhaps almost too beautiful to be true. And yet, they were real.
But if there was one thing Sirius knew, it was that this kind of happiness felt foreign to him. For five months, he’d been rowing in uncharted waters. He figured now was the best time to end it; while they were both relaxed, while things were easy. If it ended now, there wouldn't be a price to pay later—a price he didn't know how to handle. God forbid, he wouldn't end up tearing Martina’s heart out.
Sirius stopped abruptly and looked at her. "Look, Martina, we... er... I’m not looking for anything serious, you know that, right?"
Martina looked up. Calm. Unbothered. "Fine." Sirius felt a momentary surge of relief, but then something else annoyed him. She had said "fine" way too fast.
"Well, I’m going on a date with someone else then. Summer break’s started, Mr. Black, in case you hadn't noticed. Classes are done and—"
Sirius’s face dropped. He stopped listening. "What?!"
"What’s the big deal? You said you aren't serious. We hung out, we snogged, it's over."
Sirius’s mask fell right then and there. "I wasn't ready for it to be over! I just said I wasn't thinking serious. I was... you know, testing the waters. Seeing what you thought."
"But I am serious," Martina continued, her expression turning genuinely sincere. "Don't you ever want to fall in love? To actually give your heart to the right person? How long are you going to keep running on the beach at sunset, Sirius?"
Sirius took a deep breath. Dammit, she was right. "Fine. You're right. I... I just hadn't looked at it that way."
"Don't worry about it. I like that you're being straight with me, Sirius. It’s better this way. We won't waste each other’s time."
Back in 1991, James’s face was a chaotic mix of horror and pure amusement.
"And you... you said nothing? Sirius, how could you say nothing?! Padfoot, you were clearly head over heels for her, why on earth did you try to end it?"
Sirius looked away, his eyes fixed on the sugar bowl on the table. "Because... James, you of all people should get it. In that moment... I was stumped. I just thought I wasn't enough for a girl like her. But I didn't want to lose her. And I definitely didn't want to hurt her."
James shook his head. "So what happened next?"
"The 'next' part... that took weeks."
Sciacca, August 1983. Since medical school didn't start until the third week of September, Martina wasn't in Catania.
Sirius hadn't slept until half-past seven that morning, obsessively planning what to say. He’d practically begged Martina’s friends for her address, and without thinking twice, he Apparated right there, scaring the life out of two stray cats in the process.
Twenty seconds after he rang the bell, Martina opened the door. She was in her pyjamas—a strappy top and shorts—and her hair was short. She’d had it cut since he last saw her. She looked stunned to see him. "Sirius? What are you doing here at this hour? Is something wrong?"
Sirius’s voice wavered, but he stayed firm. "Yeah. Something’s wrong. I... I was an idiot. I got scared. But I’m more scared of losing you. My whole life, everything was under my control until I met you. I was used to that; people, girls, always chasing me, clinging to me—"
Martina shook her head. "God, wait. We can't do this at the door. Get inside."
Sirius stepped in, and Martina said something in Italian to the people inside while grabbing Sirius’s hand and leading him. They walked into the kitchen, where a white-haired man was plugging in a fan, chatting to Martina.
Martina sat Sirius down in a chair. "You're just in time for breakfast, Mr. Black."
"I was still explaining—"
"Later," Martina said. She said something else to her father, who replied rather loudly. Sirius’s ears turned red, thinking she was being told off, but then Martina barked back even louder. Her dad just burst out laughing. In Grimmauld Place, that kind of backtalk would have earned him a week locked in his room.
Martina left the room, leaving Sirius in a total panic. The white-haired man turned to him and asked in perfect, unaccented English, "So, are you a student as well?"
Sirius blinked, struggling to find the words. "Y-yes. Language student."
"Translation, or the Faculty of Literature?"
How was Sirius supposed to answer that? "They're pretty much the same thing, Dad," Martina’s voice cut in. Sirius exhaled in relief, thanking Merlin for her timing.
The father stood up and headed for the counter. "Does your friend drink coffee?" he asked his daughter. Martina smiled at Sirius and answered in Italian, making her father smirk at him. Then he left the kitchen, and they were finally alone.
"I hope you're hungry," she said, looking at him.
"Yeah. But I came here for something way more important."
"Must be urgent if you caught the 6:30 AM bus," Martina said, her long lashes fluttering as she studied him.
"It is. I—" Martina held up a hand to stop him because the coffee was ready. She pulled out two tiny cups, filled them quickly, and handed him one.
As Sirius stared at his cup, Martina sat back down. "Sorry, I interrupted. What were you saying?"
"I’ve thought about it. A lot. You... you’ve affected my life from a completely different angle. My world is chaos; yours is order. You're calm, warm, and simple. And I’m a moron for not seeing that and trying to push it away. Now, my feelings are just... overflowing. And I know I’m doing a rubbish job of explaining this."
"Sirius, I’m learning the most difficult subjects in my native language and I still fail exams sometimes. I’d say you're expressing yourself just fine."
"Wow. That actually feels good to hear. But I still need to step up. The point is, I haven't been worthy of you until now, I see that. From now on, I’m changing that. Seriously, I promise. And—"
Martina blinked her brown eyes. Sirius pressed on: "From now on... you're only dating me."
That famous, annoying, triumphant smile spread across Martina’s face. "Finally. You took your sweet time saying it."
When Sirius finished the story, James stayed silent for a moment. Then he leaned back in his chair, covered his face with both hands, and said in a muffled voice, "No... no... you actually lived through that. Unbelievable."
Sirius frowned. "What’s so funny?"
James lowered his hands, his eyes watering—from laughing. "Padfoot... you... you Apparated into her city at the crack of dawn and... had breakfast with her dad while she was in her pyjamas?"
Sirius grumbled, "Yes. And it was a perfectly civilised breakfast, thank you very much."
James roared, slamming his hand on the table. "I wish I’d been there! Oh, Merlin’s beard, I’m crying. I haven't laughed this hard in ages."
Sirius flushed red. "Remus reacted the same way when I told him, though his eyes stayed dry. You lot were clearly desperate to see me settled, weren't you?"
James’s expression softened. "Off course we were. I love Martina already. You made a complete fool of yourself for her, Padfoot... she’s really changed you."
Sirius fell silent. James smiled—that soft, brotherly smile. "I’ve never seen you like this. Ever. The day Sirius Black told a girl 'you're only dating me'... that belongs in the history books."
Sirius muttered, "Oh, she’d adore you too. Just wait until you see her with a syringe in one hand and a stethoscope around her neck. She couldn't find a disease to cure in me, so I reckon you're next."
James barked out a laugh. "Of course she couldn't cure you—you need a brain for that! I swear, this girl is sharper than you'll ever be."
Sirius sighed. "Go back to being a stag for a second, I want to try something. There's a forest over there; go hide in it and come back in ten years, would you?"
"And miss your wedding? Not a chance, mate!"
Sirius rolled his eyes. "Fine. But the rest is even more chaotic. Right up your alley."
"After that day, it was like a dream... well, except for Reg’s death. I couldn't tell the poor girl the truth back then; I just brushed it off, saying I had 'family matters' to attend to in London. When I got back to Italy around mid-October, Martina said, 'If we’re serious, why aren't we living together?' and invited me into that tiny student flat. It was a mistake, obviously. Stop laughing, James! I can see you! I’ll get to the part in '85 where I had to flee that flat—you can laugh then."
James nearly sprayed his orange juice across the table. Sirius pressed on: "You have no idea the hoops I jumped through to keep my cover. International Statute of Wizarding Secrecy was breathing down my neck. I even got a 'Muggle residency permit' in Italy, thinking the British Ministry of Magic wouldn't track me there. Fat chance! Old Fudge wasn't Minister yet, but he was as meddlesome as ever, keeping a special eye on me."
"To keep from being exposed, I found a part-time job. I acted like I was living on a shoestring budget. I only Apparated back to London twice to see Remus, telling Martina I was 'taking a flight'."
James raised his eyebrows. "You? A part-time job? And how exactly were you 'flying'? That—"
"Let me finish, I promise I’ll answer everything. Do you know what she told me? 'Amore, don't squander your money; you have to chip in for the gas bill. I can't ask my parents for more—I already spent their last transfer on a pair of boots.' I was gutted, James. I could have bought her ten pairs of boots! But you can't say that, can you? Because once you start lying..."
James winced. "Mate... that sounds like a bloody nightmare."
Sirius pointed a finger at him. "Liar. You’re holding your stomach to keep from howling. You're about to burst. Your cheeks are turning red."
James finally lost it. "HAHAHAHA—SIRIUS—THE BOOTS—I—I can see it! Sirius Black, standing in a queue at an Italian bank with a fistful of crumpled Lire to pay the gas bill!!"
Sirius rolled his eyes. "I paid it at the Post Office, actually. Now wait. I haven't even got to the wedding bit yet."
James, breathless: "A WEDDING? PADFOOT, PLEASE. GO ON."
Sirius leaned back dramatically. "Brace yourself, Prongs. Because this... this is the part where Martina starts putting the pieces together."
"I’d just Apparated back to Italy after Lily’s wedding—I remember the date perfectly, April 2nd," Sirius began. "For months, I’d been spinning tales about airports, trains, and flight schedules—all lies. She’d see me in a new jacket and ask, 'Is that new?' and I'd say, 'Oh, it was a gift.' After all, I was supposed to be broke."
"By the way, you asked about the planes earlier—you’re dead right not to trust 'em. Muggle transport is a shambles. When they lost my suitcase, they didn't even try to find it; they just handed me some cash and sent me on my way."
"Where was I? Right. It turns out an owl had come to our kitchen window in March while I was at the wedding. Martina was terrified of the bird at first, but the owl kept pecking at the glass. She finally plucked up the courage to take the post and opened the letter... And what do you think it was, Prongs? The Italian Ministry of Magic reminding me to renew my residency permit. She thought it was a prank at first because our flatmate at the time was this hilarious Turkish lad called Teoman—he’d tell wild stories and get me plastered. But that day, Martina turned into a regular detective. She started digging."
"Sirius... I swear, Voldemort couldn't have cornered you this well!" James said, straightening his glasses and wiping his watering eyes.
"You can't con this woman. You need that kind of brain just to survive medical school, Prongs. She obsessed over it like a character in a crime novel. She looked into everything. 'There are no flights to Catania today, Sirius—unless you’ve got your own private jet,' she told me. My face went Gryffindor red."
"So what did you do? Did you tell her the airport staff gave her the wrong info?"
"I tried!" Sirius snapped. "Don't laugh, James! When she stood there with that notebook of hers, I felt like I was in a courtroom. She keeps a diary, you see. She’d written down every day I claimed to be travelling and cross-referenced them with actual flight and train schedules. 'You’ve arrived here on days when there were no flights from London, Sirius. And I know you hate trains. Have you been lying to me?' When she said that, I caved. Wizarding Secrecy, Fudge, the Ministry... it all went out the window. All I could think was how to convince her I wasn't a schizophrenic before she had me committed."
Martina placed the letter on the table slowly, deliberately, like the final piece of evidence in a closed murder case. She didn't take her eyes off Sirius for a second.
"You said you went to London in January. You said you came back to Catania five days later. But there were no flights from London that day, Sirius. You told me you left London in the morning and were home by 1 PM local time—luckily, I noted your arrival in my diary. You played the part well, but the only flights that day landed in Rome. No bus, train, or ferry could have got you from Rome to here in that time."
"Either you aren't going to London and you’ve got another lover in another city—maybe you're married and living a double life—or worse, you're a criminal! I don't know!"
Sirius was utterly trapped. His ego, his web of lies, and the secret he’d guarded for years were being torn to shreds by a smart Muggle woman with a ballpoint pen. He looked away, swallowing hard. The famous Sirius Black mask hit the floor and shattered.
"Fine," he said at last. His voice was flat, all defences dropped. "I lied. There was no plane. I... I’m a wizard."
Martina crossed her arms. There wasn't a hint of belief or surprise on her face. Instead, she pointed mockingly at a half-full glass of water on the table. "Oh, really? Then turn that water into wine and let’s see it."
In that moment, the suppressed, hot-headed Black temper flared up. He abandoned all logic. "Martina, do I look like Jesus to you?!"
"Aha! No, no, Sirius. You aren't wriggling out of this. What’s next, tarot cards? Are you going to read my palm? Whenever you're stuck, you either talk rubbish or you lie. Come on then, Wizard Sirius—I want my wine!"
"I’m not talking rubbish, and it's not a lie! Yes, I’ve lied a lot, but not about this. I thought... I thought if I told you, I’d lose you—"
"Oh, you were thinking of me, how lovely! There’s no one in the world like you, Sirius Black—the ideal boyfriend. He thinks of me better than I think of myself and tells me lies just to prove how 'real' his love is—"
Sirius looked at the glass. It was half-full of water. Even though he was brilliant at Transfiguration, turning one liquid into another without losing the taste was a common struggle for those who hadn't mastered the art. He didn't want to make her drink something that tasted like tomato juice or vinegar. Just past the water was another glass: Teoman’s half-litre beer mug. It wasn't a special gift, as far as he knew; there were two others just like it. Teoman wouldn't kick up a fuss over it.
With a swift movement, Sirius flicked his wand from his sleeve and aimed it at the beer mug. Martina’s words died in her throat. The spell took two seconds. In place of the mug stood a yellow, medium-sized oil lamp.
The oxygen seemed to vanish from the room. Sirius couldn't breathe. Neither could Martina; she had turned to stone, her mouth open, her words frozen. Had hours passed or only seconds? Finally, a faint moan escaped her. Sirius painfully shifted his gaze from the lamp to his girlfriend. This was the worst-case scenario: he’d have to make her forget, tell her he never loved her...
Martina was staring at him. Suddenly, her voice returned, and all hell broke loose in the kitchen. "ARE. YOU. ACTUALLY. MAGICAL?"
It was a good reaction, all things considered: she hadn't fainted. She slammed her hands onto the table—no, she was leaning on it for support. Her eyes were fixed blankly on the lamp. "This... this is physically impossible. This... this..."
Sirius didn't dare move. "Yes," was all he could manage.
Martina pulled her hands back and took a step away. When Sirius turned toward her, she shrieked, "Don't touch me!" but Sirius was already three metres away.
He held up his hands in surrender and placed his wand on the table. He could never be a threat to her... never.
"So... the whole time... you lied to me." Martina’s voice cracked—not with anger, but with pure disbelief. "But this time... the scale of the lie... Sirius, this is the most ridiculous thing I’ve ever heard in my life!"
"It’s not ridiculous," Sirius said, almost in a whisper. "It’s real."
Martina took another step back, as if the room's gravity had shifted. "Real," she repeated, weighing the word. "Yes. Because a second ago, there was a beer mug on this table. And now..."
She reached out with a trembling hand but didn't dare touch the lamp. "There is this. This... it defies the laws of physics. It defies the very foundations of the universe. But it’s real."
Her eyes turned to Sirius; her gaze was sharp, fractured, like a scientist still trying to solve a defiant equation. "And you... you didn't tell me. For months. Months!"
"What was I supposed to say, really? 'Don't worry, amore, we’ll get this stain out with a flick of a wrist.' Or wait, this one’s better: 'No need to boil the water on the stove, leave it to me, I’ll have it sorted.' Between the Italian Ministry of Magic and my lot back home, the Statute of Secrecy—"
Martina let out a hiccup and stared at Sirius with wide eyes. "You heard right, there’s a Ministry of Magic in England too, and we’re bound by law—"
"Was it illegal for you to tell me?"
"My moving here was illegal! It turns out my other childhood friend was the cold-blooded killer who helped target my best friend's family. I did my part in the investigation and they threw the man in prison, but they’re still breathing down my neck because I don't follow family traditions—meaning, I don't grease the right palms to get my way!"
Martina’s eyes welled up, and Sirius suddenly realised his voice had risen. He winced. "I’m sorry."
"Apology accepted," Martina said. She let out another quiet hiccup, blinking rapidly to rein in her emotions. It was a trait Sirius truly admired, though he could never tell if she didn't cry because of nerves of steel or if she was just playing the part of the strong one.
"Look..." Sirius said uneasily. "This has been a lot. Meeting you, getting this far... you’ll probably think I’m lying again, but I’m not: this experience has been wonderful. You are wonderful. It's the first time I’ve ever let someone sweep me off my feet; I trusted you, and I’m glad I did."
Martina narrowed her eyes. Sirius continued, his voice cracking slightly: "But I’ve made a right mess of it. No one deserves this... especially not you."
"Where is this going?"
"I’m asking for your permission, for... well." He swallowed hard. "To fix this. To stop hurting you, to stop lying to you... To wipe every memory of myself from your mind."
Martina blinked again. A laugh escaped her—part nerves, part shock, part desperation. "Wipe my mind? Ha! Why am I even surprised? You lot probably invented that too. You just don't have a spell to turn water into wine."
"Actually, we do—" Sirius said, a faint smirk tugging at his lips. Martina’s eyes went wide. "I’m just rubbish at it. My best friend, the one I told you about... James. He was a master at it. Jesus, basically. But he’d have been livid if he heard that comparison."
"I see," Martina said. Despite the chaos, Sirius noticed her smile. "You said his family was targeted. Did they die? Why?"
"It’s a very, very long story. Yes, James passed away, but—Martina, in a moment, I’m going to—"
"You won't make me forget. You won't. I won't allow it."
"What? But how? No, Martina, look—this is technically illegal. It’s only allowed for close friends, partners, spouses, that sort of thing. Wizards are few in number and a very closed-off society."
"Should I be scared? If someone comes, we’ll just lie, amore. Simple as that. Besides... it felt very strange, you offering to make me forget everything just so you could be 'honest' with me. Why don't you just promise to be honest from now on instead of making my ignorance a condition? I’m Italian, yes, but I’m not a blabbermouth, don't worry."
"I wasn't worried about that, I just—" Sirius started to say, but Martina cut him off with a perfect mocking tone, switching to Italian:
"‘Oh Gianpietro, buongiorno, lovely day, isn't it? An espresso, please. Oh, did you know? My boyfriend is actually a wizard, they hang out in London in some secret cult—’"
Martina couldn't finish the sentence. The distance between them vanished instantly as they began to kiss like mad.
In 1991, James let out another roar of laughter, but his eyes were full of emotion. Once again, they received warning looks from the other tables in the café. "That is the best love story I’ve ever heard. You owe Martina a 'wine,' I’d say. So, did things get easier once the secret was out?"
"Yes and no. But... it was right. And the right things are rarely easy, Prongs."
---------------------------------
They left the café and followed a few tourists into a quieter side street. "It's much easier to Apparate in this country. Italian Muggles are masters at disappearing in an instant too. If they see a neighbour they don't like coming their way, they’re behind a car or inside the nearest bar before you can blink."
James chuckled. Sirius offered his arm. "I want to take you somewhere beautiful. Hang on."
"Is this turning into a date, Padfoot? If I’d known, I would’ve swiped something flashier from your wardrobe." They both giggled.
"Scandalous! I’m a man loyal to my lady, don't try to woo me." They laughed, James took his friend’s arm, and Sirius guided the Side-Along Apparition.
They arrived at the Colosseum. "We brought Harry here about a fortnight ago; he was spellbound. I thought you’d feel the same."
"Is Harry interested in history?" James asked curiously. James and Sirius had never cared much for History of Magic at school, only doing enough to scrape through their O.W.L.s.
"He’s got the spark, for now. He might even get better marks than we did at Hogwarts. That Snape influence and all." He noticed James’s brow furrow. "Oh, come off it, Prongs... We had plenty of reasons not to like him as kids, but things have changed."
"It still gets on my nerves that Lily chose him when there were so many other candidates, Sirius. If you had married her, I’d never have been cross with you or Remus. Besides, Lily’s a beautiful woman. Did it never cross your mind?"
"Are you mental, James? Honestly, it never did. Because... look, marriage isn't something you do just because two people want to live together. I offered Lily my own place a dozen times—security measures aside, she’s the ideal flatmate. But a romantic partnership? We would’ve throttled each other, mate. Me and Lily... our emotions overflow from our hearts, we go mad when we love... I’ve been telling you about Martina all morning, what did you gather?"
"Hah, let’s see... That she’s very patient with you, she’s sincere, and she pulls you out of your shell. I’m sure I’ve missed a few things—"
"Exactly. That’s plenty. Spot on. She takes my words and my feelings, filters them through her own perspective, and communicates back a version that’s more grounded but still full of heart. Does that make sense?"
"Not entirely... I mean, she’s not a renegade like you?"
Sirius rolled his eyes. "She’s a renegade in her own way. What I mean is... she has her reactionary moments, but usually, I’m the one flying off the handle. She takes that and every time, how do I put it—think of it like you're boiling water on the stove to make tea—"
"What if I heat it with magic?" James said, grinning from ear to ear.
"Fine, you heat it with magic. You take the hot water to Lily, but she doesn't want tea, she just wanted to wash her hands. Now she’s going to get scalded."
"But that's a rubbish example, Sirius. Lily could just tell me not to—oh."
Sirius looked at his friend and smirked. "Martina cools that water down. And where she can't cool it, she lets me know plainly. What would happen if I’d married Lily? We would’ve scalded each other. That’s why it would never have worked... and you wouldn't have worked with her either."
“Could I have done it? No... I mean, no. I’m not a difficult person, Sirius. I’m not a total berk. Or am I?”
“You’re not, really. We’ve all got rough edges that need sanding down. Maybe yours are just a bit more... prominent.”
James rolled his eyes. “My head’s a mess, Sirius. But I’m sure of one thing with all my heart: if I’d made different choices that morning—if I’d reached Remus’s house and asked for her forgiveness—we would’ve made our marriage work. I thought about it a lot that night, and I’m sure she did too... I’m certain we would’ve spoken openly and found a healthier way to communicate.”
They wandered a bit further, and after eating some gelato, Sirius showed his friend a few more historical landmarks before leading him to a three-storey building where vines clung to the garden walls.
“This is the Italian Ministry of Magic. Tiny, but functional. I always Apparated here for my residency permits.” He then spoke of the witches and wizards who no longer lived in Italy.
“If Martina and I have a child, there’s a high chance we’ll have to move back to England at some point; there are no schools here. In 1783, the magnificent wizarding school in this country was forced to close its doors forever due to rising religious pressure and endless witch hunts. The educators sent all the students home, and then the teachers burned the school down so that no one could find the ancient spells or tarnish the memories. If you ask me, that act was what truly tarnished the memories.”
Sirius paused, his gaze shifting to the vine leaves. “There’s a school in France, Beauxbatons... that could be an option. But Martina is very clear about Hogwarts. If she doesn't change her mind, we’ll be heading back to the nest, ninety percent certainty.”
“You sound like you want it to change,” James said, grinning from ear to ear. The back of his mind was already busy dreaming up scenarios of what a "Mini-Sirius" would be like.
“Martina is a Muggle, James. The Azkaban breakout happened only last month... and the Ministry is living in a banana republic fantasy of its own making... we’re a long way off from the future I’m dreaming of. In every sense.”
“Padfoot, mate... let’s face facts first: none of us were born into a safe world. Your family was extra-terrible, yeah, but the world was already a mess in the 60s. Harry wasn't born in a quiet time either; he came into the world right in the middle of a war. While we’re walking here, those escapees are probably planning something. Luckily, Hogwarts is safe. My point is... you can't put life on hold forever.”
“Fine, Harry is safe, Hogwarts is safe. But Martina? My cousin Bella is an escapee too; I’m sure she’s asking after me constantly. Narcissa’s connections might not reach Rome, but with a bit of thought, she’d deduce that 'Sirius is living in Italy.' Rookwood was already aware... Should I tell my girlfriend to 'quit her profession'? Or should I say 'don't take my surname when we marry, maybe you’ll be safer that way'?”
“Tell her whichever one won't result in you getting hit with a frying pan. But first, give me the date of the wedding.”
Sirius made a rude hand gesture.
---------------------------------
POV Amelia Bones, 1985
The latest attempt at a witch-hunt by that fool Rookwood—which he thought he was hiding so well—lay before me: a receipt for items Sirius Black had bought from a Muggle hypermarket and his pay slip. Two metres to my left, Cornelius Fudge was pacing the room, wiping sweat from his brow.
“I cannot ignore missing financial documents. If word gets out... I don't even want to think about it. Especially with that man breathing down my neck...”
He was undoubtedly referring to Rookwood. As I opened the file, he continued: “Black is living with his Muggle girlfriend, working in a library, and his weekly earnings are 130,000 Italian Lire—that's 12 Galleons! For Merlin’s sake! Did you read the shopping receipt, Bones? Is the boy taking the mickey? This is a risk to the Ministry. Furthermore... Black...”
Just as he was speaking, Rookwood entered. In his late forties, serious, a man respected and even feared within the Department of Mysteries. Fudge pressed on. “How can a Black survive on 12 Galleons a week? Where is the man’s pride? This is either a lapse in sanity or a massive theatre staged to humiliate the Ministry!”
“Mr. Fudge,” I said, keeping my voice as neutral as possible, “Sirius Black’s low salary is not a crime. The Muggle economy works differently.”
“Yes, but... but... the Black family history is well known!”
There it was again. As if a surname were a piece of criminal evidence. As if everyone’s fate were written in the family ledger. Rookwood’s voice cut in, colder and more calculated than Fudge’s: “Bones, this man is living with a Muggle lover. A Muggle student! This is a risk to the Statute of Secrecy. Furthermore... the Black family history is—” “—well known,” I finished, without lifting my eyes from the file. “I’ve heard that sentence three times today.”
Rookwood’s eyes narrowed. Fudge’s widened. Mine, however, were fixed on the receipt. Fried artichokes and laundry detergent. Lavender scented. This was the greatest evidence of Sirius Black’s crimes. Seeing me look at the receipt, Rookwood thinned his lips into a line.
“Bones, everything this man buys from a Muggle market could be a message. Coded communication. Hidden spells. Secret signs.”
“Lavender detergent?” I asked, without raising an eyebrow. “Really?”
“Bones,” he said, his voice like ice, “you are underestimating this man.”
“Rookwood,” I said, taking off my glasses to look at him, “we have reviewed the file repeatedly. Black is living in his own orbit, his own life. He eats ice cream on the beach with his girlfriend, goes to the library, and works until 7 PM. Exposing us is the last thing on his mind.”
Rookwood’s face changed in that instant. It was as if all the anger, paranoia, and obsession with control he’d bottled up for years exploded in a single point. “Enough!” he hissed. “If you speak with such certainty... then you go, Bones.”
Fudge jumped in place. “R-Rookwood... you mean... send Amelia? To Sicily? Now?”
Rookwood took a step toward me, his shadow falling across my desk. “Yes. Since she believes so strongly in Black’s harmlessness... let her go and see for herself. Since market receipts convince you so much, Bones... then go and inspect this Muggle life on the ground.”
