Fudge frantically pressed his handkerchief to his forehead. “Yes, yes! Very logical! On-site inspection! Bones, you... you are the perfect fit for this job!”
And so, I found myself in the living room of a Muggle shouting at a Napoli match, watching Sirius Black’s ears turn red. The Sicilian heat, the Muggle television... and I, the Head of the Department of Magical Law Enforcement, standing in the hallway in my robes. Rookwood’s paranoia had brought me all the way here.
“A-A-Amelia? What on earth are you doing here?” he asked in shock, his eyes wide.
“Sirius Black. They sent me to see what the hell you're playing at, but these people are Muggles! I—you gave the impression you were living alone!”
Sirius’s face fell. “Welcome, Amelia. I wish I’d known you were coming; I would’ve sent Teoman and my girlfriend out or something.”
A sandy-haired young man was sitting in the armchair in the lounge, drinking beer and watching the Napoli match. “Sirius, come here, Napoli conceded a goal—haha—what’s going on? Who’s this? Welcome, amore?” When he turned his head and saw me, he called me 'darling'. Ignoring him, I spoke to Sirius in a low voice:
“Merlin... the Ministry sent me. To check on you. Fudge and the others. They’re impatient. But Sirius... I have to ask, are you actually here watching football?”
“Yes, it’s a very entertaining life. I’ve had three beers and I really need to pee. Amelia, what the hell is happening? Look, Martina knows, she knows everything, but she’s very cool about it. I told her everything and she didn't dump me. For God’s sake, end this visit now, there’s no problem.”
I struggled not to bite my lip. Sirius was as filterless as ever. “Fudge insisted. He found gaps in your residency permit and your wizarding income declaration—”
Just then, the boy named Teoman shouted. “GOOOOAL! 2–0!”
“It’s a cup match. Carry on, Amelia.”
“Actually, I don't think it’s about Fudge. Someone else is going after you—”
“Probably someone from the old Death Eater lot. Look, Amelia, whatever is missing, send it to me by owl, I’ll fix it and bring it to London.”
“One second... Death Eaters and owls? What’s going on, Sirius?” Teoman had quietly stood up and was already only a few feet away from us.
Sirius grumbled. “Mate, are you eavesdropping?”
“No. Yes. I was going to get a new beer from the kitchen. You’re the one whispering with a beautiful woman. Of course I’d be worried.”
I furrowed my brow. It didn't take long to figure out the friendly relationship between the two. Sirius’s credibility in this house was on a thin line, which proved Fudge right. Observing Sirius’s tension, the passivity he showed toward this ‘flatmate’ was a far cry from his character in the corridors of Hogwarts. It was time to take charge.
“It’s too late, Sirius. This man will start digging into this.” I cast Obliviate, making the sandy-haired boy forget what he had just heard.
The spell caused a momentary blankness in Teoman’s eyes. The guy blinked, then as if he’d simply gone to the kitchen, he nodded and went back to sit in his armchair. Sirius’s face, however, was bone white. “Amelia... did you really have to do that?”
Just as I was about to reply, the door nearby swung open. A girl stepped out wearing an oversized grey t-shirt and a messy bun; her rucksack looked as though the stitching was holding on for dear life. Her honey-coloured eyes studied Sirius and then me. Without losing her cool for a second, she spoke: "Sirius, amore, I’m off to the library. Finals week, you know... See your guest out, would you? I’d offer an espresso if I weren't in such a rush, but I’m running late." The girl, who I realised was Martina, leaned in and kissed a stone-faced Sirius on the cheek. She gave me a polite nod and vanished, the door clicking shut behind her.
Sirius, flushed red all the way to his collar, muttered, "There you have it, Amelia... It’s a real relationship. Does Fudge need any more details for your report? Perhaps he’s curious about how many times a week we eat dinner?"
It was clearly time for me to go. I smiled at Sirius. From the lounge, Teoman shouted again, "Nooo! How do you miss a shot like that?!"
Two days later, near the end of the working day, a weary brown owl delivered Sirius Black’s letter and the missing documents for his file.
"Dear Amelia (and Cornelius, who I’m sure is peeking over your shoulder),
Since you’re so obsessed with every second of my life, here is some total 'Black' transparency for you. Last week, I took Martina out to a small tavern. We had fried artichokes, Martina had a glass of wine, and I entertained her by imitating that bizarre Muggle 'football commentary' style.
Most recently, we went on holiday to Sardinia. The sea was very salty, though not quite as salty as Cornelius’s face. As for our physical activities; we engage in 'romantic exercise' an average of four times a week. If Fudge is curious about the duration, the angles, or the calorie burn rate of said activities, tell him to send a Stopwatch Wizard next time to stand by the bed.
Also, Amelia, Martina’s birthday is in October. Tell Cornelius that since he’s so involved in our private lives, he should show some courtesy. I’ve tucked Martina’s measurements below; perhaps he’d like to buy her a posh bra from London. Make it black—family tradition, after all.
Stay lovely, or don't,
S.B."
---------------------------------
It hadn't even been 24 hours since Amelia Bones had swept through like a storm, and Sirius hadn't slept a wink. Martina was in the kitchen making coffee; she’d had little sleep too, but hers was due to exams. She had carrying-over credits from previous years, meaning she wouldn't be able to graduate next year. Right now, all she could do was try not to lose her scholarship.
Sirius stood in the doorway, wearing that familiar expression of 'I'm hiding something, but this time it's for a good reason.' "Buongiorno, amore," he said softly, "I have to go away for a bit."
Martina turned, her eyebrows raised. "Go away? Why?"
Sirius took a deep breath. "This... it isn't about us. Not this time." Martina remained silent. Sirius continued, "The Ministry is watching me. People like Rookwood are breathing down my neck. And I can't drag you, your neighbours, or anyone at school into this mess."
Martina stepped closer and placed her hand on Sirius’s arm. "Sirius... this isn't a disappearing act, is it?"
Sirius looked away. "No. But... to protect the people I love, I need to become invisible."
Martina took a deep breath. "How long?"
"I don't know," Sirius said honestly. "A week? Two months?"
Martina nodded. "Fine. But on one condition. This isn't a goodbye. It’s a tactic." Sirius’s face softened. "Yes. It's a tactic." Martina placed her hand on Sirius’s cheek. "And even when I’m not by your side, I’m on your side."
---------------------------------
Rome, 1991
As Sirius continued his story, he’d shown James the Trevi Fountain, and now they were hunting for a quiet, secluded alleyway to Apparate home. James, having consumed three different flavours of gelato in a single day, was starting to feel the first signals of hunger. Behind the apartment blocks, the sun had dipped low, turning the sky a soft, hazy blue.
“You know,” James said, walking with his hands in his pockets, “when I first saw you this morning, for a split second I thought you weren't the same Sirius I’d left behind. Same laugh, same voice, same energy... but somewhere in there, it felt like it wasn't you. I was wrong. You're still that same Sirius I grew up with. It's just... you’ve grown up too. The man walking next to me now, buying me ice creams, forcing me to take photos at the fountain, and making Fudge and Rookwood bite their own wands... he’s the same Sirius.”
Sirius let out a bark of a laugh. Just then, they found the deserted alley they were looking for, and Sirius offered his arm again. “Teaching you how to do magic again is going to be a right laugh. After that, it’s simple: we’ll make your 'living' status official and get you a new wand.”

Continue: Book 2
Meanwhile, Tonks came running through the door of the Department of Magical Law;
however she managed it, she crashed into a filing cabinet, and some documents
scattered across the room. Severus Snape stopped eating the snack in his hand with
his most normal and disapproving expression. As Damira jumped up and started
rearranging the documents, Tonks was profusely apologizing.
Severus tilted his head slightly to the side. "I want to leave early this evening, Tonks.
Can whatever legal mistake you've made wait until tomorrow?"
"Great. Let's leave together."
Severus frowned. This had to be extremely important. They stepped outside, but Tonks
was heading towards the street. "What's the plan? Seeing as you're in a 'walls have ears'
mood, it must be very urgent."
"Very urgent," Tonks repeated, far from her usual joking self. They entered a noisy fast
food chain restaurant and looked around for an empty table. When they finally sat down
at the very back, Tonks was out of breath.
"Today—this morning—something very strange happened. I have to tell you this, I have
to—and my boyfriend can hate me as much as he wants—I don't care—"
"Calm down first," Severus said, in as calm a voice as he could manage. What could
this girl have possibly done that Remus would hate her for? "Now explain it in clear
words. Straightforwardly."
Tonks looked at him. Her eyes were anxious. But not for herself—no. "James Potter isn't
dead. He arrived this morning. He's been wandering in the forest as a stag for ten years.
Moreover, he's traumatized, trembling, and crying. What are we going to do, Snape?"
That slightly weary expression on Severus's face vanished instantly. He went
completely white, as if someone had drained all the blood from his face with a wand.
He didn't even realize he was squeezing the napkin in his hand. His eyes locked onto
Tonks's face without blinking for even a second. The noise and laughter of the
surrounding Muggles suddenly became muffled; the world was muted. There were only
Tonks's lips and that impossible name.
James Potter.
The young witch continued, less panicked now, trying to manage Severus's shock. "He
came to the apartment this morning. Barely managed to get Remus's address. And
Snape, he... he is in a very bad state. But he's alive. In the flesh, breathing, and—"
"But he is in a grave. I was under the impression Sirius Black recovered the body and
buried it himself," he said in an icy voice. In the back of his mind were Lily and Harry.
God, how would they react to this news?
"The grave was empty; since the house burned down, they assumed he had turned to
ash as well. I only heard it from Remus today."
Severus’s heart rate quickened. This changed everything. "Does Lily know?"
"No," Tonks said. "Remus and Sirius couldn't bring themselves to tell her; I think they
don't want to say anything without you. Besides, I agree that getting James pulled
together first was the right move. But I... Snape, I couldn't bear the thought of you going
home and eating dinner with Lily while being completely in the dark. You have a right to
know."
Severus looked at Tonks. There was pure panic and a hint of fear in her grey eyes.
"Remus and Sirius might have told him that you’re married to Lily. By breaking it to him
gently, of course."
"Where is he now?" Severus asked, his voice sharpening like a blade. "I don’t
understand—wandering in a forest for ten years and then just appearing... This is a very
malicious act."
"As I said, I have strong suspicions that he suffered a blow to his memory. He’s lost his
sense of time and he has tremors. If he went to Italy with Sirius, Martina can diagnose
him instantly."
Severus stood up and adjusted his robes. His posture was more erect than usual. "I
must go home. Lily... she should hear this from me."
"Thank you for listening to me," Tonks said; she was still anxious but stood up. "Should
we wait to hear from you?"
Severus laughed bitterly. "I don’t know. We’ll send an owl by tomorrow. Sometimes I
can’t keep up with this life. We need to run faster after it."
Tonks smiled back at him. "Sometimes I feel the same. I hope this is just a temporary
uphill climb."
----------------------------------------
Severus Apparated to their new house in Hogsmeade. It was a rather sweet, three-story
building, located away from the shops and eateries but right on the main street,
perfectly fitting the village's atmosphere. They had organized their new lives entirely to
suit this place. Moreover, this new house featured a tiny greenhouse suitable for
growing things, and a room for potion experiments—not in the basement, but detached
from the house, right in the garden.
They couldn't be considered fully moved in yet, but everyone had a room. And since the
house was magical, it had adjusted itself to suit their 'needs'. In his thirty years of life,
Severus had never seen anywhere other than Hogwarts capable of doing such a thing.
The only person to greet him as he walked through the door was Iris. Lily's stern voice
could be heard; it seemed the boys had been caught getting up to mischief again.
Grinning, he picked up his daughter.
"Harry spilled pig root on the floor. Lou fell too," she said, laughing. Severus was
laughing simply because Iris was laughing; pig root was a prank item, the equivalent of a
banana peel, only harder to spot. He could understand why Lily was yelling at him.
"Louie," Severus repeated. "His name is Louie. You say it, too."
"Lou."
"No, no, my love, Louie."
"LOU." Severus was laughing out loud now. "Alright, alright. If you say it's Lou,"
"Lou!" Louie had just walked in and looked at his father with a pout.
"How did you fall? Did it hurt?" Severus asked in a soft voice.
"My bum hurt," Louie said. Then his features softened. "Oh, Mom got really mad at my
brother. Let him see what it's like to mess with me."
"It was just a little practical joke, Louie." He stroked his red hair. "Is your mother
upstairs? Can you play with Iris for a bit?" He left the two siblings together and went
upstairs. Lily had just cleaned the hallway; she had a scowl on her face. Her scowl
vanished when she saw Severus.
"I didn't hear you come in, darling." She closed the distance with two steps and kissed
her husband. "God, I'm going to miss working at the Ministry. Our lunch breaks." They
hugged each other.
"We'd better speak alone... Where is Harry?"
"In his room." Lily pursed her lips. "It was a simple practical joke, but what if Louie had
fallen down the stairs, Sev?" She had reverted to panicked mother mode. "That's why I
yelled at him so harshly, anyway. Now he's sulking with me."
Severus took Lily to the place they had planned to be the potions room before the
children could see them. He was thinking about how to convey what Tonks had said. He
asked Lily to sit down and started from the moment the young Auror barged into his
office.
Severus told the story Tonks had recounted in a single breath, without embellishment.
He acted with the precision of a surgeon while choosing his words; he knew it would
hurt, but he had to drain the poison. And the worst part was that as Lily cried with her
shoulders shaking across from him, all the comforting words he could say were actually
useless.
Severus could only hug her and be the solid anchor Lily could hold onto in this current.
Fortunately, that was exactly what Lily needed; she was no longer crying convulsively,
but she was still upset, and her green eyes were brimming with tears.
"Harry..." Lily said, her voice barely above a whisper this time, but holding pure terror
within. "What are we going to tell Harry? Are we going to say, 'Sorry son, your father
didn't actually die, we had it wrong'? Severus, he's only eleven years old! He's just about
to start school..."
Tears started pouring from her eyes again, but these tears weren't out of longing for
James; they were out of fear of the trauma Harry would experience.
"No, my love. We will form better sentences, but there is a more important issue. James
is a wreck right now, Lily. His memory isn't intact, he is traumatized and unstable. I will
not allow him to appear before Harry in that state. We will not."
Lily rested her head on Severus's chest, gripping his shirt tightly. "He's back, Sev...
James is back." A muffled confession spilled out between her sobs: "I... I'm not ready to
see him. I closed that chapter. I burned that book!"
Severus rested his chin on her head as he stroked his wife's hair. Lily saying "I burned
that book" had swept away the very last crumb of doubt inside Severus.
"Don't be afraid," Severus said. "No one can enter this garden without your consent.
Neither James Potter nor the past. Tomorrow we will write a letter to Sirius. We will get
the situation under control."

1 September 1991
Living quite literally in the shadow of Hogwarts, Harry had been so thrilled at the
prospect of "at least taking the train this year" that Lily and Severus couldn't bring
themselves to refuse him. Consequently, Harry had spent the previous night at the
Burrow. They were to meet at King's Cross station in the morning before the train
departed.
After Severus and Lily loaded Harry’s school trunk into the boot of their red Hyundai,
they parked the car in a lot just three minutes away from Remus's house. Connecting
the road from Hogsmeade to a motorway would have been a dreadful waste of time,
making Apparating to this car park their most practical option.
----------------------------------------
During their trip to Diagon Alley, while Harry and Severus were inspecting student
robes, Lily had run into Hagrid and chatted for a while. After enjoying some ice cream
and shopping, Lily suddenly spotted the snowy owl. They hadn't planned on getting a
pet—the Owlery was already full of post owls, after all—but Lily couldn't resist and
bought her as a Hogwarts gift.
Harry had been absolutely captivated by the white owl. Wanting to choose the perfect
name for her, he buried himself in his new textbooks and dictionaries. Severus, too,
wanted him to find it on his own, so they refrained from suggesting any names, even
though they had a few ideas of their own. Ultimately, Harry settled on the name Hedwig.
The day of the journey arrived, and Harry reached the station with Ron—who was three
times more excited than he was—and the rest of the Weasley family. Simultaneously,
Severus and Lily arrived with the two younger children and Remus. They slipped through
the concealed barrier onto the platform.
His father was the first to embrace him. "No getting into trouble, and no wandering the
corridors at night. Do not let it go to your head that your mother is at the school; she will
deduct points from you more ruthlessly than anyone else."
Ah, that was undeniably true. Truth be told, it suited Harry perfectly that Lily wouldn't
treat him as 'special' at school. Glancing sheepishly at Ron, it suddenly dawned on him:
Ron didn't know his mother was going to be a teacher. None of the Weasleys knew,
aside from Arthur and Molly. A wicked grin spread across his face.
Then his mother hugged him. "See you tonight," she murmured, pressing a kiss to his
hair. Harry flushed with embarrassment but didn't pull away. "Mum..."
Louie was looking up at Harry with a pout. "Why aren't you excited? I wouldn't be able to
stop smiling," he declared, taking Harry by surprise. He then threw his arms around his
older brother, and Harry hugged him back. Iris was too young to comprehend what was
happening, nestled securely in her mother's arms. Harry waved at her, but Iris merely
giggled in response.
Remus pulled him into a quick embrace as well. "Sirius couldn't make it. But he sent a
gift for you. Your father approved it, don't worry. Hey, don't open it here," he added
hastily.
They boarded the train. Harry wanted to sit with Ron, but everywhere else was full.
However, in a compartment towards the end of the train, Draco was sitting alone. Harry
looked at Ron first, then offered a greeting to Draco. "Hi, can we sit?"
Draco's face instantly lit up, and he nodded. Harry and Ron stowed their trunks and sat
down. Harry placed his owl's cage on the seat between himself and Ron.
"So, Harry, are you excited? Which house were you thinking of?" Draco asked.
Harry smiled. "My mum is a Gryffindor, my dad is a Slytherin. They're prepared for either
outcome. You?"
"My entire family is Slytherin," Draco said. "Maybe I'll get in too, I don't know. But if the
Sorting Hat cares about what we want... Ravenclaw."
"My brothers said Ravenclaw values intelligence," said Ron. "I hope you're smart."
Harry realized the two hadn't officially met and introduced them. Ron looked as if he
was about to say something, but held his tongue. Most likely a snide remark about his
father. Draco didn't dwell on it either. Instead, he took an interest in Ron's other
brothers, and Ron began recounting their adventures.
"I'm not sure if they were telling the truth, but that's how they told it to me," he added.
Harry could guess; Fred and George, for some reason, particularly enjoyed terrifying
Ron.
"By the way," Harry said, his voice tinged with slight embarrassment. "My mum is going
to be at the school. As the Potions Professor, no less."
The blond boy raised his eyebrows, while Ron looked absolutely horrified. "No way.
Harry, that's a nightmare."
Draco, on the other hand, was smiling. "I think it's brilliant. But it'll put pressure on you.
You'll have to be top of your class in Potions," he added.
A mild wave of panic washed over Harry; he hadn't thought of that at all. Ron nodded in
solemn agreement. "You don't have the luxury of not doing your homework or failing the
class, Harry. Naturally, neither do I, seeing as our mothers are friends."
"What if I told you that you're the only one who knows this?" Harry offered, as if trying to
make amends. "And you too, Draco. I can't even imagine Fred, George, and Percy at
dinner tonight," he added with a grin.
About an hour later, Draco caught a toad, leaving the other two boys staring in
astonishment. Draco was equally surprised. "I suppose this 'prince' must have a
princess."
As the three boys burst into laughter, the compartment door slid open with a sharp
clatter. A girl with bushy brown hair stepped inside. Behind her stood a round-faced boy
looking rather miserable. Harry recognized him; it was Neville Longbottom, though they
had only met once before.
"Has anyone seen a toad around here?" the girl asked in a bossy voice. "Neville's lost
his."
Draco hoisted the toad in his hand with the air of an aristocrat. He wore that classic
Malfoy smirk, but his demeanor was entirely playful. "Could this be the 'Prince' you are
looking for, my lady?" he asked.
Catching the joke, Harry and Ron snickered. Neville's eyes lit up. "Trevor!" he cried,
lunging forward to take the toad from Draco's hand. "Oh, thank you! We've been looking
everywhere for him."
The girl paused for a moment, momentarily disarmed by Draco's courtesy. Her eyes
darted to Harry, then to Ron, and finally back to Draco. "I'm Hermione Granger," she
said, leaning against the doorframe next to Draco. "And who are you?"
"I'm Draco Malfoy." "Ron Weasley." "Harry Potter."
Hermione's eyes locked onto the scar on Harry's forehead. "Are you really Harry Potter?
I know all about you! You're mentioned in Modern Magical History and The Rise and Fall
of the Dark Arts."
Harry shot Ron a sidelong glance. "Blimey," Ron muttered. Draco, meanwhile, was
grinning. Fortunately, the girl didn't seem overly starstruck.
"Anyway, you'd better change into your robes. We're about to arrive." She stepped out,
sliding the door shut behind her.
Ron stared after her in disbelief. "Is she a Prefect or something? Ordering us about like
that..."
Even so, they got dressed.
When the train finally hissed to a halt at Hogsmeade station half an hour later, it had
grown completely dark, and there was a freezing chill in the air. Harry, Draco, and Ron
joined the throng of students spilling out onto the platform. Ron pulled his robes tighter
around himself, his teeth chattering.
"Firs' years! Firs' years over here!"
Hagrid appeared, his massive lantern swaying like a beacon in the dark. Harry smiled
when he saw Hagrid, but Hagrid merely gave him a quick wink, continuing his duties
with practiced professionalism. "To the boats! No more 'n four to a boat!"
Harry, Ron, and Draco climbed into a boat. Neville (clutching his toad tightly) sat beside
them. When the boats moved on their own and began to glide across the famous, glass
smooth lake, everyone's breath caught in their throats. Before them, reaching into the
sky like a mountain, stood Hogwarts Castle, its windows glittering like stars.
Draco had set aside his usual cynical demeanor as he gazed at the castle. "My father
used to say the enchantments on the ceiling were wearing off, but... it looks
magnificent," he murmured.
Harry, on the other hand, had been able to see a portion of Hogwarts from his bedroom
window for the past few nights. He decided to keep this to himself so as not to sound
boastful; Draco deserved to experience the thrill.
When Professor McGonagall led them through those massive oak doors, the butterflies
in Harry's stomach began to flutter. The four House tables, the thousands of candles
suspended in mid-air, and the enchanted ceiling... It was exactly as his parents had
described, but seeing it in person was entirely different. All the first-years were now
lined up, and the Sorting Hat would place the newcomers into their Houses in front of
everyone.
Harry spotted the red-haired Weasleys at the Gryffindor table. Instead of looking at the
first-years, the three brothers were engaged in a heated conversation, drawing in those
around them. When Harry followed the direction of George's gaze, his stomach did a
slight flip. At the High Table, smiling from his gold-embroidered chair in the center, was
Dumbledore. But he wasn't the one Harry was looking for. Towards the end of the table,
wearing elegant professor's robes of blended black and emerald green, her red hair
pulled into a tight bun, sat his mother, Lily Evans-Snape.
Lily appeared to be occupied with the parchment in front of her, but suddenly she
looked up. And as if she had placed him there herself, she found Harry instantly. She
didn't wave. She didn't even smile. She merely tilted her chin up slightly, giving him that
"Stand up straight" look Harry knew so well. But her green eyes were laughing. Harry
immediately straightened his back.
"She won't throw us into a potion cauldron if we make a mistake in her class, right?"
Ron whispered. Draco snickered. Hermione turned her head to look at them, as if trying
to figure out what was going on. Her mouth opened, but the Sorting Hat began its song,
and Hermione opted to stay quiet.
Then the names began to be called. They had started with the letter A. When the name
"Granger, Hermione" was called, the girl sat on the stool; the Sorting Hat deliberated
over her much longer than the others, and then shouted "GRYFFINDOR!"
The names progressed; "Malfoy, Draco." The blond boy sat on the stool, holding himself
completely upright. Barely a second after the hat touched his head, it bellowed,
"RAVENCLAW!" The table at the far end looked stunned, but applause erupted from
another table. Draco winked at Harry and Ron as he headed over to his new House with
a massive grin on his face.
Harry and Ron exchanged a look. As a few more names were called, Harry felt his
stomach ache with nerves.
When the name "Potter, Harry" was called, the whispers swelled into a loud buzz.
"Did she say Potter?" "Harry Potter?" Harry walked up with a straight back, sat on the
stool, and the hat dropped over his eyes. Darkness.
"Hmm," said a small voice in his ear. "Very familiar... One side of you is disciplined and
ambitious, like your father. The other side... ah, that side is fiery and brave, like your
mother. But what is it that truly drives you?"
Harry thought to himself. Where did he want to belong? Draco was in Ravenclaw. Ron
would probably be in Gryffindor. His parents would support any house anyway. The hat
whispered, "A difficult decision... Very difficult..."
Harry pondered in that moment. "Let it be Gryffindor. Ron will go there... I'm not good
enough for Ravenclaw... Which one is the right one for me?" He had never expected to
struggle this much. He had felt so at ease in the boats.
"Very well, since you ask for what is right... I am sending you to the fire you belong to!"
The hat shouted to the entire hall: "GRYFFINDOR!"
A massive cheer erupted from the Gryffindor table, especially from the Weasley twins.
Ron was waving at Harry as if he had just won the lottery. Harry took off the hat and set
it down. There were a few empty seats at the Gryffindor table; he sat down across from
Hermione. The girl smiled at him.
As he settled at his new table, Draco smirked from across the hall and made a gesture
as if raising a glass to him. Percy patted his shoulder in congratulations and muttered,
"Look at the teachers' table."
He locked eyes with Lily. She gave him a thumbs-up and winked, making Harry blush.
"Why didn't you tell us your mum would be at the school, Harry?" groaned Fred with
dramatic flair. "The fact that she likes us won't stop her from taking points."
Five minutes later, Ron was also sorted into Gryffindor and sat next to Harry. "Are points
really that important?" Harry asked as he clapped for his friend.
Percy answered seriously. "This is my fifth year, and Slytherin has been the champion
for four years straight. Their Head of House is much more protective, you see."
"I wish that would change this year," said George. "But I reckon your mum will be fairer."
"Who's our Head of House?" asked Ron.
"Professor McGonagall," Percy replied. "She isn't particularly protective."
Once the Sorting ended, Dumbledore gave his warnings, and everyone began to eat. A
flying note landed on Harry's plate.
"What is it?" asked Ron curiously.
"From Draco," Harry said, smiling as he read the note. "He's asking if we want to face off
this weekend. Quidditch. But my broom is at home," he added with a sigh.
"Right, but your house is just over there," said Ron.
Harry had completely forgotten. "Oh, you're completely right. The House sorting must
have numbed my brain."
"What do you mean?" Hermione asked in surprise. Harry smiled at her.
"They moved last week. To Hogsmeade," Ron said, stuffing a massive piece of chicken
into his mouth.
Hermione was bewildered. "Then why did you take the train?" The Weasley twins burst
into laughter.
"Harry didn't want to miss the train atmosphere, that's why." The girl rolled her eyes.
Percy and the other female Prefect led the first-years to the dormitories. Everyone
picked a bed they liked.
"A package was left next to my wardrobe," Neville said in a bewildered voice. He had
chosen the bed closest to the entrance. "Harry, this is yours. Your broom. Your mum
sent it."
"What?" Harry asked in surprise. It really was his broom. Ron and the others were
doubled over with laughter. Harry's ears turned red. "Yes, yes, I am truly forgetful."
"Is your mum strict?" asked Seamus, another first-year.
"Oh, no," Harry said with a mischievous expression, "Only if she catches me spilling
potion roots on the hardwood floor." They laughed. Then everyone talked about their
own families and drifted off to sleep.
----------------------------------------
At breakfast, the Great Hall was buzzing with chatter. As Harry sat with Ron and
Hermione (while Hermione tried to make them memorize their timetables), the owl post
arrived. Hundreds of owls swooped in. Harry's white owl, Hedwig, landed next to his
plate and offered the parchment tied to her leg. This was the reply from his father; Harry
opened the letter.
"Harry,
So it's Gryffindor. Let us call it an expected 'disaster'. All jokes aside, your mother has
been walking around at breakfast this morning with that infuriating 'I told you so' smile
on her face. Congratulations, son. Red suits you.
P.S. Be careful while playing Quidditch with Draco this weekend. Your mother may be at
the school, but I am not in the hospital wing. Your father, S.S."
Harry smiled. His father wasn't upset at all; in fact, he was supportive. But how had his
mother found out he was going to play Quidditch this weekend? Ron shrugged.
"I think it's a good thing they're tracking your every move," Hermione said in a serious
tone.
"But he's safe here," Ron argued. "Those Azkaban escapees can't get in here."
Hermione shivered. "Still. My dad always says you can never be too careful."
"Is your dad a teacher?" Harry asked curiously.
"No. Both my mum and dad are dentists."
The Potions classroom was in a spacious stone building right next to the Herbology
greenhouses. Harry was surprised when they walked in. There was no trace of the "dark,
gloomy dungeons filled with dead things floating in jars" his father had described.
It was a bright, high-ceilinged classroom with massive windows. The interior was
bathed in sunlight. The scent of fresh earth and lavender drifted in through the
windows. Instead of terrifying creatures, the shelves were neatly lined with dried herbs,
colorful powders, and pristine crystal vials.
Professor Lily Snape was sorting through bunches of fresh mint at her large desk at the
front of the class. Her emerald green robes shimmered in the sunlight.
"Good morning," Lily said without looking up. Her voice echoed softly but clearly in the
classroom's acoustics. "Everyone, take your seats. You may gather your ingredients
from the benches in front of the windows."
Draco was sitting in the front row, enjoying the sunlight. Harry and Ron moved to the
empty desk next to Neville. "This place is brilliant," Hermione whispered. "Our risk of
being poisoned while brewing is much lower; the ventilation is perfect."
Lily clapped her hands to gather their attention. "Potion-making is not merely throwing
ingredients into a cauldron," she said, holding up a pinch of dried nettle. "It is
understanding nature. It is learning to extract the essence of a leaf, the power of a root."
Harry looked at his mother in awe. He tried to push aside the thought that he needed to
be the top of the class.
"Today, we will be making the Cure for Boils," said Lily. "A simple but fundamental
mixture. Remember that you must heat the porcupine quills before crushing them,
otherwise..." Lily smiled, but it was a warning smile. "...the results will not be very
pleasant."
Midway through the lesson, the classroom was filled with steam and the sound of
bubbling cauldrons. Sunlight danced within the rising vapors. However, this peaceful
atmosphere was shattered by Neville's panic.
"Harry!" whispered Neville. "Should I add them? Now?"
Harry, trying to get the color of his own potion just right—a perfect blue—replied
absently, "I don't know, Neville, check the book."
Neville threw a handful of porcupine quills into the cauldron. But he hadn't taken the
cauldron off the fire.
BANG!
The cauldron melted into a twisted lump of metal, and the acidic liquid inside spilled
across the stone floor. Neville's shoes began to hiss and melt. The poor boy was
trembling.
"Stand back!" Lily shouted. She waved her wand like an orchestral conductor, vanishing
the liquid on the floor in a single motion. She threw the windows wide open to clear the
smoke.
Neville was shaking with fear. "I'm sorry, Professor, I... I forgot."
Lily walked over to Neville. There was no anger on her face, but there was
disappointment. "Go to the hospital wing, Longbottom. Have Madam Pomfrey look at
your feet."
Then, she slowly turned to Harry. "Potter," Lily said.
Harry gulped. "Yes, Professor?"
"You did not warn your desk mate while he was heading toward a disaster. Even though
your own potion is in perfect condition." Lily cast a brief glance at Harry's cauldron. "But
a Gryffindor always looks out for their teammates."
Draco snickered from the back. Ron looked as though he wanted to hide under the
desk.
"I am taking one point from Gryffindor," Lily said, looking directly into Harry's eyes.
"Next time, do not keep your eyes solely on your own cauldron."
The classroom fell silent. Harry hadn't expected his mother to be this "fair." But
strangely, he liked it. His mother was genuinely acting like a "teacher."
As they left the class, Seamus said, "Your mum is terrifying, mate. I'd rather take
Quirrell's garlic-smelling class in the dungeons."
Harry smiled. "I'm looking forward to my dad's letter. When he hears about this, he'll
probably frame it."
----------------------------------------
Saturday morning was cold but clear. After finishing his breakfast, Harry grabbed his
broom and ran to the Quidditch pitch. Draco had come alone, without Crabbe and
Goyle by his side. He held a late-model Comet 260 in his hand. He whistled when he
saw the Nimbus 2000 in Harry's grip.
"Wow," said Draco, examining the broom. "Your dad really spared no expense, Harry.
This baby is the fastest broom on the market right now."
Harry stroked the smooth handle of the broom. "He just knows I love to fly," he said
modestly. "Come on, who's going to catch the Snitch first?"
Draco pulled a practice Golden Snitch with slightly slowed wings from his pocket and
released it into the air. They both took off at the same time.
As the two boys showed off their latest flying skills to each other, Lily and Professor
McGonagall were watching Harry and Draco from an empty classroom in the North
Tower.
"Draco's family," the wiser and older witch began, "are projecting their displeasure
regarding his House sorting heavily onto their son," she explained.
"I was astounded when Professor Ferrett, the Head of Slytherin House, revealed the
bribe he was offered. He is aware he has no such power, but even if he did... once a
student is sorted into a House, it cannot be changed. I've experienced this in the past as
well. Families can be quite dreadful sometimes, Lily."
"Don't I know it," said Lily. Sirius Black's first year had been pure hell. He had received a
Howler from home almost every week.
"It is not Harry's responsibility, of course, but it pleases me that he is befriending
Draco." Lily agreed. The two boys had landed and seemed to be joking around.
"He looks heartbreakingly like James," Minerva added. Lily's chest tightened. "Even their
flying styles are the same."
"Harry flies more cautiously when slowing down. Besides, in my opinion, he is much
more suited to the Seeker position, not Chaser," said Lily.
Minerva patted her arm affectionately. "You know, Lily... the Seeker position on the
team is open."
